## I Enter Three Hussars / 率三骠骑兵进场

===Original===
> When shall we three meet again
 

 In thunder, lightning, or in rain?
> When the hurlyburly’s done.
 

 When the battle’s lost and won.

===Chinese===
> 咱们三人何时再相逢
> 在雷霆、闪电，还是暴雨之中？
> 等这阵甚嚣尘上散了场，
> 胜败输赢已见分晓。

---

===Original===
My name is Greta Forzane. Twenty-nine and a party girl would describe me. I was born in Chicago, of Scandinavian parents, but now I operate chiefly outside space and time—not in Heaven or Hell, if there are such places, but not in the cosmos or universe you know either.

I am not as romantically entrancing as the immortal film star who also bears my first name, but I have a rough-and-ready charm of my own. I need it, for my job is to nurse back to health and kid back to sanity Soldiers badly roughed up in the biggest war going. This war is the Change War, a war of time travelers—in fact, our private name for being in this war is being on the Big Time. Our Soldiers fight by going back to change the past, or even ahead to change the future, in ways to help our side win the final victory a billion or more years from now. A long killing business, believe me.

===Chinese===
我叫格蕾塔·福尔赞（Greta Forzane）。「二十九岁，派对女郎」，这几个词就能把我交代个大概。我生在芝加哥，父母是斯堪的纳维亚人，可如今我主要在空间与时间之外讨生活——不在天堂，也不在地狱（倘若真有那种地方的话），可同样不在你所认识的这个宇宙里。

我不像那位与我同名的影坛不朽巨星那般浪漫勾魂（译注：指葛丽泰·嘉宝），但我自有一股粗粝爽利的魅力。我用得着它，因为我的差事就是护理士兵（Soldiers）——在眼下打得最凶的这场战争里被揍得七荤八素的士兵——把他们养回健康，哄回清明。这场战争便是变更战争（Change War），一场时间旅行者的战争——说实在的，我们私下里管身处这场战争叫「身处大时光（the Big Time）」（译注：the Big Time 兼有「风光的大日子」之意，与凡人的寻常时光相对）。我们的士兵打仗，靠的是回到过去改动历史，甚至赶去前方改动未来，好让我方在十亿年乃至更远的将来赢得最终胜利。相信我，这是桩漫长磨人的杀戮营生。

===Original===
You don’t know about the Change War, but it’s influencing your lives all the time and maybe you’ve had hints of it without realizing.

Have you ever worried about your memory, because it doesn’t seem to be bringing you exactly the same picture of the past from one day to the next? Have you ever been afraid that your personality was changing because of forces beyond your knowledge or control? Have you ever felt sure that sudden death was about to jump you from nowhere? Have you ever been scared of Ghosts—not the storybook kind, but the billions of beings who were once so real and strong it’s hard to believe they’ll just sleep harmlessly forever? Have you ever wondered about those things you may call devils or Demons—spirits able to range through all time and space, through the hot hearts of stars and the cold skeleton of space between the galaxies? Have you ever thought that the whole universe might be a crazy, mixed-up dream? If you have, you’ve had hints of the Change War.

===Chinese===
你们不知道变更战争这回事，可它无时无刻不在影响你们的生活，没准你们早领教过它的暗示，只是自己没觉察。

你可曾为记性发过愁，因为它一天一个样，似乎没法把过去那幅画面原封不动地端给你？你可曾害怕自己的性子正被些你无从知晓、也无力左右的力量更改？你可曾笃定死神就要不知打哪儿朝你扑来？你可曾被幽灵（Ghosts）惊吓过——不是故事书里那种，而是那亿万曾经真实而强悍、叫人难以相信从此就无害长眠的存在？你可曾掂量过那些你们或许称作恶魔（devils）或魔鬼（Demons）的东西——能横穿全部时间与空间、穿过恒星滚烫的心脏和星系之间那副冰冷骸骨的精灵？你可曾想过，整个宇宙兴许是一场疯癫错乱的梦？只要你有过，那就是变更战争的暗示。

===Original===
How I got recruited into the Change War, how it’s conducted, what the two sides are, why you don’t consciously know about it, what I really think about it—you’ll learn in due course.

===Chinese===
我是怎么被招募（Recruited）进变更战争的，这场战争怎么打，交战双方都是谁，你们为什么对它没有自觉的认识，还有我心里对它的真实想法——到时候你自会知道。

---

===Original===
The place outside the cosmos where I and my pals do our nursing job I simply call the Place. A lot of my nursing consists of amusing and humanizing Soldiers fresh back from raids into time. In fact, my formal title is Entertainer and I’ve got my silly side, as you’ll find out.

My pals are two other gals and three guys from quite an assortment of times and places. We’re a pretty good team, and with Sid bossing, we run a pretty good Recuperation Station, though we have our family troubles. But most of our troubles come slamming into the Place with the beat-up Soldiers, who’ve generally just been going through hell and want to raise some of their own. As a matter of fact, it was three newly arrived Soldiers who started this thing I’m going to tell you about, this thing that showed me so much about myself and everything.

===Chinese===
我和伙伴们在宇宙之外干护理活的那处地界，我就径直管它叫「场所」（the Place）。我的护理营生里有好大一块，是给刚从时空突袭里折返的士兵逗闷子、找回合乎人情的感觉。说真的，我的正式头衔是招待员（Entertainer），何况我这人本来就有股傻气，这一点往后你会领教的。

我的伙伴是另外两个姑娘加三个爷们，来自形形色色的时代和地界。我们这拨班子挺不赖，有西德（Sid）掌舵，我们把这座疗养站（Recuperation Station）经营得有模有样，虽说家里也免不了磕磕碰碰。不过我们的大多数麻烦，都是跟着那些被打得焦头烂额的士兵一道横冲直撞进场所来的——这帮人通常刚从地狱里滚了一遭，正巴不得自己也掀一座起来。说实在的，拉开我这就开讲的这件事的，正是三名新到的士兵；这件事让我把自己看了个透，也把其余一切看了个透。

===Original===
When it started, I had been on the Big Time for a thousand sleeps and two thousand nightmares, and working in the Place for five hundred-one thousand. This two-nightmares routine every time you lay down your dizzy little head is rough, but you pretend to get used to it because being on the Big Time is supposed to be worth it.

===Chinese===
事情开张那会儿，我已经在大时光里熬过一千次入眠、两千场噩梦，这一千次入眠里有五百次是在场所里当的班。每回把你那颗发晕的小脑袋一搁下，就雷打不动来上两场噩梦，这滋味够呛，可你还得装作习以为常，因为据说身处大时光，是值当的。

===Original===
The Place is midway in size and atmosphere between a large nightclub where the Entertainers sleep in and a small zeppelin hangar decorated for a party, though a zeppelin is one thing we haven’t had yet. You go out of the Place, but not often if you have any sense and if you are an Entertainer like me, into the cold light of a morning filled with anything from the earlier dinosaurs to the later spacemen, who look strangely similar except for size.

===Chinese===
论块头和气氛，场所介于一家招待员睡在店里的特大夜总会和一座装点停当、准备开派的飞艇库之间——虽说飞艇这物件，我们至今还没弄到过一艘。你可以走出场所——可你要是脑子灵光，又是我这样的招待员，就不怎么出去——走进清晨寒冷的光里，那片光里从早期的恐龙到晚期的宇航员一应俱全，这两号东西除了个头，模样像得出奇。

===Original===
Solely on doctor’s orders, I have been on cosmic leave six times since coming to work at the Place, meaning I have had six brief vacations, if you care to call them that, for believe me they are busman’s holidays, considering what goes on in the Place all the time. The last one I spent in Renaissance Rome, where I got a crush on Cesare Borgia, but I got over it. Vacations are for the birds, anyway, because they have to be fitted by the Spiders into serious operations of the Change War, and you can imagine how restful that makes them.

“See those Soldiers changing the past? You stick along with them. Don’t go too far up front, though, but don’t wander off either. Relax and enjoy yourself.”

Ha! Now the kind of recuperation Soldiers get when they come to the Place is a horse of a far brighter color, simply dazzling by comparison. Entertainment is our business and we give them a bang-up time and send them staggering happily back into action, though once in a great while something may happen to throw a wee shadow on the party.

===Chinese===
纯粹是遵医嘱，自打来场所上工，我休过六回宇宙假（cosmic leave），意思是说我度了六个短假——你若愿意这么叫的话——因为相信我，跟场所里昼夜不歇的动静比，那全是「车夫的假日」（译注：busman's holidays，指放假了还得照干本行）。末一回我是在文艺复兴时期的罗马过的，在那儿我对切萨雷·博尔贾（Cesare Borgia）动了心，不过后来好了。再说休假本来就是白搭，因为它们必须由蜘蛛们见缝插针嵌进变更战争的正经行动里去，你尽可以想象那能有多解乏。

「瞧见那些正在改动过去的士兵没？你就跟他们待一块儿。不过别一个劲儿往最前头凑，也别走散了。放轻松，好好玩。」

哈！士兵们上场所来享受的这种疗养，可就是另一匹毛色鲜亮得多的马了（译注：a horse of a far brighter color，戏仿英语成语 a horse of a different color「截然不同的另一码事」），两相对照简直晃眼。娱乐便是我们的本行，我们让他们痛快个够，再让他们乐得踉踉跄跄重返战场——只是偶尔偶尔，也会出点什么事，往这场派对上投下一小片阴影。

---

===Original===
I am dead in some ways, but don’t let that bother you—I am lively enough in others. If you met me in the cosmos, you would be more apt to yak with me or try to pick me up than to ask a cop to do same or a father to douse me with holy water, unless you are one of those hard-boiled reformer types. But you are not likely to meet me in the cosmos, because (bar Basin Street and the Prater) 15th Century Italy and Augustan Rome—until they spoiled it—are my favorite (Ha!) vacation spots and, as I have said, I stick as close to the Place as I can. It is really the nicest Place in the whole Change World. (Crisis! I even *think* of it capitalized!)

===Chinese===
从某些方面讲，我已经死了，可你别让这个绊着——在别的方面，我鲜活得很。你要是在宇宙里撞见我，多半更想同我闲扯两句，或者干脆把我搭讪上手，而不至于去喊警察来办我，或者请你老爸拿圣水浇我——除非你是那种心肠铁硬的改良派。不过你多半不会在宇宙里撞见我，因为（撇开贝森街和普拉特游乐场不谈）15 世纪的意大利和奥古斯都时代的罗马——在他们把后者糟蹋掉之前——是我顶顶中意的（哈！）度假去处，再说我也讲过了，我能贴着场所多近就贴多近。放眼整个变更世界（Change World），它实在是顶惬意的一处。（了不得！我连*想*它，想的都是大写！）

===Original===
Anyhoo, when this thing started, I was twiddling my thumbs on the couch nearest the piano and thinking it was too late to do my fingernails and whoever came in probably wouldn’t notice them anyway.

The Place was jumpy like it always is on an approach and the gray velvet of the Void around us was curdled with the uneasy lights you see when you close your eyes in the dark.

Sid was tuning the Maintainers for the pickup and the right shoulder of his gold-worked gray doublet was streaked where he’d been wiping his face on it with quick ducks of his head.

===Chinese===
总而言之呢，这事儿开张的时候，我正窝在离钢琴顶近的那张躺椅上闲得转大拇指，心里琢磨这会儿再涂指甲已经太晚，反正不管谁进来，八成也不会留神我的指甲。

场所跟每回有「接客」临近时一样，绷得紧紧的，我们四周虚空（the Void）的灰丝绒上，凝着一层不安生的光，就像你在黑暗里合上眼看见的那种。

西德正调校两台维持器（Maintainer）准备接人，他那件绣金线的灰紧身衣右肩上蹭出一道道印子——那是他一遍遍飞快低头、拿肩膀擦脸上的汗蹭出来的。

===Original===
Beauregard was leaning as close as he could over Sid’s other shoulder, one white-trousered knee neatly indenting the rose plush of the control divan, and he wasn’t missing a single flicker of Sid’s old fingers on the dials; Beau’s copilot besides piano player. Beau’s face had that dead blank look it must have had when every double eagle he owned and more he didn’t were riding on the next card to be turned in the gambling saloon on one of those wedding-cake Mississippi steamboats.

Doc was soused as usual, sitting at the bar with his top hat pushed back and his knitted shawl pulled around him, his wide eyes seeing whatever horrors a life in Nazi-occupied Czarist Russia can add to being a drunk Demon in the Change World.

===Chinese===
博雷加德（Beauregard）从西德另一边肩头竭力凑过去，一条裹着白裤的膝盖，端端正正在控制躺椅的玫瑰红长毛绒上压出个坑，西德那双老手在旋钮上的每一下轻颤，他一处不落；博除了是钢琴师，还兼副驾驶。博那张脸木然得没有一丝生气——当年在密西西比河上那些婚礼蛋糕似的明轮蒸汽船上，在赌厅里，他把自己拥有的每一枚双鹰金币、外加好些并不属于他的，全押在下一张翻开的牌上时，脸上想必就是这副模样。

大夫（Doc）照例灌得烂醉，坐在吧台边，大礼帽推到脑后，针织披肩紧紧裹着，一双瞪圆的眼睛里，映着「在纳粹占领下的沙皇俄国熬一辈子」能给「当变更世界里一个醉醺醺的魔鬼」再添上去的种种恐怖。

===Original===
Maud, who is the Old Girl, and Lili—the New Girl, of course—were telling the big beads of their identical pearl necklaces.

You might say that all us Entertainers were a bit edgy; being Demons doesn’t automatically make us brave.

Then the red telltale on the Major Maintainer went out and the Door began to darken in the Void facing Sid and Beau, and I felt Change Winds blowing hard and my heart missed a couple of beats, and the next thing three Soldiers had stepped out of the cosmos and into the Place, their first three steps hitting the floor hard as they changed times and weights.

===Chinese===
莫德（Maud）——就是「老姑娘」（Old Girl）——和莉莉（Lili）——当然是「新姑娘」（New Girl）——正各自捻着一模一样的珍珠项链数珠子。

你可以说，我们这帮招待员个个都有点紧张兮兮；当了魔鬼，并不自动附带胆量。

接着，主维持器上的红色指示灯熄了，西德和博对面的虚空里，门（the Door）开始转暗，我觉出变更之风刮得正凶，心口漏跳了两拍，再一眨眼，三名士兵已经从宇宙跨进了场所，头三步踏得地板咚咚作响——他们身上的时代和体重正跟着换挡。

---

===Original===
They were dressed as officers of hussars, as we’d been advised, and—praise the Bonny Dew!—I saw that the first of them was Erich, my own dear little commandant, the pride of the von Hohenwalds and the Terror of the Snakes. Behind him was some hard-faced Roman or other, and beside Erich and shouldering into him as they stamped forward was a new boy, blond, with a face like a Greek god who’s just been touring a Christian hell.

===Chinese===
他们按事先关照的，扮作骠骑兵军官——而且，赞美好天神（the Bonny Dew）！（译注：格蕾塔仿法语 le bon Dieu「好天主」捏出的谐音诨名）——我看见头一个便是埃里希（Erich），我亲爱的小指挥官，冯·霍亨瓦尔德家的骄傲，叫蛇方闻风丧胆的凶神。他身后跟着某个面色冷硬的罗马人，而挤在埃里希身边、踏着步子朝前拱的，是个新来的小子，金头发，一张脸活像刚参观完一遍基督教地狱的希腊天神。

===Original===
They were uniformed exactly alike in black—shakos, fur-edged pelisses, boots, and so forth—with white skull emblems on the shakos. The only difference between them was that Erich had a Caller on his wrist and the New Boy had a black-gauntleted glove on his left hand and was clenching the mate in it, his right hand being bare like both of Erich’s and the Roman’s.

===Chinese===
三个人一身黑制服，齐整得分毫不差——筒状军帽、毛皮镶边的骑兵短外套、马靴，诸如此类——军帽上一律缀着白色骷髅徽记。三人唯一的分别在于：埃里希腕上扣着一台呼叫器（Caller），新小子左手上套着一只黑长手套，手套里攥着与它配对的那只，右手光着——跟埃里希与罗马人的双手一样，都是空手。

===Original===
“You’ve made it, lads, hearts of gold,” Sid boomed at them, and Beau twitched a smile and murmured something courtly and Maud began to chant, “Shut the Door!” and the New Girl copied her and I joined in because the Change Winds do blow like crazy when the Door is open, even though it can’t ever be shut tight enough to keep them from leaking through.

“Shut it before it blows wrinkles in our faces,” Maud called in her gamin voice to break the ice, looking like a skinny teenager in the tight, knee-length frock she’d copied from the New Girl.

===Chinese===
「到啦，小伙子们，一颗颗金子心，」西德朝他们朗声喝道，博扯出一丝笑，低低咕哝了句什么客套话，莫德已经开始念咒似的唱：「把门关上！」新姑娘跟着唱，我也掺了进去——门敞着的时候，变更之风（Change Winds）实在刮得邪乎，虽说它从来也没法关得严丝合缝，一丝风都挡不住。

「趁它还没往咱们脸上吹出皱纹来，快关上。」莫德用她那顽童似的脆嗓喊道，想松快松快空气；她穿着打新姑娘那儿照抄来的紧身及膝小连衣裙，活脱脱一个瘦骨伶仃的少女。

===Original===
But the three Soldiers weren’t paying attention. The Roman—I remembered his name was Mark—was blundering forward stiffly as if there were something wrong with his eyes, while Erich and the New Boy were yelling at each other about a kid and Einstein and a summer palace and a bloody glove and the Snakes having booby-trapped Saint Petersburg. Erich had that taut sadistic smile he gets when he wants to hit me.

===Chinese===
可那三名士兵压根没搭理。罗马人——我想起他叫马克（Mark）——直挺挺跌跌撞撞往前闯，好像眼睛出了毛病；埃里希和新小子却面对面吼开了，为着一个小孩、爱因斯坦、一座夏宫、一只血手套，还有什么蛇方在圣彼得堡埋了饵雷，冲着彼此大吼。埃里希脸上挂着那种绷紧的、虐待狂式的笑——他想动手揍我的时候，就是这副笑。

===Original===
The New Boy was in a tearing rage. “Why’d you pull us out so bloody fast? We fair chewed the Nevsky Prospekt to pieces galloping away.”

“Didn’t you feel their stun guns, *Dummkopf*, when they sprung the trap—too soon, *Gott sei Dank*?” Erich demanded.

“I did,” the New Boy told him. “Not enough to numb a cat. Why didn’t you show us action?”

“Shut up. I’m your leader. I’ll show you action enough.”

“You won’t. You’re a filthy Nazi coward.”

“*Weibischer Engländer!*”

“Bloody Hun!”

“*Schlange!*”

===Chinese===
新小子火冒三丈。「你干嘛把咱们拽出来拽得那么急？咱们一路狂奔，差点把涅瓦大街嚼成了碎片。」

「陷阱弹开的时候——弹得可是太早，*Gott sei Dank*（谢天谢地）——你没觉出他们的眩晕枪吗，*Dummkopf*（蠢货）？」埃里希质问。

「觉出了，」新小子回他，「麻一只猫都麻不倒。你干嘛不亮出点真家伙给咱们瞧瞧？」

「闭嘴。我是你们的头儿。真家伙，我会让你们瞧个够。」

「你不会的。你是个肮脏的纳粹胆小鬼。」

「*Weibischer Engländer!*（娘娘腔英国佬！）」

「血腥的德国佬！」

「*Schlange!*（毒蛇！）」

===Original===
The blond lad knew enough German to understand that last crack. He threw back his sable-edged pelisse to clear his sword arm and he swung away from Erich, which bumped him into Beau. At the first sign of the quarrel, Beau had raised himself from the divan as quickly and silently as a—no, I won’t use that word—and slithered over to them.

“Sirs, you forget yourselves,” he said sharply, off balance, supporting himself on the New Boy’s upraised arm. “This is Sidney Lessingham’s Place of Entertainment and Recuperation. There are ladies—”

===Chinese===
金发小子懂得的那点德语，足够他听懂这最后一记损人的。他把貂皮镶边的外套往后一甩，腾出持剑的手臂，整个人从埃里希身边抡了开去，这一下撞上了博。争吵刚冒头，博就从躺椅上起了身，那份迅捷无声，活像一条——不，这个字眼我才不用——径直朝他们滑了过去。

「两位先生，你们失态了，」他厉声喝道，自己却站了个趔趄，撑在新小子扬起的手臂上。「此处是悉尼·莱辛厄姆（Sidney Lessingham）的娱乐疗养之所。此处还有女士——」

---

===Original===
With a contemptuous snarl, the New Boy shoved him off and snatched with his bare hand for his saber. Beau reeled against the divan, it caught him in the shins and he fell toward the Maintainers. Sid whisked them out of the way as if they were a couple of beach radios—simply nothing in the Place is nailed down—and had them back on the coffee table before Beau hit the floor. Meanwhile, Erich had his saber out and had parried the New Boy’s first wild slash and lunged in return, and I heard the scream of steel and the rutch of his boot on the diamond-studded pavement.

===Chinese===
新小子轻蔑地嗥了一声，把他搡开，光着手就去抓佩剑。博一个趔趄撞上躺椅，躺椅磕了他的小腿，他朝维持器栽过去。西德把两台维持器一把掂开，仿佛它们不过是两台海滩收音机——场所里简直没有一件东西是钉死的——又在博扑到地板上之前把它们放回了咖啡桌。与此同时，埃里希已经拔剑出鞘，格开新小子劈头第一记乱斩，回敬一记突刺，我听见钢铁的啸叫，还有他的靴子碾上钻石缀饰地面时那一声刮响。

---

===Original===
Beau rolled over and came up pulling from the ruffles of his shirt bosom a derringer I knew was some other weapon in disguise—a stun gun or even an Atropos. Besides scaring me damp for Erich and everybody, that brought me up short: us Entertainers’ nerves must be getting as naked as the Soldiers’, probably starting when the Spiders canceled all cosmic leaves twenty sleeps back.

===Chinese===
博打个滚爬起来，从衬衫胸前的褶皱里抽出一支小手枪——我知道那其实是别的什么家伙装扮的：一支眩晕枪，或者干脆是一台阿特罗波斯（Atropos）。这除了把我为埃里希和所有人的心吓得汗津津，还叫我猛地一凛：咱们招待员的神经，怕也快晒得跟士兵们一样赤条条了，多半打二十次入眠前蜘蛛们取消全部宇宙假那阵子就开始了。

===Original===
Sid shot Beau his look of command, rapped out, “I’ll handle this, you whoreson firebrand,” and turned to the Minor Maintainer. I noticed that the telltale on the Major was glowing a reassuring red again, and I found a moment to thank Mamma Devi that the Door was shut.

Maud was jumping up and down, cheering I don’t know which—nor did she, I bet—and the New Girl was white and I saw that the sabers were working more businesslike. Erich’s flicked, flicked, flicked again and came away from the blond lad’s cheek spilling a couple of red drops. The blond lad lunged fiercely, Erich jumped back, and the next moment they were both floating helplessly in the air, twisting like they had cramps.

===Chinese===
西德朝博甩过去一道号令的眼神，脆声喝道：「这儿我来料理，你这婊子养的纵火坯。」说着转向副维持器。我留意到主维持器上的指示灯又亮回了那叫人安心的红，我还挤出一星工夫感谢德维妈妈（Mamma Devi）：门，关上了。

莫德蹦得老高，一上一下，不知给哪边叫好——我敢打赌连她自己也未必知道——新姑娘脸色煞白，我看得出，两口军刀越斗越见章法。埃里希的剑尖一挑、一挑、又一挑，从金发小子颊边撤回来，洒下两滴红。金发小子凶狠一记直刺，埃里希向后跃开，再一眨眼，两个人都没着没落飘在了半空，抽筋似的扭动。

===Original===
I realized quick enough that Sid had shut off gravity in the Door and Stores sectors of the Place, leaving the rest of us firm on our feet in the Refresher and Surgery sectors. The Place has sectional gravity to suit our Extraterrestrial buddies—those crazy E.T.s sometimes come whooping in for recuperation in very mixed batches.

From his central position, Sid called out, kindly enough but taking no nonsense, “All right, lads, you’ve had your fun. Now sheathe those swords.”

For a second or so, the two black hussars drifted and contorted. Erich laughed harshly and neatly obeyed—the commandant is used to free fall. The blond lad stopped writhing, hesitated while he glared upside down at Erich and managed to get his saber into its scabbard, although he turned a slow somersault doing it. Then Sid switched on their gravity, slow enough so they wouldn’t get sprained landing.

===Chinese===
我很快便醒过神来：西德把场所里门区和库房区的重力给掐了，我们其余的人仍稳稳当当站在盥洗区和手术区的地界上。场所的重力是分区调配的，为的是将就我们那些外星弟兄——那些疯狂的 E.T. 有时会呼啸而至，拖家带口、五花八门地来疗养。

西德在场地正中发话，口气够和软，却半点不容胡闹：「行了，小伙子们，乐子也乐够了。这就把剑入鞘。」

有那么一两秒，两名黑衣骠骑兵在半空漂着、拧着。埃里希刺耳地笑了一声，干脆利落地照办——指挥官是惯了自由落体的。金发小子不拧了，倒悬着朝埃里希瞪了一眼，犹豫片刻，竟把剑捅回了鞘，尽管为此慢悠悠翻了一个筋斗。随后西德把他们那片的重力重新拧开，拧得够慢，免得他们落地崴了脚。

---

===Original===
Erich laughed, lightly this time, and stepped out briskly toward us. He stopped to clap the New Boy firmly on the shoulder and look him in the face.

“So, now you get a good scar,” he said.

===Chinese===
埃里希笑了，这回轻快了些，脚下利落地朝我们迈过来。他停下来，在新小子肩上结结实实一拍，直望着他的脸。

「瞧，这下你挣到一道好疤了。」他说。

===Original===
The other didn’t pull away, but he didn’t look up and Erich came on. Sid was hurrying toward the New Boy, and as he passed Erich, he wagged a finger at him and gayly said, “You rogue.” Next thing I was giving Erich my “Man, you’re home” hug and he was kissing me and cracking my ribs and saying, “*Liebchen! Doppchen!*”—which was fine with me because I do love him and I’m a good lover and as much a Doubleganger as he is.

===Chinese===
对方没有闪开，却也不抬眼，埃里希便接着往前走。西德正大步赶往新小子那儿，打埃里希身边过的时候，朝他晃了晃一根指头，兴冲冲道：「你这泼皮。」紧接着，我就在给埃里希我的「伙计，你可到家了」式拥抱，他吻着我，把我肋骨勒得咯咯响，嘴里念：「*Liebchen*（亲爱的）！*Doppchen*（小二重身）！」——这正中我下怀，因为我确实爱他，而且我是个顶好的情人，跟他一样，是个不折不扣的二重身（Doubleganger）。

===Original===
We had just pulled back from each other to get a breath—his blue eyes looked so sweet in his worn face—when there was a thud behind us. With the snapping of the tension, Doc had fallen off his bar stool and his top hat was over his eyes. As we turned to chuckle at him, Maud squeaked and we saw that the Roman had walked straight up against the Void and was marching along there steadily without gaining a foot, like it does happen, his black uniform melting into that inside-your-head gray.

===Chinese===
我们刚从彼此怀里撤开换口气——他一双蓝眼睛搁在那张劳损的脸上，瞧着那么甜——身后咚地一声。绷了半天的弦一松，大夫从吧台凳上出溜了下去，大礼帽扣在眼睛上。我们转身正要笑话他，莫德尖叫一声：那罗马人一头直挺挺顶上了虚空，还在那儿一板一眼地行军，半尺也没挪——这种事倒也真出过——他的黑制服一点点化进那片「你脑子内侧的」灰。

===Original===
Maud and Beau rushed over to fish him back, which can be tricky. The thin gambler was all courtly efficiency again. Sid supervised from a distance.

“What’s wrong with him?” I asked Erich.

===Chinese===
莫德和博抢过去把他钓回来——这活计有时候挺玄。瘦削的赌徒又是一派优雅干练。西德隔开一段督着阵。

「他这是怎么了？」我问埃里希。

===Original===
He shrugged. “Overdue for Change Shock. And he was nearest the stun guns. His horse almost threw him. *Mein Gott*, you should have seen Saint Petersburg, *Liebchen*: the Nevsky Prospekt, the canals flying by like reception carpets of blue sky, a cavalry troop in blue and gold that blundered across our escape, fine women in furs and ostrich plumes, a monk with a big tripod and his head under a hood—it gave me the horrors seeing all those Zombies flashing past and staring at me in that sick unawakened way they have, and knowing that some of them, say the photographer, might be Snakes.”

===Chinese===
他耸耸肩。「变更休克早过了期限还没发作。再说，离眩晕枪顶近的就是他。他的马差点把他颠下来。*Mein Gott*（我的老天），你真该瞧瞧圣彼得堡，*Liebchen*（亲爱的）：涅瓦大街；一条条运河飞掠而过，活像一方方蓝天的迎宾地毯；一支蓝底金饰的骑兵队阴差阳错，横穿我们的逃亡路线；披毛皮、插鸵鸟毛的美人；一个扛着大三脚架、脑袋蒙在黑布里的修士——眼看着那一个个僵尸（Zombies）扑面闪过，用他们那副病恹恹、未醒透的眼神瞪我，心里还明白他们里头保不齐哪位——比方说那位照相师——就是蛇方，我这心里直冒凉气。」

===Original===
Our side in the Change War is the Spiders, the other side is the Snakes, though all of us—Spiders and Snakes alike—are Doublegangers and Demons too, because we’re cut out of our lifelines in the cosmos. Your lifeline is all of you from birth to death. We’re Doublegangers because we can operate both in the cosmos and outside of it, and Demons because we act reasonably alive while doing so—which the Ghosts don’t. Entertainers and Soldiers are all Demon-Doublegangers, whichever side they’re on—though they say the Snake Places are simply ghastly. Zombies are dead people whose lifelines lie in the so-called past.

===Chinese===
在变更战争里，我们这一方是蜘蛛（the Spiders），另一方是蛇（the Snakes），不过我们大伙儿——蜘蛛也好，蛇也好——也个个都是二重身兼魔鬼，因为我们都是从宇宙里自己的生命线上剜出来的。你的生命线，就是你打出生到断气的全部。我们是二重身，因为我们既能在宇宙里，也能在宇宙外头活动；是魔鬼，因为我们这么干的时候还显得有几分生气——幽灵们可办不到。招待员和士兵，不论替哪边卖命，一律是魔鬼兼二重身——虽说人们讲，蛇方的场所简直是恶梦成了精。僵尸呢，是生命线摊在所谓「过去」里的死人。

---

===Original===
“What were you doing in Saint Petersburg before the ambush?” I asked Erich. “That is, if you can talk about it.”

“Why not? We were kidnapping the infant Einstein back from the Snakes in 1883. Yes, the Snakes got him, *Liebchen*, only a few sleeps back, endangering the West’s whole victory over Russia—”

===Chinese===
「中埋伏之前，你们在圣彼得堡干什么来着？」我问埃里希。「当然，若是能讲的话。」

「有什么不能讲？我们是回 1883 年，把襁褓里的爱因斯坦从蛇方手里重新绑回来。没错，蛇方把他抢到手了，*Liebchen*，就在没几个入眠之前，这可威胁到西方对俄国的整个胜局——」

===Original===
“—which gave your dear little Hitler the world on a platter for fifty years and got me loved to death by your sterling troops in the Liberation of Chicago—”

“—but which leads to the ultimate victory of the Spiders and the West over the Snakes and Communism, *Liebchen*, remember that. Anyway, our counter-snatch didn’t work. The Snakes had guards posted—most unusual and we weren’t warned. The whole thing was a great mess. No wonder Bruce lost his head—not that it excuses him.”

===Chinese===
「——这场胜局让你那位亲爱的小希特勒白白端走了五十年的天下，还让我在芝加哥解放战里，差点被你们那些一等一的队伍『爱』到死——」

「——可顺着它走到头，是蜘蛛和西方压倒蛇方和共产主义的最终胜利，*Liebchen*，记住这一条。总而言之，我们这趟反截胡没成。蛇方设了岗哨——极不寻常，而且事先没人给咱们递话。整件事乱成了一锅粥。也难怪布鲁斯（Bruce）昏了头——虽说这开脱不了他。」

===Original===
“The New Boy?” I asked. Sid hadn’t got to him and he was still standing with hooded eyes where Erich had left him, a dark pillar of shame and rage.

“*Ja*, a lieutenant from World War One. An Englishman.”

“I gathered that,” I told Erich. “Is he really effeminate?”

“*Weibischer?*” He smiled. “I had to call him something when he said I was a coward. He’ll make a fine Soldier—only needs a little more shaping.”

“You men are so original when you spat.” I lowered my voice. “But you shouldn’t have gone on and called him a Snake, Erich mine.”

“*Schlange?*” The smile got crooked. “Who knows—about any of us? As Saint Petersburg showed me, the Snakes’ spies are getting cleverer than ours.” The blue eyes didn’t look sweet now. “Are you, *Liebchen*, really nothing more than a good loyal Spider?”

“Erich!”

“All right, I went too far—with Bruce and with you too. We’re all hacked these days, riding with one leg over the breaking edge.”

===Chinese===
「新小子？」我问。西德还没挪到他跟前，他还站在埃里希撂下他的地方，眼皮半耷，像一根用羞愤铸成的黑柱子。

「*Ja*（是），第一次世界大战来的陆军中尉。英国人。」

「这我听出来了，」我告诉埃里希。「他当真女里女气？」

「*Weibischer*（娘娘腔）？」他莞尔一笑。「他骂我胆小鬼，我总得回敬他个什么。他会是个好士兵——只欠再捏一捏形。」

「你们男人对骂起来，可真有原创精神。」我压低了嗓音。「可你不该顺杆爬，骂他是蛇，我的埃里希。」

「*Schlange*（毒蛇）？」那笑歪了。「谁说得准呢——咱们中的哪一个？圣彼得堡那趟就叫我看明白了，蛇方的探子比咱们的越发精灵。」蓝眼睛这下可一点不甜了。「那你呢，*Liebchen*，当真不过是一条忠心耿耿的好蜘蛛？」

「埃里希！」

「好吧，是我过了头——对布鲁斯，对你也是。这些日子咱们全都垮着半口气，骑在断崖边上，一条腿已经悬了出去。」

===Original===
Maud and Beau were supporting the Roman to a couch, Maud taking most of his weight, with Sid still supervising and the New Boy still sulking by himself. The New Girl should have been with him, of course, but I couldn’t see her anywhere and I decided she was probably having a nervous breakdown in the Refresher, the little jerk.

“The Roman looks pretty bad, Erich,” I said.

“Ah, Mark’s tough. Got virtue, as his people say. And our little starship girl will bring him back to life if anybody can and if …”

“… you call this living,” I filled in dutifully.

===Chinese===
莫德和博正架着罗马人往一张躺椅挪，大半分量压在莫德身上，西德还在远处督着，新小子还在一边独自窝火。按理新姑娘该守着他才是，可我四下里瞧不见她，便断定她多半正窝在盥洗区里闹精神崩溃呢，小蠢货。

「罗马人瞧着不大妙，埃里希。」我说。

「啊，马克硬朗着呢。用他自己同胞的话说，有『勇德』。再说，咱们的小星舰姑娘比谁都能叫他起死回生，只要……」

「……你还肯管这叫活。」我尽本分地接了下茬。

---

===Original===
He was right. Maud had fifty-odd years of psychomedical experience, 23rd Century at that. It should have been Doc’s job, but that was fifty drunks back.

===Chinese===
他说得没错。莫德有五十来年心理医学的经验，还是 23 世纪水平的。这本该是大夫的分内事，可那得往回数五十场醉。

===Original===
“Maud and Mark, that will be an interesting experiment,” Erich said. “Reminiscent of Goering’s with the frozen men and the naked gypsy girls.”

“You are a filthy Nazi. She’ll be using electrophoresis and deep suggestion, if I know anything.”

“How will you be able to know anything, *Liebchen*, if she switches on the couch curtains, as I perceive she is preparing to do?”

“Filthy Nazi I said and meant.”

“Precisely.” He clicked his heels and bowed a millimeter. “Erich Friederich von Hohenwald, *Oberleutnant* in the army of the Third Reich. Fell at Narvik, where he was Recruited by the Spiders. Lifeline lengthened by a Big Change after his first death and at latest report Commandant of Toronto, where he maintains extensive baby farms to provide him with breakfast meat, if you believe the handbills of the *voyageurs* underground. At your service.”

===Chinese===
「莫德加马克，这将是场有趣的实验，」埃里希说。「叫人想起戈林拿冻僵的汉子和光屁股吉卜赛姑娘做的那几场。」

「你可真是个肮脏的纳粹。要是我还懂点什么的话，她要用的会是电泳法和深度暗示。」

「她要是把躺椅的围幔拉上——我瞧她正打算这么办——你又打哪儿懂去呢，*Liebchen*？」

「肮脏的纳粹，我说了，也是这个意思。」

「正是。」他碰响靴跟，躬了一毫米的身。「埃里希·弗里德里希·冯·霍亨瓦尔德（Erich Friederich von Hohenwald），第三帝国国防军*Oberleutnant*（中尉）。阵亡于纳尔维克，就在那里被蜘蛛方招募。此后一场大变更又把他的生命线抻长了——那是他头一遭阵亡之后的事——据最新通报，现任多伦多指挥官，在当地广开婴儿农场，给他供应早餐肉，这话你若信得过*voyageurs*（远行者）地下组织的传单的话。听候差遣。」

===Original===
“Oh, Erich, it’s all so lousy,” I said, touching his hand, reminded that he was one of the unfortunates Resurrected from a point in their lifelines well before their deaths—in his case, because the date of his death had been shifted forward by a Big Change after his Resurrection. And as every Demon finds out, if he can’t imagine it beforehand, it is pure hell to remember your future, and the shorter the time between your Resurrection and your death back in the cosmos, the better. Mine, bless Bab-ed-Din, was only an action-packed ten minutes on North Clark Street.

===Chinese===
「噢，埃里希，这一切都糟透了。」我说，碰了碰他的手，心里想起他属于那类倒霉人：被复活（Resurrected）时取的是生命线上远在死亡之前的节点——他的情形是，复活之后一场大变更把他死期往后挪了。而每个魔鬼迟早会发现：这事要是事先想象不来，那么「记得你的未来」就是不折不扣的地狱；复活点到你在宇宙里咽气那一站之间，隔得越短越好。我那一程，托巴布丁（Bab-ed-Din）的福，不过是北克拉克街上紧凑热闹的十分钟。

===Original===
Erich put his other hand lightly over mine. “Fortunes of the Change War, *Liebchen*. At least I’m a Soldier and sometimes assigned to future operations—though why we should have this monomania about our future personalities back there, I don’t know. Mine is a stupid *Oberst*, thin as paper—and frightfully indignant at the *voyageurs*! But it helps me a little if I see him in perspective and at least I get back to the cosmos pretty regularly, *Gott sei Dank*, so I’m better off than you Entertainers.”

===Chinese===
埃里希把另一只手轻轻覆上我的手。「变更战争的世道沉浮，*Liebchen*。好歹我是个士兵，有时还能派去未来战线的行动——虽说我不明白，咱们干嘛对自己在那边的未来人格这么一根筋。我那位是个蠢透的*Oberst*（上校），薄得跟纸似的——还叫那帮*voyageurs*（远行者）气得七窍生烟！不过能把他摆远了看，多少帮我撑一撑，况且我到底还能隔三差五回趟宇宙，*Gott sei Dank*（谢天谢地），所以比你们招待员的处境强。」

===Original===
I didn’t say aloud that a Changing cosmos is worse than none, but I found myself sending a prayer to the Bonny Dew for my father’s repose, that the Change Winds would blow lightly across the lifeline of Anton A. Forzane, professor of physiology, born in Norway and buried in Chicago. Woodlawn Cemetery is a nice gray spot.

“That’s all right, Erich,” I said. “We Entertainers Got Mittens too.”

He scowled around at me suspiciously, as if he were wondering whether I had all my buttons on.

“Mittens?” he said. “What do you mean? I’m not wearing any. Are you trying to say something about Bruce’s gloves—which incidentally seem to annoy him for some reason. No, seriously, Greta, why do you Entertainers need mittens?”

“Because we get cold feet sometimes. At least I do. Got Mittens, as I say.”

===Chinese===
我没说出口——一个不断被改来改去的宇宙，比干脆没有宇宙还糟——却发觉自己已经向好天神祷告起来，为我父亲的安息，求变更之风轻些拂过安东·A·福尔赞（Anton A. Forzane）的生命线：生理学教授，生在挪威，葬在芝加哥。伍德劳恩墓园是块挺不错的灰色地界。

「这不碍事，埃里希，」我说。「咱们招待员也拿到连指手套（Got Mittens）了。」（译注：谐音德语 Gott mit uns「上帝与我们同在」，德军腰带扣上的铭文。）

他狐疑地四下里朝我皱眉打量，好像在掂量我脑子里是不是缺了颗螺丝。

「连指手套？」他说。「什么意思？我可一双也没戴。你是不是想影射布鲁斯那副手套——顺带一提，那东西好像不知怎么正招他烦。不，说正经的，格蕾塔，你们招待员要连指手套干什么？」

「因为咱们有时脚底发凉（译注：英语 cold feet，兼有『临阵胆怯』之意）。至少我是这样。所以说，连指手套是拿到了的。」

---

===Original===
A sickly light dawned in his Prussian puss. He muttered, “Got mittens … *Gott mit uns* … God with us,” and roared softly, “Greta, I don’t know how I put up with you, the way you murder a great language for cheap laughs.”

===Chinese===
他那张普鲁士脸盘上，透出一丝病恹恹的曙光。他喃喃道：「Got mittens……*Gott mit uns*（上帝与我们同在）……」随即压着嗓门吼：「格蕾塔，真不知道我怎么受得了你，就为你几个廉价笑声，这么作践一门伟大的语言。」

===Original===
“You’ve got to take me as I am,” I told him, “mittens and all, thank the Bonny Dew—” and hastily explained, “That’s French—*le bon Dieu*—the good God—don’t hit me. I’m not going to tell you any more of my secrets.”

He laughed feebly, like he was dying.

“Cheer up,” I said. “I won’t be here forever, and there are worse places than the Place.”

He nodded grudgingly, looking around. “You know what, Greta, if you’ll promise not to make some dreadful joke out of it: on operations, I pretend I’ll soon be going backstage to court the world-famous ballerina Greta Forzane.”

===Chinese===
「你就得照我本来的样子收下我，」我告诉他，「连指手套一并全收，谢谢好天神——」又赶忙解释，「那是法语——*le bon Dieu*——好天主——别打我。我的秘密，再不告诉你半个。」

他有气无力地笑了一声，像要断气。

「振作点，」我说。「我不会在这儿赖一辈子，再说比场所糟心的地方有的是。」

他不情不愿地点点头，四下张望。「跟你说个事，格蕾塔，只要你担保别拿它编什么可怕的笑话：出任务的时候，我就假想自己马上要进后台，去巴结那位举世闻名的芭蕾女伶格蕾塔·福尔赞。」

===Original===
He was right about the backstage part. The Place is a regular theater-in-the-round with the Void for an audience, the Void’s gray hardly disturbed by the screens masking Surgery (Ugh!), Refresher and Stores. Between the last two are the bar and kitchen and Beau’s piano. Between Surgery and the sector where the Door usually appears are the shelves and taborets of the Art Gallery. The control divan is stage center. Spaced around at a fair distance are six big low couches—one with its curtains now shooting up into the gray—and a few small tables. It is like a ballet set and the crazy costumes and characters that turn up don’t ruin the illusion. By no means. Diaghilev would have hired most of them for the Ballet Russe on first sight, without even asking them whether they could keep time to music.

===Chinese===
后台这半截，他没说错。场所就是一座地道的中心舞台剧场，观众席便由虚空充任；遮着手术区（呕！）、盥洗区和库房区的那几面屏风，几乎搅不动那一片灰。后两者之间，是吧台、厨房和博的钢琴。手术区与门惯常现身的那一区之间，是艺术品廊的一格格架子和小圆柜。控制躺椅正在舞台中央。隔开些距离，四下里摆着六张宽大的矮躺椅——其中一张的围幔这会儿正嗖嗖窜上灰暗里——另有几张小桌。整个像一出芭蕾舞剧的布景，而那些冒出来的疯癫行头和角色，一点儿也败不了这个幻象。压根儿败不了。佳吉列夫见了他们中大多数，头一面就会签进俄国芭蕾舞团，连问都不问一声他们踩不踩得上音乐的拍子。

## II A Right-Hand Glove / 一只右手手套

===Original===
> Last week in Babylon,
 

 Last night in Rome,

===Chinese===
> 上礼拜在巴比伦，

> 昨夜在罗马，

---

===Original===
Beau had gone behind the bar and was talking quietly at Doc, but with his eyes elsewhere, looking very sallow and professional in his white, and I thought—Damballa!—I’m in the French Quarter. I couldn’t see the New Girl. Sid was at last getting to the New Boy after the fuss about Mark. He threw me a sign and I started over with Erich in tow.

===Chinese===
博已经绕到吧台后头，正低声跟大夫说着话，眼睛却望着别处，一身白更衬得他蜡黄而专业，我心说——丹巴拉（Damballa，伏都教蛇神）！——我这是进了新奥尔良的法语区。新姑娘我没瞧见。折腾完马克那一通，西德总算腾出手来照应新小子。他朝我打了个暗号，我便拖着埃里希启程过去。

===Original===
“Welcome, sweet lad. Sidney Lessingham’s your host, and a fellow Englishman. Born in King’s Lynn, 1564, schooled at Cambridge, but London was the life and death of me, though I outlasted Bessie, Jimmie, Charlie, and Ollie almost. And what a life! By turns a clerk, a spy, a bawd—the two trades are hand in glove—a poet of no account, a beggar, and a peddler of resurrection tracts. Beau Lassiter, our throats are tinder!”

At the word “poet,” the New Boy looked up, but resentfully, as if he had been tricked into it.

“And to spare your throat for drinking, sweet gallant, I’ll be so bold as to guess and answer one of your questions,” Sid rattled on. “Yes, I knew Will Shakespeare—we were of an age—and he was such a modest, mind-your-business rogue that we all wondered whether he really did write those plays. Your pardon, ’faith, but that scratch might be looked to.”

===Chinese===
「欢迎欢迎，可爱的小伙子。悉尼·莱辛厄姆这厢做东，还是你的英国同胞。生在金斯林，1564 年，剑桥念的书，可伦敦才是我的生死之地，虽然我把贝西、吉米、查理都熬死了，跟奥利嘛，也就差一口气（译注：指伊丽莎白一世、詹姆士一世、查理一世和克伦威尔）。这日子过的！先后当过书记、密探、老鸨——这两门营生本来就是穿一只手套的——不入流的诗人、叫花子，还有专卖复活小册子的货郎。博·拉西特（Beau Lassiter），咱们的喉咙干成火绒啦！」

听到「诗人」二字，新小子抬起了头，却是满脸不情愿，仿佛中了圈套才认下这个身份。

「为了省下你的喉咙好喝酒，可爱的骑士，我斗胆猜上一猜、答上一答你的一个问题，」西德滔滔不绝。「没错，我认得威尔·莎士比亚——咱们同庚——他是那么个低调、不多管闲事的滑头，以至于我们全都犯嘀咕，那些戏到底是不是他写的。恕我冒昧，说真的，你脸上那道口子该料理料理了。」

===Original===
Then I saw that the New Girl hadn’t lost her head, but gone to Surgery (Ugh!) for a first-aid tray. She reached a swab toward the New Boy’s sticky cheek, saying rather shrilly, “If I might …”

Her timing was bad. Sid’s last words and Erich’s approach had darkened the look in the young Soldier’s face and he angrily swept her arm aside without even glancing at her. Erich squeezed my arm. The tray clattered to the floor—and one of the drinks that Beau was bringing almost followed it. Ever since the New Girl’s arrival, Beau had been figuring that she was his responsibility, though I don’t think the two of them had reached an agreement yet. Beau was especially set on it because I was thick with Sid at the time and Maud with Doc, she loving tough cases.

“Easy now, lad, and you love me!” Sid thundered, again shooting Beau the “Hold it” look. “She’s just a poor pagan trying to comfort you. Swallow your bile, you black villain, and perchance it will turn to poetry. Ah, did I touch you there? Confess, you are a poet.”

===Chinese===
这时我才看出，新姑娘并没有慌神，而是去了手术区（呕！）取急救托盘。她把一支药棉探向新小子血糊糊的脸颊，嗓音颇尖地说了句：「要是我可以……」

她这个时机糟透了。西德的末句话和埃里希的逼近，已经让这年轻士兵脸上的神色沉了下去，他恼怒地一挥胳膊把她挡开，连看都没看她一眼。埃里希捏了捏我的胳膊。托盘哗啦摔在地上——博端来的一杯酒也差点跟了去。打新姑娘来那天起，博就一直盘算她是他的责任，虽说我认为他们俩还没就此达成协议来着。博尤其打定这个主意，是因为那阵子我跟西德黏得紧，莫德又跟大夫黏得紧——她就好这口疑难杂症。

「消消气，小子，你要还爱我的话！」西德雷鸣般喝道，又朝博射去那道「稳住」的眼神。「她不过是个可怜的异教徒，想安慰安慰你。把你的火气咽下去，你这黑心的小坏蛋，说不定它能酿成诗呢。啊呀，叫我戳着痛处了？招了吧，你就是个诗人。」

---

===Original===
There isn’t much gets by Sid, though for a second I forgot my psychology and wondered if he knew what he was doing with his insights.

“Yes, I’m a poet, all right,” the New Boy roared. “I’m Bruce Marchant, you bloody Zombies. I’m a poet in a world where even the lines of the King James and your precious Will whom you use for laughs aren’t safe from Snakes’ slime and the Spiders’ dirty legs. Changing our history, stealing our certainties, claiming to be so blasted all-knowing and best intentioned and efficient, and what does it lead to? This bloody S.I. glove!”

===Chinese===
很少有什么能逃过西德的眼睛，虽说有一秒钟我忘了自己的心理学本行，纳闷他那些洞见究竟是有意为之还是无心插柳。

「对，我就是个诗人，没错，」新小子咆哮道。「我是布鲁斯·马钱特（Bruce Marchant），你们这帮该死的僵尸。我是个诗人，活在一个连钦定本《圣经》的字句、连你们拿来当笑料的宝贝威尔都难逃蛇方黏涎和蜘蛛脏腿的世界里。改写咱们的历世，偷走咱们的确定性，口口声声自己该死的全知全能、用心良善、办事高效，到头来落个什么？落这么一只该死的蜘蛛配发（S.I.）手套！」

===Original===
He held up his black-gloved left hand which still held the mate and he shook it.

“What’s wrong with the Spider Issue gauntlet, heart of gold?” Sid demanded. “And you love us, tell us.” While Erich laughed, “Consider yourself lucky, *Kamerad*. Mark and I didn’t draw any gloves at all.”

“What’s wrong with it?” Bruce yelled. “The bloody things are both lefts!” He slammed it down on the floor.

We all howled, we couldn’t help it. He turned his back on us and stamped off, though I guessed he would keep out of the Void. Erich squeezed my arm and said between gasps, “*Mein Gott, Liebchen*, what have I always told you about Soldiers? The bigger the gripe, the smaller the cause! It is infallible!”

===Chinese===
他举起戴黑手套的左手——那只手里还攥着配对的另一只——用力摇晃。

「蜘蛛配发的长手套怎么了，金子心？」西德质问。「你要还爱我们，就说出来。」埃里希则笑道：「你就知足吧，*Kamerad*（伙计）。马克和我压根儿一双也没领到。」

「怎么了？」布鲁斯吼道。「这该死的东西两只都是左手！」他把它掼在地板上。

我们全都嚎起来，忍都忍不住。他掉过背去，跺着脚走开了，不过我猜他还晓得避开虚空。埃里希捏着我的胳膊，喘着气说：「*Mein Gott, Liebchen*（我的老天，亲爱的），关于士兵我常跟你说什么来着？牢骚越大，事由越小！百试百灵！」

===Original===
One of us didn’t laugh. Ever since the New Girl heard the name Bruce Marchant, she’d had a look in her eyes like she’d been given the sacrament. I was glad she’d got interested in something, because she’d been pretty much of a snoot and a wet blanket up until now, although she’d come to the Place with the recommendation of having been a real whoopee girl in London and New York in the Twenties. She looked disapprovingly at us as she gathered up the tray and stuff, not forgetting the glove, which she placed on the center of the tray like a holy relic.

===Chinese===
我们中间有一个没笑。打新姑娘听见布鲁斯·马钱特这个名字起，她眼里就有了一种神色，像刚领过圣餐。我很高兴她总算对什么来了兴趣，因为此前她大抵是副翘鼻子加扫兴样，虽说她来场所时带着的推荐是：二十年代在伦敦和纽约当过真资格的「哇嗷女郎」（whoopee girl，纵情狂欢的时髦女郎）。她收拾托盘和那些零碎时朝我们投来不以为然的目光，也没忘那只手套——她把它端端正正供在托盘中央，像件圣物。

---

===Original===
Beau cut over and tried to talk to her, but she ghosted past him and once again he couldn’t do anything because of the tray in his hands. He came over and got rid of the drinks quick. I took a big gulp right away because I saw the New Girl stepping through the screen into Surgery and I hate to be reminded we have it and I’m glad Doc is too drunk to use it, some of the Arachnoid surgical techniques being very sickening as I know only too well from a personal experience that is number one on my list of things to be forgotten.

===Chinese===
博斜刺里插过去想跟她搭话，可她幽灵似的擦身而过，他又一次因为手里托盘而束手无策。他踱了过来，三下两下把酒打发掉。我立刻灌下一大口，因为我看见新姑娘掀帘进了手术区，而我最怕人提醒咱们有这么个地方，也庆幸大夫醉得没法用它——某些蛛族手术手法非常叫人作呕，这一点我凭一段亲身经历知道得再清楚不过，那段经历在我那张「待忘事清单」上稳居头名。

===Original===
By that time, Bruce had come back to us, saying in a carefully hard voice, “Look here, it’s not the dashed glove itself, as you very well know, you howling Demons.”

“What is it then, noble heart?” Sid asked, his grizzled gold beard heightening the effect of innocent receptivity.

===Chinese===
这时布鲁斯已回到我们中间，拿一种刻意硬起来的嗓音说：「听好了，倒不是这只混账手套本身，这一点你们清楚得很，你们这帮嚎丧的魔鬼。」

「那是什么呢，高贵的心？」西德问，他那一部金灰相间的胡子，把一脸无辜的虚怀若谷衬得更足了。

===Original===
“It’s the principle of the thing,” Bruce said, looking around sharply, but none of us cracked a smile. “It’s this mucking inefficiency and death of the cosmos—and don’t tell me that isn’t in the cards!—masquerading as benign omniscient authority. The Spiders—and we don’t know who they are ultimately; it’s just a name; we see only agents like ourselves—the Spiders pluck us from the quiet graves of our lifelines—”

“Is that bad, lad?” Sid murmured, innocently straight-faced.

“—and Resurrect us if they can and then tell us we must fight another time-traveling power called the Snakes—just a name, too—which is bent on perverting and enslaving the whole cosmos, past, present and future.”

“And isn’t it, lad?”

===Chinese===
「是这里头的原则，」布鲁斯说，目光凌厉地扫视四周，可我们谁也没露出半点笑纹。「是这个操蛋的低效和宇宙的死亡——别跟我说这不在命数里！——偏偏还装扮成一副仁慈全知的权威嘴脸。蜘蛛们——他们归根结底是谁，咱们不知道；不过是个名号；咱们见到的只是跟咱们一样的代理人——蜘蛛们把我们从生命线安静的坟墓里拔出来——」

「那很糟吗，小伙子？」西德喃喃道，一脸无辜的木然。

「——要是办得到，就把我们复活，然后告诉我们，必须同另一个叫蛇方的时间旅行强权作战——也不过是个名号——它一门心思要败坏和奴役整个宇宙，过去、现在和将来。」

「难道不是吗，小伙子？」

===Original===
“Before we’re properly awake, we’re Recruited into the Big Time and hustled into tunnels and burrows outside our space-time, these miserable closets, gray sacks, puss pockets—no offense to this Place—that the Spiders have created, maybe by gigantic implosions, but no one knows for certain, and then we’re sent off on all sorts of missions into the past and future to change history in ways that are supposed to thwart the Snakes.”

“True, lad.”

“And from then on, the pace is so flaming hot and heavy, the shocks come so fast, our emotions are wrenched in so many directions, our public and private metaphysics distorted so insanely, the deepest thread of reality we cling to tied in such bloody knots, that we never can get things straight.”

“We’ve all felt that way, lad,” Sid said soberly; Beau nodded his sleek death’s head; “You should have seen me, *Kamerad*, my first fifty sleeps,” Erich put in; while I added, “Us girls, too, Bruce.”

===Chinese===
「还没等咱们醒利索，咱们就被招募进大时光，塞进咱们时空之外的坑坑洞洞，那些凄凄惨惨的小黑屋、灰口袋、脓疮窝——此地除外，恕我失敬——都是蜘蛛们造出来的，也许是拿天崩地裂的大内爆造的，谁也说不准，然后咱们就被打发去过去和将来执行五花八门的任务，按所谓的遏制蛇方的路数去改写历史。」

「没错，小伙子。」

「打那以后，步子迈得又急又狠，打击接二连三，咱们的情绪被往多少个方向拧，公共的和私人的形而上学被扭曲得丧心病狂，咱们死死攥着的那根最深的现实线被结成一个个血淋淋的死扣，咱们永远理不出个头绪。」

「咱们谁没这么觉得过呢，小伙子，」西德正色道；博点了点他那颗油光水滑的骷髅头；「你真该瞧瞧我头五十次入眠那会儿，*Kamerad*，」埃里希插话；我也添了一句：「我们姑娘家也一样，布鲁斯。」

===Original===
“Oh, I know I’ll get hardened to it, and don’t think I can’t. It’s not that,” Bruce said harshly. “And I wouldn’t mind the personal confusion, the mess it’s made of my spirit, I wouldn’t even mind remaking history and destroying priceless, once-called imperishable beauties of the past, if I felt it were for the best. The Spiders assure us that, to thwart the Snakes, it is all-important that the West ultimately defeat the East. But what have they done to achieve this? I’ll give you some beautiful examples. To stabilize power in the early Mediterranean world, they have built up Crete at the expense of Greece, making Athens a ghost city, Plato a trivial fabulist, and putting all Greek culture in a minor key.”

===Chinese===
「哦，我知道我会硬起心肠习惯的，别以为我做不到。不是那么回事，」布鲁斯声色俱厉。「个人的昏乱、这摊子把我心灵搅成的烂账，我也不在乎；哪怕是重铸历史、毁掉往昔那些曾经号称不朽的无价之美，只要我觉得这是为了至善，我也不会介意。蜘蛛们向我们担保，为了挫败蛇方，西方最终战胜东方是重中之重。可他们为此干了些什么？我给你们举几个漂亮的例子。为了稳定早期地中海世界的权力格局，他们扶植克里特、牺牲希腊，把雅典变成一座幽灵城，把柏拉图变成无足轻重的寓言家，把整个希腊文化调进了小调。」

---

===Original===
“You got time for culture?” I heard myself say and I clapped my hand over my mouth in gentle reproof.

“But *you* remember the dialogues, lad,” Sid observed. “And rail not at Crete—I have a sweet Keftian friend.”

===Chinese===
「你们还有闲心谈文化？」我听见自己脱口而出，随即轻轻一掌拍上自己的嘴，聊作自惩。

「可*你*还记得那些对话录呢，小伙子，」西德点评道。「也别咒骂克里特——我有位甜蜜的凯夫特朋友（译注：Keftian，古埃及人对克里特人的称呼）。」

===Original===
“For how long will I remember Plato’s dialogues? And who after me?” Bruce challenged. “Here’s another. The Spiders want Rome powerful and, to date, they’ve helped Rome so much that she collapses in a blaze of German and Parthian invasions a few years after the death of Julius Caesar.”

This time it was Beau who butted in. Most everybody in the Place loves these bull sessions. “You omit to mention, sir, that Rome’s newest downfall is directly due to the Unholy Triple Alliance the Snakes have fomented between the Eastern Classical World, Mohammedanized Christianity, and Marxist Communism, trying to pass the torch of power futurewards by way of Byzantium and the Eastern Church, without ever letting it pass into the hands of the Spider West. That, sir, is the Snakes’ Three-Thousand-Year Plan which we are fighting against, striving to revive Rome’s glories.”

“Striving is the word for it,” Bruce snapped. “Here’s yet another example. To beat Russia, the Spiders kept England and America out of World War Two, thereby ensuring a German invasion of the New World and creating a Nazi empire stretching from the salt mines of Siberia to the plantations of Iowa, from Nizhni Novgorod to Kansas City!”

===Chinese===
「我还能把柏拉图的对话录记多久？我之后还有谁记得？」布鲁斯发难。「再举一个。蜘蛛们想让罗马强大，而到头来，他们把罗马『帮』得那么厉害，以至于恺撒死后没几年，她就在日耳曼和帕提亚入侵的烈焰里轰然倒塌。」

这回插话的是博。场所里的大多数人都爱这种侃大山。「先生，您漏说了一节：罗马这最近一次的倾覆，直接源于蛇方在东方古典世界、伊斯兰化的基督教和马克思主义共产主义之间煽动起的邪恶三国同盟，他们企图借道拜占庭和东方教会，把权力的火炬朝未来传递，却绝不让它落到蜘蛛西方的手里。先生，那便是我们正与之作战的蛇方三千年计划，我们力争复兴罗马的荣光。」

「『力争』这词用得好，」布鲁斯抢白道。「再举一个例子。为了打败俄国，蜘蛛们把英格兰和美利坚挡在了第二次世界大战之外，从而确保了德国人对新世界的入侵，造出一个纳粹帝国，从西伯利亚的盐矿绵延到艾奥瓦的种植园，从下诺夫哥罗德到堪萨斯城！」

===Original===
He stopped and my short hairs prickled. Behind me, someone was chanting in a weird spiritless voice, like footsteps in hard snow.

“*Salz, Salz, bringe Salz. Kein’ Peitsch’, gnädige Herren. Salz, Salz, Salz.*”

I turned and there was Doc waltzing toward us with little tiny steps, bent over so low that the ends of his shawl touched the floor, his head crooked up sideways and looking through us.

I knew then, but Erich translated softly. “ ‘Salt, salt, I bring salt. No whip, merciful sirs.’ He is speaking to my countrymen in their language.” Doc had spent his last months in a Nazi-operated salt mine.

===Chinese===
他停住口，我后颈的汗毛竖了起来。我背后有人在念诵，嗓音空洞怪异，像坚雪上的脚步声。

「*Salz, Salz, bringe Salz. Kein’ Peitsch’, gnädige Herren. Salz, Salz, Salz.*（盐，盐，我送盐来。别用鞭子，慈悲的老爷们。盐，盐，盐。）」

我转过身，大夫正迈着细碎的小步朝我们跳华尔兹，腰弯得那么低，披肩的两梢都扫着地，脑袋侧着朝上勾起，目光穿透我们望向远处。

我当时就明白了，但埃里希轻声翻译了出来。「『盐，盐，我送盐来。别用鞭子，慈悲的老爷们。』他在用我同胞的语言对他们说话。」大夫的最后几个月，就是在纳粹经营的盐矿里过的。

---

===Original===
He saw us and got up, straightening his top hat very carefully. He frowned hard while my heart thumped half a dozen times. Then his face slackened, he shrugged his shoulders and muttered, “*Nichevo*.”

“And it does not matter, sir,” Beau translated, but directing his remark at Bruce. “True, great civilizations have been dwarfed or broken by the Change War. But others, once crushed in the bud, have bloomed. In the 1870s, I traveled a Mississippi that had never known Grant’s gunboats. I studied piano, languages, and the laws of chance under the greatest European masters at the University of Vicksburg.”

===Chinese===
他看见了我们，直起身来，仔仔细细把大礼帽扶正。他使劲皱着眉，我的心咚咚撞了六七下。随后他的脸松了下来，耸耸肩，咕哝一句：「*Nichevo*（尼切沃，俄语『没什么』）。」

「意思是『不要紧』，先生，」博翻译道，话却是冲着布鲁斯去的。「诚然，伟大的文明被变更战争矮化过、打断过。可另一些曾经被掐死在蕾芽里的，反倒开了花。1870 年代，我游历过一条从未见识过格兰特炮舰的密西西比河。我在维克斯堡大学（University of Vicksburg）师从最伟大的欧洲大师，修习钢琴、语言，还有机遇的法则。」

===Original===
“And you think your pipsqueak steamboat culture is compensation for—” Bruce began but, “Prithee none of that, lad,” Sid interrupted smartly. “Nations are as equal as so many madmen or drunkards, and I’ll drink dead drunk the man who disputes me. Hear reason: nations are not so puny as to shrivel and vanish at the first tampering with their past, no, nor with the tenth. Nations are monsters, boy, with guts of iron and nerves of brass. Waste not your pity on them.”

===Chinese===
「你倒觉得你那点蒸汽船小文化，就能抵偿——」布鲁斯开了腔，可是「求你了，别说这个，小伙子，」西德利落地打断他。「国家这东西，就像一群疯子或醉汉，个个彼此彼此；谁要跟我争，我就跟谁喝到烂醉。听道理：国家没那么孱弱，不至于一碰它的过去就蔫了没了，不会，碰第十次也不会。国家是怪物，小子，生着铁打的肠胃、黄铜铸的神经。别把你的怜悯浪费在它们身上。」

===Original===
“True indeed, sir,” Beau pressed, cooler and keener for the attack on his Greater South. “Most of us enter the Change World with the false metaphysic that the slightest change in the past—a grain of dust misplaced—will transform the whole future. It is a long while before we accept with our minds as well as our intellects the law of the Conservation of Reality: that when the past is changed, the future changes barely enough to adjust, barely enough to admit the new data. The Change Winds meet maximum resistance always. Otherwise the first operation in Babylonia would have wiped out New Orleans, Sheffield, Stuttgart, and Maud Davies’ birthplace on Ganymede!

===Chinese===
「千真万确，先生，」博紧追不舍，他那「大南方」挨了冒犯，人反而更冷静、更锋利。「我们大多数人初进变更世界时，都带着一套错误的形而上学，以为过去改动一丝一毫——一粒尘土放错了地方——就会改写整个未来。要过了好久，我们才连同心灵带头脑一起，接受现实守恒定律：过去改变时，未来只变到刚刚够调整的地步，刚刚够接纳新的数据。变更之风遇到的，永远是最大的阻力。不然的话，在巴比伦的第一场行动，早把新奥尔良、谢菲尔德、斯图加特，还有莫德·戴维斯的出生地木卫三，统统抹掉了！

===Original===
“Note how the gap left by Rome’s collapse was filled by the imperialistic and Christianized Germans. Only an expert Demon historian can tell the difference in most ages between the former Latin and the present Gothic Catholic Church. As you yourself, sir, said of Greece, it is as if an old melody were shifted into a slightly different key. In the wake of a Big Change, cultures and individuals are transposed, it’s true, yet in the main they continue much as they were, except for the usual scattering of unfortunate but statistically meaningless accidents.”

“All right, you bloody savants—maybe I pushed my point too far,” Bruce growled. “But if you want variety, give a thought to the rotten methods we use in our wonderful Change War. Poisoning Churchill and Cleopatra. Kidnapping Einstein when he’s a baby.”

“The Snakes did it first,” I reminded him.

“Yes, and we copied them. How resourceful does that make us?” he retorted, arguing like a woman. “If we need Einstein, why don’t we Resurrect him, deal with him as a man?”

===Chinese===
「且看罗马崩塌留下的缺口，是怎样由帝国主义、又基督教化了的日耳曼人填补的。在多数时代，只有专家级的魔鬼史学家，才分得清从前那个拉丁教会和如今这个哥特公教会的差别。先生，正像您自己谈希腊时说的，这好比一支老曲子被移进了一个稍有不同的调。大变更过后，文化和个人确实被移了位，可大体上照旧运转，只除了照例散落几桩不幸然而在统计上毫无意义的事故。」

「行，你们这帮该死的学问家——也许我把话推得太远了，」布鲁斯咆哮。「可你们要听新鲜的，就想想咱们在这场美妙绝伦的变更战争里使的烂手段吧。给丘吉尔和克利奥帕特拉下毒。在爱因斯坦还是奶娃娃的时候绑架他。」

「蛇方先动的手，」我提醒他。

「是啊，咱们照抄了他们。这显得咱们多有出息？」他反唇相讥，那架势活像个女人。「咱们要是需要爱因斯坦，为什么不复活他，把他当个人来打交道？」

---

===Original===
Beau said, serving his culture in slightly thicker slices, “*Pardonnez-moi*, but when you have enjoyed your status as Doubleganger a soupçon longer, you will understand that great men can rarely be Resurrected. Their beings are too crystallized, sir, their lifelines too tough.”

“Pardon me, but I think that’s rot. I believe that most great men refuse to make the bargain with the Snakes, or with us Spiders either. They scorn Resurrection at the price demanded.”

“Brother, they ain’t that great,” I whispered, while Beau glided on with, “However that may be, you have accepted Resurrection, sir, and so incurred an obligation which you as a gentleman must honor.”

===Chinese===
博开了腔，端上来的文化切片切得稍稍厚了些：「*Pardonnez-moi*（恕我直言），等您把二重身的身份多消受那么一星半点，您就会明白：伟人极少能被复活。他们的存在结晶得太厉害，先生，他们的生命线太韧。」

「恕我直言，我认为这是屁话。我相信大多数伟人是不肯跟蛇方做那笔交易的，跟咱们蜘蛛方也不肯。要那个价，他们宁死不食嗟来之食，耻于复活。」

「老兄，他们没那么伟大，」我悄声道，博则从容续道：「无论真相如何，先生，您已经接受了复活，由此背上了一桩义务，以您的绅士身份，非践履不可。」

===Original===
“I accepted Resurrection all right,” Bruce said, a glare coming into his eyes. “When they pulled me out of my line at Passchendaele in ’17 ten minutes before I died, I grabbed at the offer of life like a drunkard grabs at a drink the morning after. But even then I thought I was also seizing a chance to undo historic wrongs, work for peace.” His voice was getting wilder all the time. Just beyond our circle, I noticed the New Girl watching him worshipfully. “But what did I find the Spiders wanted me for? Only to fight more wars, over and over again, make them crueler and stinkinger, cut the swath of death a little wider with each Big Change, work our way a little closer to the death of the cosmos.”

===Chinese===
「复活我是接受了，没错，」布鲁斯说，眼里腾起一股凶光。「当他们在『’17 年』的帕斯亨达勒、在我死前十分钟把我从生命线上拽走时，我扑向那条活路，就像宿醉的酒鬼扑向清早那一杯。可即便在那时，我也以为自己顺手抓住了一个纠正历史冤屈、为和平出力的机会。」他的嗓音越来越狂乱。就在我们这个圈子外一步，我注意到新姑娘正满怀崇敬地望着他。「可我发现蜘蛛们要我来干什么？不过是打更多的仗，一遍又一遍，把它们打得更残、更臭，随每次大变更把死亡的刈幅再割宽一点，一步步凑近宇宙的死亡。」

===Original===
Sid touched my wrist and, as Bruce raved on, he whispered to me, “What kind of ball, think you, will please and so quench this fire-brained rogue? And you love me, discover it.”

I whispered back without taking my eyes off Bruce either, “I know somebody who’ll be happy to put on any kind of ball he wants, if he’ll just notice her.”

“The New Girl, sweetling? ’Tis well. This rogue speaks like an angry angel. It touches my heart and I like it not.”

===Chinese===
西德碰了碰我的手腕，趁布鲁斯滔滔控诉，悄声问我：「依你看，什么样的一场舞会，能哄住、浇熄这个脑子冒火的小子？你要还爱着我，就去打听出来。」

我也目不转睛地盯着布鲁斯，悄声回他：「我认得一个人，只要他肯正眼看她一眼，他想要什么样的舞会，她都乐意张罗。」

「新姑娘吗，小甜心？那就好。这小子说话像个发怒的天使。它碰着我的心了，我不喜欢这感觉。」

===Original===
Bruce was saying hoarsely but loudly, “And so we’re sent on operations in the past and from each of those operations the Change Winds blow futurewards, swiftly or slowly according to the opposition they breast, sometimes rippling into each other, and any one of those Winds may shift the date of our own death ahead of the date of our Resurrection, so that in an instant—even here, outside the cosmos—we may molder and rot or crumble to dust and vanish away. The wind with our name in it may leak through the Door.”

===Chinese===
布鲁斯正嘶哑而高声地说：「于是我们被派去过去执行行动，而每一场行动都会朝未来吹起变更之风，快慢全看它们顶头遭遇的阻力，有时彼此荡漾交叠，而其中任何一阵风，都可能把我们自己的死期挪到复活之日前头，于是一眨眼——哪怕在这儿，宇宙之外——我们就会霉烂腐朽，或者碎成尘土，消散无踪。带着我们名字的那阵风，是可能从门缝里漏进来的。」

---

===Original===
Faces hardened at that, because it’s bad form to mention Change Death, and Erich flared out with, “*Halts Maul, Kamerad!* There’s always another Resurrection.”

But Bruce didn’t keep his mouth shut. He said, “Is there? I know the Spiders promise it, but even if they do go back and cut another Doubleganger from my lifeline, is he me?” He slapped his chest with his bare hand. “I don’t think so. And even if he is me, with unbroken consciousness, why’s he been Resurrected again? Just to refight more wars and face more Change Death for the sake of an almighty power—” his voice was rising to a climax—“an almighty power so bloody ineffectual, it can’t furnish one poor Soldier pulled out of the mud of Passchendaele, one miserable Change Commando, one Godforsaken Recuperee a proper issue of equipment!”

===Chinese===
话音落地，一张张脸都硬了，因为提「变更之死」（Change Death）是失礼的，埃里希勃然发作：「*Halts Maul, Kamerad!*（闭上嘴，伙计！）总还有下一次复活。」

可布鲁斯没有闭嘴。他说：「有吗？我知道蜘蛛们许过这个诺，可就算他们真能折回去，从我的生命线上再割下一个二重身，他还是我吗？」他用光着的手拍打胸口。「我不认为是。就算他还是我，意识也没断过，那干嘛又复活他一回？就为了替一个全能的强权再打更多仗、再迎更多变更之死吗——」他的嗓门冲向顶点——「一个全能到这么不中用的强权，连一个从帕斯亨达勒泥沼里拽出来的可怜士兵、一个可怜巴巴的变更突击队员、一个天不管地不顾的疗养客，都没法给配上一套像样的装备！」

===Original===
And he held out his bare right hand toward us, fingers spread a little, as if it were the most amazing object and most deserving of outraged sympathy in the whole world.

The New Girl’s timing was perfect. She whisked through us, and before he could so much as wiggle the fingers, she whipped a black gauntleted glove on it and anyone could see that it fitted his hand perfectly.

This time our laughing beat the other. We collapsed and slopped our drinks and pounded each other on the back and then started all over.

===Chinese===
说着他朝我们伸出光着的右手，五指微微张开，仿佛那是全世界最叫人惊奇的物件、最值得为之义愤填膺地同情的东西。

新姑娘这一手时机拿捏得妙极。她一阵风穿过我们，趁他还来不及动一动指头，唰地给他套上一只黑长手套，谁都看得出来，那只手套跟他的手严丝合缝。

这回我们的笑闹压过了上一回。我们笑瘫了，泼了酒，彼此捶背，缓过来又从头笑起。

===Original===
“*Ach, der Handschuh, Liebchen!* Where’d she get it?” Erich gasped in my ear.

“Probably just turned the other one inside out—that turns a left into a right—I’ve done it myself,” I wheezed, collapsing again at the idea.

“That would put the lining outside,” he objected.

“Then I don’t know,” I said. “We got all sorts of junk in Stores.”

“It doesn’t matter, *Liebchen*,” he assured me. “*Ach, der Handschuh!*”

All through it, Bruce just stood there admiring the glove, moving the fingers a little now and then, and the New Girl stood watching him as if he were eating a cake she’d baked.

===Chinese===
「*Ach, der Handschuh*（啊，这只手套），*Liebchen*！她打哪儿弄来的？」埃里希在我耳边喘着气问。

「八成是把另一只翻了个里外——左手就翻成了右手——我自己就干过，」我喘着说，一想到这主意又笑瘫了。

「那衬里不就露在外头了。」他提出异议。

「那我就不知道了，」我说。「咱们库房区里各色破烂货都有的是。」

「不要紧，*Liebchen*，」他宽我的心。「*Ach, der Handschuh*（啊，这只手套）！」

这一路笑闹里，布鲁斯只顾站在那儿欣赏那只手套，时不时动一动手指，新姑娘站在一边望着他，仿佛他吃的是她亲手烤的蛋糕。

---

===Original===
When the hysteria quieted down, he looked up at her with a big smile. “What did you say your name was?”

“Lili,” she said, and believe you me, she was Lili to me even in my thoughts from then on, for the way she’d handled that lunatic.

===Chinese===
等这阵歇斯底里平息下去，他抬起头朝她绽开一个大大的微笑。「你说你叫什么来着？」

「莉莉。」她说，而且你信我，从那一刻起，连在我心里她都只是莉莉了，就看她收拾那个疯子的这一手。

===Original===
“Lilian Foster,” she explained. “I’m English also. Mr. Marchant, I’ve read *A Young Man’s Fancy* I don’t know how many times.”

“You have? It’s wretched stuff. From the Dark Ages—I mean my Cambridge days. In the trenches, I was working up some poems that were rather better.”

“I won’t hear you say that. But I’d be terribly thrilled to hear the new ones. Oh, Mr. Marchant, it was so strange to hear you call it Passiondale.”

“Why, if I may ask?”

“Because that’s the way I pronounce it to myself. But I looked it up and it’s more like Pas-ken-*da*-luh.”

“Bless you! All the Tommies called it Passiondale, just as they called Ypres Wipers.”

“How interesting. You know, Mr. Marchant, I’ll wager we were Recruited in the same operation, summer of 1917. I’d got to France as a Red Cross nurse, but they found out my age and were going to send me back.”

“How old were you—are you? Same thing, I mean to say.”

“Seventeen.”

“Seventeen in ’17,” Bruce murmured, his blue eyes glassy.

===Chinese===
「莉莲·福斯特（Lilian Foster），」她解释道。「我也是英国人。马钱特先生，您的《年轻人的遐想》（*A Young Man’s Fancy*），我读过不知多少遍。」

「是吗？那是蹩脚货。黑暗时代写的——我是说我在剑桥的日子。在战壕里，我正写着几首好些的。」

「不许您这么讲。不过听新的那些，我会激动坏的。哦，马钱特先生，听您管它叫『帕雄代尔』，真是奇怪极了。」

「为什么？方便问一句吗？」

「因为我心里也是这么念的。可我查过，它更接近『帕斯亨-*达*-勒』。」

「老天保佑你！大兵们全管它叫帕雄代尔，就像他们管伊普尔叫『怀珀斯』一样。」

「真有意思。您知道吗，马钱特先生，我敢打赌，咱们是同一场行动里被招募的，1917 年夏天。我当红十字护士到了法国，可他们查出了我的年纪，正要把我送回去呢。」

「你当时多大——多大了？我是说，反正一回事。」

「十七。」

「十七岁，在 ’17 年。」布鲁斯喃喃道，蓝眼睛蒙着一层玻璃。

===Original===
It was real corny dialogue and I couldn’t resent the humorous leer Erich gave me as we listened to them, as if to say, “Ain’t it nice, *Liebchen*, Bruce has a silly little English schoolgirl to occupy him between operations?”

Just the same, as I watched Lili in her dark bangs and pearl necklace and tight little gray dress that reached barely to her knees, and Bruce hulking over her tenderly in his snazzy hussar’s rig, I knew that I was seeing the start of something that hadn’t been part of me since Dave died fighting Franco years before I got on the Big Time, the sort of thing that almost made me wish there could be children in the Change World. I wondered why I’d never thought of trying to work things so that Dave got Resurrected and I told myself: no, it’s all changed, I’ve changed, better the Change Winds don’t disturb Dave or I know about it.

===Chinese===
这对话俗套得可以，可我也没法怪埃里希一边听一边朝我挤的那记诙谐的斜眼，仿佛在说：「多美啊，*Liebchen*，布鲁斯有了一个傻乎乎的英国小女生，好歹在行动间隙给他解闷？」

话虽如此，当我望着刘海乌黑、珍珠项链贴颈、紧身小灰裙勉强齐膝的莉莉，望着布鲁斯裹着那身漂亮骠骑兵行头、温存地俯就着她，我知道自己正目睹一样东西的开端——自打戴夫（Dave）死在同佛朗哥作战的战场上、而我还未踏上大时光的那些年前起，这东西就再没在我身上住过；正是那种东西，几乎让我巴望变更世界里能有孩子。我纳闷自己怎么从没想过设法把事情安排妥当，让戴夫得着复活，随即又劝自己：不，一切都变了，我也变了，还是让变更之风别去搅动戴夫为好，也别让我知道。

===Original===
“No, I didn’t die in 1917—I was merely Recruited then,” Lili was telling Bruce. “I lived all through the Twenties, as you can see from the way I dress. But let’s not talk about that, shall we? Oh, Mr. Marchant, do you think you can possibly remember any of those poems you started in the trenches? I can’t fancy them bettering your sonnet that concludes with, ‘The bough swings in the wind, the night is deep; Look at the stars, poor little ape, and sleep.’ ”

That one almost made me whoop—what monkeys we are, I thought—though I’d be the first to admit that the best line to use on a poet is one of his own—in fact, as many as possible. I decided I could safely forget our little Britons and devote myself to Erich or whatever needed me.

===Chinese===
「不，我不是 1917 年死的——只是那年被招募，」莉莉正对布鲁斯说。「整个二十年代我都活着，这从我的穿戴上就看得出来。可咱们别谈那个了，好不好？哦，马钱特先生，您觉得您还记得起来战壕里起头的那些诗吗？哪怕一首？我想象不出还能有哪首，赛得过您那首十四行，尤其是收尾那两句：『风里枝影摇，夜色已深沉；仰望群里吧，可怜的小猿，且入梦。』」

这一句差点让我叫出好来——咱们都是些什么猴儿啊，我心想——尽管我第一个承认：对付诗人，最好使的诗句就是他自己写的那句——实际上，越多越好。我断定，咱们这对小不列颠人尽可以放心撇下不管，我还是专心伺候埃里希，或者专心伺候任何用得着我的地方吧。

## III Nine for a Party / 九人成派对

===Original===
> Hell is the place for me. For to Hell go the fine churchmen, and the fine knights, killed in the tourney or in some grand war, the brave soldiers and the gallant gentlemen. With them will I go. There go also the fair gracious ladies who have lovers two or three beside their lord. There go the gold and the silver, the sables and ermine. There go the harpers and the minstrels and the kings of the earth.

===Chinese===
> 地狱才是我的去处。因为下地狱的，有好教士，有好骑士——比武场上死的，大战里死的——有勇武的士兵、豪侠的君子。我跟他们同去。那儿还去那些美丽高贵的夫人，除了自家夫君，还养着两三个情郎。那儿去金子，去银子，去黑貂和银貂。那儿去竖琴师、吟游诗人，去地上的君王。（译注：化用中古法国传奇《奥卡森与妮科莱特》。）

---

===Original===
I exchanged my drink for a new one from another tray Beau was bringing around. The gray of the Void was beginning to look real pleasant, like warm thick mist with millions of tiny diamonds floating in it. Doc was sitting grandly at the bar with a steaming tumbler of tea—a chaser, I guess, since he was just putting down a shot glass. Sid was talking to Erich and laughing at the same time and I said to myself it begins to feel like a party, but something’s lacking.

===Chinese===
我从博端来的另一只托盘上换了个新杯子。虚空的灰开始显得相当宜人，像一团温厚的雾，浮着亿万粒小钻屑。大夫气派十足地坐在吧台边，捧着一杯冒热气的浓茶——我猜是拿来追酒的，因为他刚放下一只烈酒盅。西德一边跟埃里希说话一边大笑，我对自己说：有点派对的架势了，可还缺个什么。

===Original===
It wasn’t anything to do with the Major Maintainer; its telltale was glowing a steady red like a nice little home fire amid the tight cluster of dials that included all the controls except the lonely and frightening Introversion switch that was never touched. Then Maud’s couch curtains winked out and there were she and the Roman sitting quietly side by side.

===Chinese===
缺的可不是主维持器；它的指示灯稳稳亮着红，像一小炉旺家的炉火，映在密匝匝一圈旋钮中间——那圈旋钮囊括了全部操控件，只除了那个孤零零、叫人心里发毛、从来无人触碰的内闭（Introversion）拨杆。这时，莫德那张躺椅的围幔熄了，她和那罗马人已然并肩安安静静坐着。

===Original===
He looked down at his shiny boots and the rest of his black duds like he was just waking up and couldn’t believe it all, and he said, “*Omnia mutantur, nos et mutamur in illis*,” and I raised my eyebrows at Beau, who was taking the tray back, and he did proud by old Vicksburg by translating: “All things change and we change with them.”

Then Mark slowly looked around at us, and I can testify that a Roman smile is just as warm as any other nationality, and he finally said, “We are nine, the proper number for a party. The couches, too. It is good.”

===Chinese===
他低头看看自己锃亮的马靴和其余的黑行头，像刚睡醒还不信这一切似的，然后说道：「*Omnia mutantur, nos et mutamur in illis*。」我朝正把托盘送回去的博扬了扬眉毛，他没给老维克斯堡丢脸，翻译道：「万物皆变，我们亦随之而变。」

随后马克缓缓环视我们，我可以作证：罗马人的微笑跟任何别国的一样暖。他终于开口：「我们九个，正是办派对的合适数目。躺椅也正好。这很好。」

===Original===
Maud chuckled proudly and Erich shouted, “Welcome back from the Void, *Kamerad*,” and then, because he’s German and thinks all parties have to be noisy and satirically pompous, he jumped on a couch and announced, “*Herren und Damen*, permit me to introduce the noblest Roman of them all, Marcus Vipsaius Niger, legate to Nero Claudius (called Germanicus in a former time stream) and who in 763 AUC (Correct, Mark? It means 10 AD, you meatheads!) died bravely fighting the Parthians and the Snakes in the Battle of Alexandria. *Hoch, hoch, hoch!*”

===Chinese===
莫德自豪地轻笑，埃里希高喊：「欢迎从虚空归来，*Kamerad*（伙计），」随即，因为他是德国人，认定一切派对都必须吵闹而煞有介事，他跳上一张躺椅，宣布道：「*Herren und Damen*（先生们女士们），请允许我介绍罗马人中最最高贵的一位：马库斯·维普萨伊乌斯·尼日尔（Marcus Vipsaius Niger），尼禄·克劳狄乌斯座下的使者（在前一条时间流里人称日耳曼尼库斯），于罗马纪年 763 年（说对了没，马克？换成公元就是 10 年，你们这帮榆木脑袋！）在亚历山大港战役中勇战帕提亚人与蛇方，壮烈殉国。*Hoch, hoch, hoch!*（万岁，万岁，万岁！）」

---

===Original===
We all swung our glasses and cheered with him and Sid yelled at Erich, “Keep your feet off the furniture, you unschooled rogue,” and grinned and boomed at all three hussars, “Take your ease, Recuperees,” and Maud and Mark got their drinks, the Roman paining Beau by refusing Falernian wine in favor of scotch and soda, and right away everyone was talking a mile a minute.

===Chinese===
我们全都挥着杯子跟他欢呼，西德朝埃里希嚷：「把你的脚从家具上拿下来，你这没教养的泼皮，」随即咧嘴一笑，冲三名骠骑兵朗声道：「各位宽心随意吧，疗养客们。」莫德和马克拿到了各自的酒——罗马人不要法勒年陈酒，偏要威士忌苏打，叫博好一阵心疼——转眼间人人都在滔滔不绝，一分钟跑一里地。

===Original===
We had a lot to catch up on. There was the usual yak about the war—“The Snakes are laying mine fields in the Void,” “I don’t believe it, how can you mine nothing?”—and the shortages—bourbon, bobby pins, and the stabilitin that would have brought Mark out of it faster—and what had become of people—“Marcia? Oh, she’s not around any more,” (She’d been caught in a Change Gale and green and stinking in five seconds, but I wasn’t going to say that)—and Mark had to be told about Bruce’s glove, which convulsed us all over again, and the Roman remembered a legionary who had carried a gripe all the way to Octavius because he’d accidentally been issued the unbelievable luxury item sugar instead of the usual salt, and Erich asked Sid if he had any new Ghostgirls in stock and Sid sucked his beard like the old goat he is. “Dost thou ask me, lusty Allemand? Nay, there are several great beauties, amongst them an Austrian countess from Strauss’s Vienna, and if it were not for sweetling here … Mnnnn.”

===Chinese===
要补的闲篇儿有的是。照例是谈战争——「蛇方正在虚空里布雷。」「我才不信，『没有』这种东西怎么布雷？」——谈短缺——波本酒、发卡，还有本可以让马克醒得更利索些的稳定素（stabilitin）——谈各人的下落——「玛西娅？噢，她不在啦。」（她是撞上了一阵变更飓风（Change Gale），五秒钟就发了绿、发了臭，可这话我可不打算说出口）——还得把布鲁斯那只手套讲给马克听，这又把我们全体笑翻了一回；罗马人则想起一个军团兵，因为被错发了一件难以置信的奢侈品——糖，而不是照例的盐——一路把牢骚告到了屋大维御前；埃里希问西德库房里可进了新的幽灵少女，西德像他这头老山羊惯常的那样嘬着胡子。「你问我吗，生龙活虎的日耳曼郎？不瞒你说，这儿有几位绝色，其中一位是施特劳斯时代维也纳来的奥地利伯爵夫人，要不是有咱们小甜心在这儿……唔嗯嗯。」

===Original===
I poked a finger in Erich’s chest between two of the bright buttons with their tiny death’s heads. “You, my little von Hohenwald, are a menace to us real girls. You have too much of a thing about the unawakened, ghost kind.”

He called me his little Demon and hugged me a bit too hard to prove it wasn’t so, and then he suggested we show Bruce the Art Gallery. I thought this was a real brilliant idea, but when I tried to argue him out of it, he got stubborn. Bruce and Lili were willing to do anything anyone wanted them to, though not so willing to pay any attention while doing it. The saber cut was just a thin red line on his cheek; she’d washed away all the dried blood.

===Chinese===
我用一根手指戳进埃里希胸前两粒亮锃锃的小骷髅头纽扣之间。「你呀，我的小冯·霍亨瓦尔德，是咱们这些真姑娘的一大祸害。你对那种未醒的、幽灵式的货色，癖好未免太重。」

他管我叫他的小魔鬼，还把我搂得略过了点劲儿，以证此言不实，接着他提议带布鲁斯去逛逛艺术品廊。我觉得这真是个绝妙的主意，可等我后来想劝他别去时，他又犟上了。布鲁斯和莉莉倒是愿意别人叫他们干什么就干什么，只是干的时候不太愿意分心。军刀的口子在他脸颊上只剩一道细红线；干涸的血痂她已经全擦洗净了。

===Original===
The Gallery gets you, though. It’s a bunch of paintings and sculptures and especially odd knickknacks, all made by Soldiers recuperating here, and a lot of them telling about the Change War from the stuff they’re made of—brass cartridges, flaked flint, bits of ancient pottery glued into futuristic shapes, mashed-up Incan gold rebeaten by a Martian, whorls of beady Lunan wire, a picture in tempera on a crinkle-cracked thick round of quartz that had filled a starship porthole, a Sumerian inscription chiseled into a brick from an atomic oven.

===Chinese===
不过艺术品廊就是勾人。里面是一堆画、雕塑，尤其是些古里古怪的小玩意儿，全是在这儿疗养的士兵亲手做的，其中不少单凭用料就在讲变更战争的故事——黄铜弹壳、敲落的燧石片、粘成未来主义造型的碎古陶、被一个火星人重新捶打的捣碎的印加金子、月球人珠状细线绕出的涡纹、一幅蛋彩画画在布满细裂纹的厚石英圆片上——那圆片曾嵌在某艘星舰的舷窗里——还有一句苏美尔铭文，凿在一块出自原子炉的砖上。

---

===Original===
There are a lot of things in the Gallery and I can always find some I haven’t ever seen before. It gets you, as I say, thinking about the guys that made them and their thoughts and the far times and places they came from, and sometimes, when I’m feeling low, I’ll come and look at them so I’ll feel still lower and get inspired to kick myself back into a good temper. It’s the only history of the Place there is and it doesn’t change a great deal, because the things in it and the feelings that went into them resist the Change Winds better than anything else.

===Chinese===
廊里东西多得很，我回回都能找出几件从没见过的。它就是勾人，我说过，勾着你去想造它们的那些家伙、他们的心思、他们来自的遥远时代和地方；有时候我情绪低落，就来看看它们，好让自己更低落，低到奋起一脚把自己踹回好心情里去。这是场所仅有的一部历史，而且变动不大，因为里面的东西，还有倾注进那些东西里的感情，抵抗变更之风的本事赛过一切。

===Original===
Right now, Erich’s witty lecture was bouncing off the big ears I hide under my pageboy bob and I was thinking how awful it is that for us that there’s not only change but Change. You don’t know from one minute to the next whether a mood or idea you’ve got is really new or just welling up into you because the past has been altered by the Spiders or Snakes.

Change Winds can blow not only death but anything short of it, down to the featheriest fancy. They blow thousands of times faster than time moves, but no one can say how much faster or how far one of them will travel or what damage it’ll do or how soon it’ll damp out. The Big Time isn’t the little time.

===Chinese===
这会儿，埃里希那套妙趣横生的讲解，全从我藏在波波头底下的大耳朵上弹开了，我正想着：对我们来说，糟糕就糟糕在，世上不光有寻常的变化，还有「变更」。你从这一分钟到下一分钟，都闹不清自己的一阵心绪、一个念头，到底是真新鲜，还是仅仅因为过去被蜘蛛或蛇方改过，才汩汩涌进你脑子里。

变更之风刮来的不只是死亡，还有死亡以内的任何东西，直到最轻若无物的一丝幻想。它们刮得比时间的脚步快上千倍万倍，可谁也说不出到底快多少，也说不出其中一阵会刮多远、会闯下什么祸、多久才会平息。大时光可不是寻常时光。

===Original===
And then, for the Demons, there’s the fear that our personality will just fade and someone else climb into the driver’s seat and us not even know. Of course, we Demons are supposed to be able to remember through Change and in spite of it; that’s why we are Demons and not Ghosts like the other Doublegangers, or merely Zombies or Unborn and nothing more, and as Beau truly said, there aren’t any great men among us—and blamed few of the masses, either—we’re a rare sort of people and that’s why the Spiders have to Recruit us where they find us without caring about our previous knowledge and background, a Foreign Legion of time, a strange kind of folk, bright but always in the background, with built-in nostalgia and cynicism, as adaptable as Centaurian shape-changers but with memories as long as a Lunan’s six arms, a kind of Change People, you might say, the cream of the damned.

But sometimes I wonder if our memories are as good as we think they are and if the whole past wasn’t once entirely different from anything we remember, and we’ve forgotten that we forgot.

As I say, the Gallery gets you feeling real low, and so now I said to myself, “Back to your lousy little commandant, kid,” and gave myself a stiff boot.

===Chinese===
再说了，对魔鬼们而言，还有一层恐惧：怕我们的个性就这么淡掉，另一个什么爬上驾驶座，而我们自己还蒙在鼓里。当然，按说法，我们魔鬼能够记得穿变更、逆变更而记；这正是我们是魔鬼、而不是其他二重身那样的幽灵、更不是单单的僵尸或未生者（Unborn）的缘故。而且博说得不错，我们中间没有伟人——芸芸众生里也少得可怜——我们是稀有的一类人，所以蜘蛛们才不得不在哪儿碰上就在哪儿招募我们，全不计较我们先前的知识和出身：一支时间里的外籍军团，一族怪人，聪明，却永远站在幕后，天生自带乡愁和犬儒，适应起来跟半人马星的变身族一样随遇而安，记性却跟月球人的六条胳膊一样长——你可以说，是一种「变更之民」，该死之辈里的精华。

可有的时候我会犯嘀咕：我们的记性，是不是真有我们自以为的那么好；整个过去，会不会曾经同我们记得的一切截然不同，而我们把「忘了」这件事也给忘了。

我说过，艺术品廊就是勾得你情绪低落，所以这会儿我对自己说：「回到你那讨嫌的小指挥官身边去吧，丫头，」狠狠给了自己一脚。

===Original===
Erich was holding up a green bowl with gold dolphins or spaceships on it and saying, “And, to my mind, this proves that Etruscan art is derived from Egyptian. Don’t you agree, Bruce?”

Bruce looked up, all smiles from Lili, and said, “What was that, dear chap?”

===Chinese===
埃里希正举着一只绿碗，碗上描着金海豚，要不就是宇宙飞船，一边说：「而且依我看，这件东西证明伊特鲁里亚艺术源出埃及。你不同意吗，布鲁斯？」

布鲁斯抬起头，那一脸笑是冲莉莉的，答道：「你说什么来着，亲爱的伙计？」

---

===Original===
Erich’s forehead got dark as the Door and I was glad the hussars had parked their sabers along with their shakos, but before he could even get out a Jerry cussword, Doc breezed up in that plateau-state of drunkenness so like hypnotized sobriety, moving as if he were on a dolly, ghosted the bowl out of Erich’s hand, said, “A beautiful specimen of Middle Systemic Venusian. When Eightaitch finished it, he told me you couldn’t look at it and not feel the waves of the Northern Venusian Shallows rippling around your hoofs. But it might look better inverted. I wonder. Who are you, young officer? *Nichevo*,” and he carefully put the bowl back on its shelf and rolled on.

===Chinese===
埃里希的额头黑得跟门似的，幸好骠骑兵们早把军刀连同筒帽一起卸下了，可他连一句德国粗口都还没骂出口，大夫就飘了过来，带着他那高原期的醉态——活像被催眠了的清醒——整个人像搁在滑轨车上来似的平移过来，幽灵一样把碗从埃里希手里抽走，说道：「中系统纪金星期的一件漂亮标本。八艾奇（Eightaitch）做完它的时候告诉我，你只要看上一眼，就不可能感觉不到金星北浅海的波浪在你蹄子周围荡漾。不过倒过来看没准更好。我在想。你是谁，年轻军官？*Nichevo*（没什么）。」说着他把碗仔仔细细放回架上，滚滚而去。

===Original===
It’s a fact that Doc knows the Art Gallery better than any of us, really by heart, he being the oldest inhabitant, though he maybe picked a bad time to show off his knowledge. Erich was going to take out after him, but I said, “Nix, *Kamerad*, remember gloves and sugar,” and he contented himself with complaining, “That *nichevo*—it’s so gloomy and hopeless, *ungeheuerlich*. I tell you, *Liebchen*, they shouldn’t have Russians working for the Spiders, not even as Entertainers.”

I grinned at him and squeezed his hand. “Not much entertainment in Doc these days, is there?” I agreed.

===Chinese===
大夫比我们谁都熟艺术品廊，这是实情，简直烂熟于心，他毕竟是这里资格最老的住户，虽说这会儿炫耀学问，时机怕是挑坏了。埃里希正要追上去理论，我说：「算了，*Kamerad*，想想手套和糖的事儿。」他只好退而抱怨：「那句*Nichevo*——太阴郁，太绝望了，*ungeheuerlich*（不像话）。我告诉你，*Liebchen*，蜘蛛们就不该用俄国人为他们做事，哪怕当招待员也不行。」

我朝他咧嘴一笑，捏了捏他的手。「这些日子大夫身上也没多少娱乐性了，对吧？」我附和道。

===Original===
He grinned back at me a shade sheepishly and his face smoothed and his blue eyes looked sweet again for a second and he said, “I shouldn’t want to claw out at people that way, Greta, but at times I am just a jealous old man,” which is not entirely true, as he isn’t a day over thirty-three, although his hair is nearly white.

Our lovers had drifted on a few steps until they were almost fading into the Surgery screen. It was the last spot I would have picked for the formal preliminaries to a little British smooching, but Lili probably didn’t share my prejudices, though I remembered she’d told me she’d served a brief hitch in an Arachnoid Field Hospital before being transferred to the Place.

===Chinese===
他也朝我咧嘴笑了笑，有几分赧然，脸庞舒展开来，蓝眼睛有一秒钟重新显得温柔，他说：「我不该那样张牙舞爪地冲人去，格蕾塔，可有的时候，我就是个爱吃醋的老头子。」这话不全对，他还不到三十三岁呢，虽说头发已经近乎全白。

咱们那对恋人又漂出去几步，几乎要化进手术区的屏风里了。要给一场英式小亲小吻挑个举行正式前奏的地点，我最不会挑的就是那儿，不过莉莉八成没有我这些偏见，虽然我记得她说过，调来场所之前，她在一座蛛族野战医院短期服役过。

===Original===
But she couldn’t have had anything like the experience I’d had during my short and sour career as a Spider nurse, when I’d acquired my best-hated nightmare and flopped completely (jobwise, but on the floor, too) at seeing a doctor flick a switch and a being, badly injured but human, turn into a long cluster of glistening strange fruit—ugh, it always makes me want to toss my cookies and my buttons. And to think that dear old Daddy Anton wanted his Greta chile to be a doctor.

===Chinese===
可她绝不可能有我那种经历——我在蜘蛛方当护士的那段又短又苦的职业生涯里，挣来了我最恨的那场噩梦：眼看着一位医生啪嗒一拨开关，一个重伤但分明是人的生命，变成一长串亮晶晶的怪果子——呃，一想起来我就想把隔夜的饼干连同纽扣一起吐出去。想想吧，亲爱的老安东爹爹，当年还想让他家格蕾塔闺女去当医生呢。

---

===Original===
Well, I could see this wasn’t getting me anywhere I wanted to go, and after all there was a party going on.

Doc was babbling something at a great rate to Sid—I just hoped Doc wouldn’t get inspired to go into his animal imitations, which sound pretty fierce and once seriously offended some recuperating E.T.s.

Maud was demonstrating to Mark a 23rd Century two-step and Beau sat down at the piano and improvised softly on her rhythm.

===Chinese===
得了，我看这条路通不了我想去的地方，再说派对不是正开着嘛。

大夫正对着西德滔滔不绝地咕哝什么——我只盼他别一时兴起表演起动物模仿来，那玩意儿听上去相当凶，还曾严重开罪过几位前来疗养的 E.T.。

莫德正给马克示范 23 世纪的两步舞，博在钢琴前坐下，就着她的节奏轻轻即兴。

===Original===
As the deep-thrumming relaxing notes hit us, Erich’s face brightened and he dragged me over. Pleasantly soon I had my feet off the diamond-rough floor, which we don’t carpet because most of the E.T.s, the dear boys, like it hard, and I was shouldering back deep into the couch nearest the piano, with cushions all around me and a fresh drink in my hand, while my Nazi boy friend was getting ready to discharge his *Weltschmerz* as song, which didn’t alarm me too much, as his baritone is passable.

Things felt real good, like the Maintainer was just idling to keep the Place in existence and moored to the cosmos, not exerting itself at all or at most taking an occasional lazy paddle stroke. At times the Place’s loneliness can be happy and comfortable.

===Chinese===
深沉嗡鸣的松弛音符拂到我们身上，埃里希的脸亮了起来，把我拽了过去。不多会儿，惬意地，我的双脚已经离开了钻石般粗粝的地面——那地面我们不铺地毯，因为大多数 E.T.，那些好孩子，就好这口硬的——我正深深陷进离钢琴最近的那张躺椅，四下垫满靠垫，手里一杯新酒，而我那位纳粹男朋友正准备把他的*Weltschmerz*（世界苦闷）当歌唱出来，这倒没太吓着我，他的男中音还过得去。

一切都感觉好极了，仿佛维持器只是空转着，维系场所的存在、维系它与宇宙的系泊，一点不费力气，顶多偶尔懒懒划一桨。有的时候，场所的孤寂也可以是快乐而熨帖的。

===Original===
Then Beau raised an eyebrow at Erich, who nodded, and next thing they were launched into a song we all know, though I’ve never found out where it originally came from. This time it made me think of Lili, and I wondered why—and why it’s a tradition at Recuperation Stations to call the new girl Lili, though in this case it happened to be her real name.

> Standing in the Doorway just outside of space,
 

 Winds of Change blow ’round you but don’t touch your face;
 

 You smile as you whisper tenderly,
 

 “Please cross to me, Recuperee;
 

 The operation’s over, come in and close the Door.”

===Chinese===
然后博朝埃里希扬了扬眉毛，埃里希点头，紧接着两人就放声唱起一首我们都会的歌，尽管我始终没打听出它最早打哪儿来。这一次它让我想起了莉莉，我纳闷为什么——也纳闷为什么疗养站有个传统，管新来的姑娘一律叫莉莉，虽说这一回，碰巧就是她的真名。

> 立在门畔，空间之外那一侧，
> 变更之风绕着你打转，却碰不着你的脸；
> 你微笑着，温柔地低语：
> 「请渡过来吧，疗养客；
> 行动已终，进来，把门关严。」

## IV SOS from Nowhere / 来自乌有之处的求救

===Original===
> De Bailhache, Fresca, Mrs. Cammel, whirled
 

 Beyond the circuit of the shuddering Bear
 

 In fractured atoms.

===Chinese===
> 德·贝拉什、弗蕾斯卡、卡梅尔太太，被卷出
> 颤抖的大熊星环之外，
> 裂成碎原子。（译注：出自 T. S. 艾略特《荒原》删改残稿。）

---

===Original===
I realized the piano had deserted Erich and I cranked my head up and saw Beau, Maud and Sid streaking for the control divan. The Major Maintainer was blinking emergency-green and fast, but the code was plain enough for even me to recognize the Spider distress call and for a second I felt just sick. Then Erich blew out his reserve breath in the middle of “Door” and I gave myself another of those helpful mental boots at the base of the spine and we hurried after them toward the center of the Place along with Mark.

===Chinese===
我发觉埃里希身边的钢琴声断了，一拧头，看见博、莫德和西德正朝控制躺椅狂奔。主维持器正急促地闪着紧急绿，可那码子简单得连我都认出来了：蜘蛛方求救信号。有那么一秒钟我只觉得反胃。随后埃里希把憋着的气一口吐在「门」字正中间，我照着尾椎骨又给自己来了一记提神的心理猛踹，我们便连同马克一起，匆匆朝场所中央追了过去。

===Original===
The blinks faded as we got there and Sid told us not to move because we were making shadows. He glued an eye to the telltale and we held still as statues as he caressed the dials like he was making love.

One sensitive hand flicked out past the Introversion switch over to the Minor Maintainer and right away the Place was dark as your soul and there was nothing for me but Erich’s arm and the knowledge that Sid was nursing a green light I couldn’t even see, although my eyes had plenty time to accommodate.

Then the green light finally came back very slowly and I could see the dear reliable old face—the green-gold beard making him look like a merman—and then the telltale flared bright and Sid flicked on the Place lights and I leaned back.

===Chinese===
我们赶到时，闪光淡了下去，西德叫我们别动，说我们在投影子。他把一只眼睛贴上指示灯，我们僵得像一排雕像，任他爱抚那些旋钮，像在行鱼水之欢。

一只灵巧的手掠过内闭拨杆，轻轻点到副维持器上，场所立刻黑得跟你的灵魂一样，留给我的只剩埃里希的胳膊，外加一个念想：西德正守着一线我根本看不见的绿光——虽说我的眼睛有的是时间适应黑暗。

那线绿光终于极缓极缓地回来了，我看见了那张可亲可靠的老脸——金绿相间的胡子衬得他像个人鱼——随即指示灯大亮，西德啪地打开场所的灯，我向后一靠。

===Original===
“That nails them, lads, whoever and whenever they may be. Get ready for a pickup.”

Beau, who was closest of course, looked at him sharply. Sid shrugged uneasily. “Meseemed at first it was from our own globe a thousand years before our Lord, but that indication flickered and faded like witchfire. As it is, the call comes from something smaller than the Place and certes adrift from the cosmos. Meseemed too at one point I knew the fist of the caller—an antipodean atomicist named Benson-Carter—but that likewise changed.”

===Chinese===
「锁定他们了，小伙子们，管他们是谁、管他们是什么年头的。准备接人。」

站得最近的博尖利地看了他一眼。西德不安地耸耸肩。「起先我觉着，信号来自我们这颗地球、我主降生前一千年的光景，可那迹象偏偏像鬼火，闪了闪就灭了。照现在的情形，呼叫出自一件比场所还小的东西，而且确乎脱开了宇宙、正四处漂流。有个当口我也觉着认出了呼叫者的指法——一位南半球来的原子学家，叫本森-卡特（Benson-Carter）——可这也变了。」

===Original===
Beau said, “We’re not in the right phase of the cosmos-Places rhythm for a pickup, are we, sir?”

Sid answered, “Ordinarily not, boy.”

Beau continued, “I didn’t think we had any pickups scheduled. Or standby orders.”

Sid said, “We haven’t.”

Mark’s eyes glowed. He tapped Erich on the shoulder. “An octavian denarius against ten Reichsmarks it is a Snake trap.”

Erich’s grin showed his teeth. “Make it first through the Door next operation and I’m on.”

===Chinese===
博说：「按宇宙同各场所的节奏相位，现在轮不到我们接人，对吧，先生？」

西德答：「照常理轮不到，孩子。」

博续道：「我还以为咱们没有安排任何接送。也没有待命令。」

西德说：「没有。」

马克目光灼灼。他拍了拍埃里希的肩。「一枚奥古斯都第纳里乌斯银币，对你们十枚帝国马克，赌这是个蛇方陷阱。」

埃里希龇牙一笑。「加一条：下次行动谁第一个冲进门——赌约成立。」

---

===Original===
It didn’t take that to tell me things were serious, or the thought that there’s always a first time for bumping into something from really outside the cosmos.
