The Snakes have broken our code more than once. Maud was quietly serving out weapons and Doc was helping her. Only Bruce and Lili stood off. But they were watching.

The telltale brightened. Sid reached toward the Maintainer, saying, “All right, my hearties. Remember, through this Doorway pass the fishiest finaglers in and out of the cosmos.”

===Chinese===
用不着这桩赌注来告诉我事态严重，也用不着去想「撞上真正来自宇宙之外的东西，总有头一回」这类念头。蛇方破译我们的密码已不止一次。莫德正不动声色地分发武器，大夫在帮她。只有布鲁斯和莉莉袖手站在一边。但他们都在看着。

指示灯亮了。西德伸手向维持器，口中说道：「好了，我的心肝们。记住，打这扇门进进出出的，全是宇宙内外最滑不留手的骗子和老千。」

===Original===
The Door appeared to the left and above where it should be and darkened much too fast. There was a gust of stale salt seawind, if that makes sense, but no stepped-up Change Winds I could tell—and I had been bracing myself against them. The Door got inky and there was a flicker of gray fur whips and a flash of copper flesh and gilt and something dark and a clump of hoofs and Erich was sighting a stun gun across his left forearm, and then the Door had vanished like that and a tentacled silvery Lunan and a Venusian satyr were coming straight toward us.

===Chinese===
门出现在偏左、偏高于它惯常的位置，暗下来得也快得不像话。涌进来一股发陈的咸腥海风——如果这说法讲得通的话——却觉不出骤然加强的变更之风——我原本一直绷着劲防它们呢。门变成墨色，有一瞬是灰皮毛鞭子的翻飞、铜色皮肉和金饰的闪光、一团黑影、一簇蹄子，埃里希已经把眩晕枪架在左小臂上瞄准，然后门就那么消失了，一个遍生触手的银色月球人和一个金星半人羊，正径直朝我们走来。

===Original===
The Lunan was hugging a pile of clothes and weapons. The satyr was helping a wasp-waisted woman carry a heavy-looking bronze chest. The woman was wearing a short skirt and high-collared bolero jacket of leather so dark brown it was almost black. She had a two-horned Petsofa hairdress and she was boldly gilded here and there and wore sandals and copper anklets and wristlets—one of them a copper-plated Caller—and from her wide copper belt hung a short-handled double-headed ax. She was dark-complexioned and her forehead and chin receded, but the effect was anything but weak; she had a face like a beautiful arrowhead—and a familiar one, by golly!

===Chinese===
月球人怀里抱着一大摞衣甲兵器。半人羊搀着一个蜂腰女子，抬着一只看上去沉甸甸的青铜箱。那女子穿着深棕近黑的皮短裙和立领短上衣，梳着双角的佩索法式发髻（译注：Petsofa，克里特古风发式），身上几处大胆地贴着金箔，脚蹬凉鞋，腕上踝上扣着铜环——其中一只腕环是镀铜的呼叫器——宽宽的铜腰带上挂着一柄短柄双头斧。她肤色深暗，额头和下巴后收，可这效果绝说不上孱弱；她那张脸像一枚美丽的箭镞——而且好家伙，还是一张熟脸！

===Original===
But before I could say, “Kabysia Labrys,” Maud shrilly beat me to it with, “It’s Kaby with two friends. Break out a couple of Ghostgirls.”

And then I saw it really was old-home week because I recognized my Lunan boy friend Ilhilihis, and in the midst of all the confusion I got a nice kick out of knowing I was getting so I could tell the personality of one silver-furred muzzle from another.

===Chinese===
不等我喊出「卡比西娅·拉布里斯（Kabysia Labrys）」，莫德已经尖着嗓子抢了先：「是卡比，还带了两位朋友。去库房领两位幽灵少女出来。」

跟着我就明白，这真是一场他乡遇故知大会，因为我认出了我的月球人男朋友伊尔希利希斯（Ilhilihis），而在这一片乱糟糟当中，我美滋滋地得意了一把：我已经练到能从一张银毛口鼻上读出个性，跟别的口鼻不搞混了。

===Original===
They reached the control divan and Illy dumped his load and the others let down the chest, and Kaby staggered but shook off the two E.T.s when they started to support her, and she looked daggers at Sid when he tried to do the same, although she’s his “sweet Keftian friend” he’d mentioned to Bruce.

===Chinese===
他们到了控制躺椅跟前，伊尔利撂下他那一摞，其他人放下青铜箱，卡比晃了晃，却在两个 E.T. 上来搀扶时甩开了他们；西德想故技重施，也被她怒目相向——虽说她正是他跟布鲁斯提过的那位「甜蜜的凯夫特朋友」。

---

===Original===
She leaned straight-armed on the divan and took two gasping breaths so deep that the ridges of her spine showed through her brown-skinned waist, and then she threw up her head and commanded, “Wine!”

While Beau was rushing it, Sid tried to take her hand again, saying, “Sweetling, I’d never heard you call before and knew not this pretty little fist,” but she ripped out, “Save your comfort for the Lunan,” and I looked and saw—Hey, Zeus!—that one of Ilhilihis’ six tentacles was lopped off halfway.

===Chinese===
她直着双臂撑在躺椅上，深吸了两口气，深到她棕色腰背上的一道道脊棱都显了出来，然后她昂起头，发令：「酒！」

博飞奔去取的当口，西德再次想去拉她的手，说：「小甜心，从前从未听你呼叫过，也不认得这只可爱的小手。」她却厉声道：「把你那份体贴省给月球人吧。」我一看——嘿，宙斯！（译注：Hey Zeus，谐音 Jesus「天哪」。）——伊尔希利希斯的六条触手，有一条齐中给削断了。

===Original===
That was for me, and, going to him, I fast briefed myself: “Remember, he only weighs fifty pounds for all he’s seven feet high; he doesn’t like low sounds or to be grabbed; the two legs aren’t tentacles and don’t act the same; uses them for long walks, tentacles for leaps; uses tentacles for close vision too and for manipulation, of course; extended, they mean he’s at ease; retracted, on guard or nervous; sharply retracted, disgusted; greeting—”

Just then, one of them swept across my face like a sweet-smelling feather duster and I said, “Illy, man, it’s been a lot of sleeps,” and brushed my fingers across his muzzle. It still took a little self-control not to hug him, and I did reach a little cluckingly for his lopped tentacle, but he wafted it away from me and the little voice-box belted to his side squeaked, “Naughty, naughty. Papa will fix his little old self. Greta girl, ever bandaged even a Terra octopus?”

===Chinese===
那是我的活儿，我朝他走过去，一边飞快给自己过了一遍须知：「记住，他虽然有七英尺高，体重才五十磅；他不喜欢低沉的声响，也不喜欢被人一把抓；那两条腿不是触手，路数不一样——走远路用腿，跳跃用触手；触手还管近距离视觉，当然也管操作；触手舒展，说明他安然自得；收拢，是戒备或紧张；猛地紧缩，是厌恶；打招呼么——」

正想着，一条触手已经像一把香喷喷的鸡毛掸子扫过我的脸，我说：「伊尔利，老伙计，好些个入眠不见了。」指尖拂过他的口鼻。不冲上去抱他，还是费了我一点自制力；我也确实咂着嘴朝那截断触手伸了伸手，他却把它轻轻荡开了，扣在他身侧的吱吱匣（squeakbox）说道：「淘气，淘气。老爹爹会把自己这小老样子修好的。格蕾塔姑娘，你给地球的章鱼包扎过没有？」

===Original===
I had, an intelligent one from around a quarter billion AD, but I didn’t tell him so. I stood and let him talk to the palm of my hand with one of his tentacles—I don’t savvy feather-talk but it feels good, though I’ve often wondered who taught him English—and watched him use a couple others to whisk a sort of Lunan band-aid out of his pouch and cap his wound with it.

Meanwhile, the satyr knelt over the bronze chest, which was decorated with little death’s heads and crosses with hoops at the top and swastikas, but looking much older than Nazi, and the satyr said to Sid, “Quick thinkin’, Gov, when ya saw the Door comin’ in high ’n’ soffened up grav’ty unner it, but cud I hav’ sum hep now?”

===Chinese===
包扎过，还是一只聪明章鱼，公元两亿五千万年前后的货色，可我没告诉他。我站着，让一条触手在我掌心里絮语——羽语我不懂，可感觉很好，虽说我一直纳闷是谁教会了他英语——一面看他用另外几条触手从囊袋里拂出一枚月球人创可贴似的东西，罩住了伤口。

与此同时，半人羊跪在青铜箱旁——箱面上装饰着小骷髅、顶上带环的十字和卐字，看着可比纳粹古老得多——他对西德说：「脑瓜子转得真快，长官，一瞅见门从高处进来，就把底下的重力放软乎了。可俺这会儿能讨点搭把手的么？」

===Original===
Sid touched the Minor Maintainer and we all got very light and my stomach did a flip-flop while the satyr piled on the chest the clothes and weapons that Illy had been carrying and pranced off with it all and carefully put it down at the end of the bar. I decided the satyr’s English instructor must have been quite a character, too. Wish I’d met him—her—it.

Sid thought to ask Illy if he wanted Moon-normal gravity in one sector, but my boy likes to mix, and being such a lightweight, Earth-normal gravity doesn’t bother him. As he said to me once, “Would Jovian gravity bother a beetle, Greta girl?”

===Chinese===
西德碰了碰副维持器，我们全体轻飘飘起来，我胃里翻了个个儿，半人羊把伊尔利先前抱着的衣甲兵器摞到箱子上，颠着蹄子驮着这一担走开，仔仔细细卸在了吧台尽头。我断定，这位半人羊的英文老师也准是个妙人。真想见见他——她——它。

西德想起问伊尔利，要不要单独给他一区月球标准重力，可我这位就爱混着来，再说体重那么轻，地球标准重力也难不倒他。用他跟我说过的话说：「木星重力会为难一只甲虫吗，格蕾塔姑娘？」

---

===Original===
I asked Illy about the satyr and he squeaked that his name was Sevensee and that he’d never met him before this operation. I knew the satyrs were from a billion years in the future, just as the Loonies were from a billion in the past, and I thought—Kreesed us!—but it must have been a real big or emergency-like operation to have the Spiders using those two for it, with two billion years between them—a time-difference that gives you a feeling of awe for a second, you know.

===Chinese===
我问伊尔利那半人羊的来历，他吱吱作答：他叫七观（Sevensee），这次行动之前两人从没打过照面。我知道半人羊族来自十亿年后的未来，正如月球佬来自十亿年前的过去，我心想——克里老天保佑！——能让蜘蛛方把这两位派进同一场行动，中间还隔着二十亿年——这准是场真正的大行动，或是火烧眉毛的急事——这种时间差，你懂的，会让人肃然起敬那么一秒钟。

===Original===
I started to ask Illy about it, but just then Beau came scampering back from the bar with a big red-and-black earthenware goblet of wine—we try to keep a variety of drinking tools in stock so folks will feel more at home. Kaby grabbed it from him and drained most of it in one swallow and then smashed it on the floor. She does things like that, though Sid’s tried to teach her better. Then she stared at what she was thinking about until the whites showed all around her eyes and her lips pulled way back from her teeth and she looked a lot less human than the two E.T.s, just like a fury. Only a time traveler knows how like the wild murals and engravings of them some of the ancients can look.

===Chinese===
我正要细问，博已经抱着一只红黑两色的大陶酒杯从吧台一路小跑回来——各类酒器我们尽量备得齐全，好叫大家宾至如归。卡比一把夺过，一口灌掉大半，随即把杯子掼碎在地板上。她就爱来这套，虽说西德一直想调教她学点规矩。然后她死死盯住自己心里想的东西，盯到眼白四露、嘴唇朝后绷离牙齿，那模样比两位 E.T. 少了不止一分人味，活脱一尊复仇女神。只有时间旅行者才知道，某些古人可以长得多像壁画和雕版上那些狂野的神祇。

===Original===
My hair stood up at the screech she let out. She smashed a fist into the divan and cried, “Goddess! Must I see Crete destroyed, revived, and now destroyed again? It is too much for your servant.”

Personally, I thought she could stand anything.

There was a rush of questions at what she said about Crete—I asked one of them, for the news certainly frightened me—but she shot up her arm straight for silence and took a deep breath and began.

“In the balance hung the battle. Rowing like black centipedes, the Dorian hulls bore down on our outnumbered ships. On the bright beach, masked by rocks, Sevensee and I stood by the needle gun, ready to give the black hulls silent wounds. Beside us was Ilhilihis, suited as a sea monster. But then … then …”

Then I saw she wasn’t altogether the iron babe, for her voice broke and she started to shake and to sob rackingly, although her face was still a mask of rage, and she threw up the wine. Sid stepped in and made her stop, which I think he’d been wanting to do all along.

===Chinese===
她迸出的那声尖叫叫我毛发倒竖。她一拳砸进躺椅，高呼：「女神啊！难道必须让我眼看着克里特毁灭，复兴，如今又再度毁灭？这对你卑微的仆人，太过分了。」

就我个人而言，我觉得她什么都扛得住。

她这一提克里特，问题劈头盖脸涌过去——我也问了一个，因为这消息着实吓着了我——可她手臂笔直竖起，喝令肃静，深吸一口气，开了腔。

「战局悬于一线。多利安人的船壳像一群黑蜈蚣，划桨压向我方寡不敌众的船队。明亮的海滩上，礁石掩着身形，七观和我守着针枪，预备给那些黑船壳留下无声的创伤。我们旁边是伊尔希利希斯，装扮成一头海怪。可就在那时……那时……」

这时我才看出，她并不全然是铁打的婆娘——她的嗓子哑了，浑身发抖，抽噎得五脏六腑都在翻，尽管脸上还罩着一层怒气的面具，酒也吐了出来。西德上前劝止了她——这一手，我想他从头到尾都憋着想使来着。

## V Sid Insists on Ghostgirls / 西德坚持要幽灵少女

===Original===
> Whenever I take up a newspaper and read it, I fancy I see ghosts creeping between the lines. There must be ghosts all over the world. They must be as countless as the grains of the sands, it seems to me.

===Chinese===
> 每当我拿起一份报纸来读，我总仿佛看见幽灵在字里行间爬动。全世界一定到处都是幽灵。依我看，它们必定多得数不清，像沙粒一样。（译注：易卜生《当我们死而复苏时》。）

---

===Original===
My Elizabethan boy friend put his fists on his hips and laid down the law to us as if we were a lot of nervous children who’d been playing too hard.

“Look you, masters, this is a Recuperation Station and I am running it as such. A plague of all operations! I care not if the frame of things disjoints and the whole Change World goes to ruin, but you, warrior maid, are going to rest and drink more wine slowly before you tell your tale and your colleagues are going to be properly companioned. No questions, anyone. Beau, and you love us, give us a lively tune.”

Kaby relaxed a little and let him put his hand carefully against her back in token of support and she said grudgingly, “All right, Fat Belly.”

Then, so help me, to the tune of the Muskrat Ramble, which I’d taught Beau, we got girls for those two E.T.s and everybody properly paired up.

===Chinese===
我那位伊丽莎白时代的男朋友叉起双拳抵在胯上，对我们发号施令，仿佛我们是一群玩过了头、神经紧张的娃娃。

「都听着，各位师傅，这里是疗养站，我就按疗养站的规矩来办。一切行动都见鬼去吧！哪怕乾坤的架子散了套、整个变更世界塌了台，我也不管；可是你，女战士，得先歇着，慢慢再喝点酒，然后才讲你的故事；你的两位同僚呢，也得有人好好作陪。谁都不许提问。博，你要还爱我们，就给咱们来支热闹的曲子。」

卡比松泛了一点，任他小心翼翼把手贴上她的背，以示扶持，她勉勉强强地说：「行吧，胖肚皮。」

接着——老天作证——伴着《麝鼠漫步》（Muskrat Ramble，我教给博的曲子）的调子，我们给那两位 E.T. 各配上了姑娘，人人得到了妥帖的配对。

===Original===
Right here I want to point out that a lot of the things they say in the Change World about Recuperation Stations simply aren’t so—and anyway they always leave out nine-tenths of it. The Soldiers that come through the Door are looking for a good time, sure, but they’re hurt real bad too, every one of them, deep down in their minds and hearts, if not always in their bodies or so you can see it right away.

Believe me, a temporal operation is no joke, and to start with, there isn’t one person in a hundred who can endure to be cut from his lifeline and become a really wide-awake Doubleganger—a Demon, that is—let alone a Soldier. What does a badly hurt and mixed-up creature need who’s been fighting hard? *One individual* to look out for him and feel for him and patch him up, and it helps if the one is of the opposite sex—that’s something that goes beyond species.

There’s your basis for the Place and the wild way it goes about its work, and also for most other Recuperation Stations or Entertainment Spots. The name Entertainer can be misleading, but I like it. She’s got to be a lot more than a good party girl—or boy—though she’s got to be that too. She’s got to be a nurse and a psychologist and an actress and a mother and a practical ethnologist and a lot of things with longer names—and a reliable friend.

===Chinese===
我正好要在这儿指出来：变更世界里流传的那些关于疗养站的闲话，多半根本不是那么回事——再说，他们讲起来总漏掉十之八九。打门里进来的士兵是想寻个痛快，这没错，可他们个个也伤得很重，伤在头脑和心灵的深处，哪怕不一定伤在身上、也不一定一眼看得出来。

相信我，时间行动可不是闹着玩的；头一条，一百个人里也挑不出一个，受得了从自己的生命线上被割下来、变成一个真正彻底清醒的二重身——也就是魔鬼——更别提当士兵了。一个刚恶战过、伤得厉害又晕头转向的生灵，需要什么？需要*单独一个人*来看顾他、体贴他、把他修补好；而这一个人若是异性，效果更佳——这是超出了物种界限的事。

这就是场所的根基，也是它那套疯疯癫癫办事路数的根基，还是大多数别的疗养站或娱乐站的根基。「招待员」这名目容易叫人误会，可我喜欢。她得远远不止是个好派的派对女郎——或男孩——虽说这一条她也得够格。她得同时是护士、心理学家、女演员、母亲、实务民俗学家，外加一长串名头更长的行当——还要是个靠得住的朋友。

---

===Original===
None of us are all those things perfectly or even near it. We just try. But when the call comes, Entertainers have to forget grudges and gripes and envies and jealousies—and remember, they’re lively people with sharp emotions—because there isn’t any time then for anything but *help and don’t ask who*!

And, deep inside her, a good Entertainer doesn’t care who. Take the way it shaped up this time. It was pretty clear to me I ought to shift to Illy, although I wasn’t quite easy in my mind about leaving Erich, because the Lunan was a long time from home and, after all, Erich was among anthropoids. Ilhilihis needed someone who was *simpatico*.

===Chinese===
我们谁也没法把这些角色样样演到满分，连边都沾不上。我们只是尽力。可号令一到，招待员就必须忘掉宿怨、牢骚、眼红和醋意——别忘了，他们是情感锋利的活泼人——因为那时候容不下任何别的事，只容得下*救人，别问是谁*！

而且，一个出色的招待员，心底深处根本不在乎那「谁」是谁。就拿这一回的格局来说吧。我心里明明白白，我该转去照应伊尔利，虽说撇下埃里希让我多少有点不踏实——因为这位月球人离家太久太远，而埃里希好歹身处类人猿的窝里。伊尔希利希斯需要一个*意气相投*（*simpatico*）的人。

===Original===
I like Illy and not just because he is a sort of tall cross between a spider monkey and a persian cat—though that is a handsome combo when you come to think of it. I like him for himself. So when he came in all lopped and shaky after a mean operation, I was the right person to look out for him. Now I’ve made my little speech and know-nothings in the Change World can go on making their bum jokes. But I ask you, how could an arrangement between Illy and me be anything but Platonic?

We might have had some octopoid girls and nymphs in stock—Sid couldn’t be sure until he checked—but Ilhilihis and Sevensee voted for real people and I knew Sid saw it their way. Maud squeezed Mark’s hand and tripped over to Sevensee (“Those are sharp hoofs you got, man”—she’s picked up some of my language, like she has everything else), though Beau did frown over his shoulder at Lili from the piano, maybe to argue that she ought to take on the E.T., as Mark had been a real casualty and could use live nursing. But it was plain as day to anybody but Beau that Bruce and Lili were a big thing and the last to be disturbed.

===Chinese===
我喜欢伊尔利，倒不光因为他像蜘蛛猴和波斯猫杂交出的一副高挑身段——虽说这么一想，这组合也真叫俊。我喜欢的是他本人。所以，当他经历了一场卑劣的行动、缺着触手、浑身发颤地进来时，照应他的正该是我。好，我的小小演说讲完了，变更世界里那些一无所知的家伙尽可以继续编他们的烂笑话。可我倒要问问你：我和伊尔利之间的安排，除了柏拉图式的，还能是别的什么？

库房里兴许还存着几位章鱼姑娘和宁芙——西德不查一查也不敢说死——可伊尔希利希斯和七观都投了真人一票，我也看得出西德随他们的意。莫德捏了捏马克的手，蹦蹦跳跳奔七观去了（「你这蹄子可够尖利的，伙计」——她捡了我一些口头语，就像她捡起其他一切那样快），只是博从钢琴那边回头朝莉莉皱了皱眉，兴许是想争一句：该她来接待这位 E.T.，因为马克是货真价实的伤员，用得上活人护理。可除了博谁都看得出，布鲁斯和莉莉正是一对天雷地火，最碰不得。

===Original===
Erich acted stiffly hurt at losing me, but I knew he wasn’t. He thinks he has a great technique with Ghostgirls and he likes to show it off, and he really is pretty slick at it, if you go for that sort of thing and—yang my yin!—who doesn’t at times?

===Chinese===
埃里希装出一副失去我而僵僵的委屈样，可我知道他才不委屈。他自认对付幽灵少女有一套绝技，又爱显摆，而且他那手确实相当溜——如果你好这一口的话——而且——给我的阴来点阳！——谁又没有好这一口的时候呢？

===Original===
And when Sid formally wafted the Countess out of Stores—a real blonde stunner in a white satin hobble skirt with a white egret swaying up from her tiny hat, way ahead of Maud and Lili and me when it comes to looks, though transparent as cigarette smoke—and when Erich clicked his heels and bowed over her hand and proudly conducted her to a couch, black Svengali to her Trilby, and started to German-talk some life into her with much head cocking and toothy smiling and a flow of witty flattery, and when she began to flirt back and the dream look in her eyes sharpened hungrily and focused on him—well, then I knew that Erich was happy and felt he was doing proud by the Reichswehr. No, my little commandant wasn’t worrying me on that score.

===Chinese===
再说，当西德郑重其事地把伯爵夫人（the Countess）从库房区里请出来——一位真正的金发尤物，白色缎面窄摆裙，小帽上摇曳着一枝白鹭羽，论容貌把莫德、莉莉和我甩出几条街，虽说透明得像香烟的烟——当埃里希碰响靴跟，吻她的手，风风光光把她引到一张躺椅上，当她的黑斯文加利，配他那特丽尔比（译注：Svengali 与 Trilby，杜·莫里耶小说《软帽子》里的催眠大师与受其摆布的歌女），歪着脑袋、龇着牙笑，用滔滔不绝的机智恭维外加德语，给她嘴里说进一点生气，而她也回抛起媚眼、眼里那副梦茫茫的神色饿津津地聚焦到他身上——好吧，那时候我就知道，埃里希是快活的，还觉得他给国防军争了光。不，我那位小指挥官在那一头上不叫我操心。

---

===Original===
Mark had drawn a Greek hetaera, name of Phryne; I suppose not the one who maybe still does the famous courtroom striptease back in Athens, and he was waking her up with little sips of his scotch and soda, though, from some looks he’d flashed, I got the idea Kaby was the kid he really went for. Sid was coaxing the fighting gal to take some high-energy bread and olives along with the wine, and, for a wonder, Doc seemed to be carrying on an animated and rational conversation with Sevensee and Maud, maybe comparing notes on the Northern Venusian Shallows, and Beau had got on to Panther Rag, and Bruce and Lili were leaning on the piano, smiling very appreciatively, but talking to each other a mile a minute.

Illy turned back from inspecting them all and squeaked, “Animals with clothes are so refreshing, dahling! Like you’re all carrying banners!”

===Chinese===
马克抽到的是一位希腊赫泰拉，名叫弗里妮（Phryne）；想必不是雅典那边也许还在重演法庭脱衣名场面的那一位。他正用他的威士忌苏打一小口一小口把她唤醒，不过，从他抛过去的几记眼风判断，卡比才是他真正相中的妞。西德正哄着那位女斗士就着酒吃几口高能面包和橄榄；而说来稀奇，大夫似乎正同七观和莫德进行一场热烈而条理清楚的谈话，没准在切磋金星北浅海的心得；博已经弹到了《黑豹拉格泰姆》（Panther Rag）；布鲁斯和莉莉倚着钢琴，笑得满怀欣赏，嘴里却互相说个不停，一分钟一里地。

伊尔利把众人巡视了一圈，回过头吱吱道：「穿衣裳的动物们真让人耳目一新，达令！就像你们个个都举着旗！」

===Original===
Maybe he had something there, though my banners were kind of Ash Wednesday, a charcoal gray sweater and skirt. He looked at my mouth with a tentacle to see how I was smiling and he squeaked softly, “Do I seem dull and commonplace to you, Greta girl, because I haven’t got banners? Just another Zombie from a billion years in your past, as gray and lifeless as Luna is today, not as when she was a real dreamy sister planet simply bursting with air and water and feather forests. Or am I as strangely interesting to you as you are to me, girl from a billion years in my future?”

“Illy, you’re sweet,” I told him, giving him a little pat. I noticed his fur was still vibrating nervously and I decided the heck with Sid’s orders, I’m going to pump him about what he was doing with Kaby and the satyr. Couldn’t have him a billion years from home and bottled up, too. Besides, I was curious.

===Chinese===
这话也许有点道理，虽说我的旗有点「圣灰星期三」的味道：炭灰色的毛衣加裙子。他用一条触手端详我的嘴，看我笑得如何，然后轻轻地吱吱道：「就因为我没打旗号，我在你眼里就显得乏味、平常吗，格蕾塔姑娘？不过是来自你那十亿年过去的又一个僵尸，灰扑扑、没生气，跟今天的月亮一样——可不是当年那颗名副其实、如梦似幻的姐妹行星，满得要溢出空气、海洋和羽毛森林。又或者，我在你眼里，也像你在我眼里一样，是那样一种奇异的有趣吗，来自我这十亿年未来的姑娘？」

「伊尔利，你真贴心。」我对他说，轻轻拍了拍他。我注意到他的毛还在紧张地颤，我便打定主意：西德的命令见鬼去吧，我要套一套他跟卡比和半人羊到底在干什么。总不能让他离家十亿年，心里的事还闷着罐。再说，我也好奇。

## VI Crete Circa 1300 BC / 克里特，约公元前 1300 年

===Original===
> Maiden, Nymph, and Mother are the eternal royal Trinity of the island, and the Goddess, who is worshipped there in each of these aspects, as New Moon, Full Moon, and Old Moon, is the sovereign Deity.

===Chinese===
> 少女、宁芙与母亲，是这座岛屿永恒的王家三一体；而女神在这三种面目中受人崇拜——新月、满月与残月——她便是至高的主宰。（译注：罗伯特·格雷夫斯《白色女神》。）

---

===Original===
Kaby pushed back at Sid some seconds of bread and olives, and, when he raised his bushy eyebrows, gave him a curt nod that meant she knew what she was doing. She stood up and sort of took a position. All the talk quieted down fast, even Bruce’s and Lili’s. Kaby’s face and voice weren’t strained now, but they weren’t relaxed either.

“Woe to Spider! Woe to Cretan! Heavy is the news I bring you. Bear it bravely, like strong women. When we got the gun unlimbered, I heard seaweed fry and crackle. We three leaped behind the rock wall, saw our gun grow white as sunlight in a heat-ray of the Serpents! Natch, we feared we were outnumbered and I called upon my Caller.”

===Chinese===
卡比把面包和橄榄朝西德推回去了几秒钟，等他扬起浓眉，便朝他利落地一点头，意思是她知道自己在于什么。她站起身，摆出了一种登台的架势。所有谈话声飞快静了下来，连布鲁斯和莉莉的也一样。卡比的脸和嗓音此刻都不紧绷了，可也谈不上松弛。

「蜘蛛遭殃！克里特遭殃！我带来的消息沉甸甸。要像刚强的女子那样，勇敢地担着。我们刚把炮架开，我就听见海草炸得噼啪响。我们三个跃到石墙后，眼看着我们的炮在蛇方的热射线里白得像太阳！自然，我们怕对方人多，我便向我的呼叫器呼告。」

===Original===
I don’t know how she does it, but she does—in English too. That is, when she figures she’s got something important to report, and maybe she needs a little time to get ready.

Beau claims that all the ancients fit their thoughts into measured lines as naturally as we pick a word that will do, but I’m not sure how good the Vicksburg language department is. Though why I should wonder about things like that when I’ve got Kaby spouting the stuff right in front of me, I don’t know.

“But I didn’t die there, kiddos. I still hoped to hurt the Greek ships, maybe with the Snake’s own heat gun. So I quick tried to outflank them. My two comrades crawled beside me—they are males, but they have courage. Soon we spied the ambush-setters. They were Snakes and they were many, filthily disguised as Cretans.”

===Chinese===
我不知道她是怎么做到的，反正她做到了——还是用英语。也就是说，当她认定自己有重要军情要汇报的时候；没准她还需要一点时间酝酿。

博声称，古人把思想装进格律句行，就像我们随口挑个现成的词一样自然，可我拿不准维克斯堡大学语言系的水准有多高。虽说，卡比正在我眼前滔滔不绝地喷这路货色，我还犯什么嘀咕，我也不知道。

「可我没死在那儿，小家伙们。我还想伤一伤希腊人的船，没准就用蛇方自己的热枪。于是我赶紧想绕他们的侧翼。我那两位战友在我身边匍匐——他们是男的，可有胆量。很快我们窥见了设伏的家伙。是蛇方，人数众多，肮脏地装扮成克里特人。」

===Original===
There was an indignant murmur at this, for our cutthroat Change War has its code, the Soldiers tell me. Being an Entertainer, I don’t have to say what I think.

“They had seen us when we saw them,” Kaby swept on, “and they loosed a killing volley. Heat- and knife-rays struck about us in a storm of wind and fire, and the Lunan lost a feeler, fighting for Crete’s Triple Goddess. So we dodged behind a sand hill, steered our flight back toward the water. It was awful, what we saw there: Crete’s brave ships all sunk or sinking, blue sky sullied by their death-smoke. Once again the Greeks had licked us!—aided by the filthy Serpents.

“Round our wrecks, their black ships scurried, like black beetles, filth their diet, yet this day they dine on heroes. On the quiet sunlit beach there, I could feel a Change Gale blowing, working changes deep inside me, aches and pains that were a stranger’s. Half my memories were doubled, half my lifeline crooked and twisted, three new moles upon my sword-hand. Goddess, Goddess, Triple Goddess—”

===Chinese===
此话一出，四下响起愤愤的低吼，因为士兵们告诉我，咱们这场杀人不见血的变更战争也有它的规矩。身为招待员，我心里怎么想，不必说出口。

「我们望见他们的当口，他们也望见了我们，」卡比滔滔而下，「他们放出一阵索命的齐射。热射线和刀子射线裹着风火的风暴砸在我们四周，月球人为克里特三重女神而战，折了一条触手。于是我们闪进一座沙丘背后，掉头朝水边败退。我们在那儿看见的景象可怕极了：克里特英勇的船队尽数沉没或正在沉没，蓝天被它们的死亡之烟熏得污浊。希腊人又一次把我们打趴了！——靠着那群肮脏的蛇方帮衬。

「在我们的残骸四周，他们的黑船窜来窜去，像一群黑甲虫，以污秽为食，这一天却拿英雄下酒。在那片安静向阳的海滩上，我觉出一阵变更飓风在刮，在我身体深处动着手脚，一阵阵酸痛，都是陌生人的。我一半的记忆翻成了双份，一半的生命线折了又拧，持剑的手上添了三颗新痣。女神，女神，三重女神——」

---

===Original===
Her voice wavered and Sid reached out a hand, but she straightened her back.

“Triple Goddess, give me courage to tell everything that happened. We ran down into the water, hoping to escape by diving. We had hardly gotten under when the heat-rays hit above us, turning all the cool green surface to a roaring white inferno. But as I believe I told you, I was calling on my Caller, and a Door now opened to us, deep below the deadly steam-clouds. We dived in like frightened minnows and a lot of water with us.”

Off Chicago’s Gold Coast, Dave once gave me a lesson in skin-diving and, remembering it, I got a flash of Kaby’s Door in the dark depths.

===Chinese===
她的声音发颤，西德伸出手去，她却挺直了背脊。

「三重女神，赐我勇气，讲出发生过的一切。我们奔进水里，指望潜水脱身。我们刚没顶，热射线就砸在我们头顶，把整片清凉的绿面变成一片咆哮的白色炼狱。可我相信我说过了，我一直在向我的呼叫器呼告，于是一扇门向我们敞开，就在那要命的热蒸汽云底下深处。我们像受惊的小鲦鱼一样扎了进去，还连带卷进了一大股水。」

在芝加哥黄金海岸的外海，戴夫曾给我上过一课赤身潜泳，记着那一课，我脑中闪现出卡比那扇门悬在幽暗深处的样子。

===Original===
“For a moment, all was chaos. Then the Door slammed shut behind us. We’d been picked up in time’s nick by—an Express Room of our Spiders!—sloshing two feet deep in water, much more cramped for space than this Place. It was manned by a magician, an old coot named Benson-Carter. He dispelled the water quickly and reported on his Caller. We’d got dry, were feeling human, Illy here had shed his swimsuit, when we looked at the Maintainer. It was glowing, changing, melting! And when Benson-Carter touched it, he fell backward—death was in him. Then the Void began to darken, narrow, shrink and close around us, so I called upon my Caller—without wasting time, let me tell you!”

“We can’t say for sure what was it slowly squeezed that sweet Express Room, but we fear the dirty Snakes have found a way to find our Places and attack outside the cosmos!—found the Spiderweb that links us in the Void’s gray less-than-nothing.”

===Chinese===
「有一阵子，全是混乱。随后门在我们身后砰地关死。我们在千钧一发之际被接走——接进咱们蜘蛛方的一间特快室（Express Room）！——里面积水深及两英尺，比这场所逼仄得多。室里只有一位术士主事，一个叫本森-卡特的老怪物。他飞快排掉了水，对着呼叫器汇报。我们刚弄干身子、找回人样，这儿的伊尔利也褪下了他的潜泳衣，就在这时我们望向维持器。它正发着光，变着形，熔化着！本森-卡特一碰它，便向后栽倒——死亡已经进了他的身子。接着虚空开始发暗、收窄、紧缩，朝我们围拢过来，我便向我的呼叫器呼告——一点工夫也没敢耽搁，我告诉你们！」

「到底是什么东西慢慢勒死了那间甜美的特快室，我们说不准，可我们担心，肮脏的蛇方已经找到了发现我们各处场所、在宇宙外头发起攻击的路数！——摸到了那张维系我们大家的蜘蛛网，就在虚空那片灰色的、比『没有』还少的所在。」

===Original===
No murmur this time. This reaction was genuine; we’d been hit where we lived and I could see everybody was scared as sick as I was. Except maybe Bruce and Lili, who were still holding hands and beaming gently. I decided they were the kind that love makes brave, which it doesn’t do to me. It just gives me two people to worry about.

“I can see you dig our feelings,” Kaby continued. “This thing scared the pants off of us. If we could have, we’d have even Introverted the Maintainer, broken all the ties that bind us, chanced it incommunicado. But the little old Maintainer was a seething red-hot puddle filled with bubbles big as handballs. We sat tight and watched the Void close. I kept calling on my Caller.”

===Chinese===
这回没有低吼。这一下反应是实打实的；我们被人打到了家门口，我看得出每个人都被吓得跟我一样五内翻腾。也许布鲁斯和莉莉除外——他们还牵着手，柔光焕发。我断定他们是那种被爱情壮了胆的人；爱情可没这功效——对我而言。它只给我添两个要操心的人。

「我看得出来，你们懂我们的心情，」卡比续道。「这玩意儿把我们的裤子都吓掉了。要是由得我们，我们连内闭维持器都干了——扯断一切拴着我们的纽带，豁出去与世隔绝碰运气。可那位小老维持器已经烧成一汪滚烫通红的浆水，咕嘟咕嘟顶着有手球大的泡。我们死死坐定，看着虚空合拢。我一遍又一遍向我的呼叫器呼告。」

---

===Original===
I squeezed my eyes shut, but that made it easier to see the three of them with the Void shutting down on them. (Was ours still behaving? Yes, Bibi Miriam.) Poetry or no poetry, it got me.

“Benson-Carter, lying dying, also thought the Snakes had done it. And he knew that death was in him, so he whispered me his mission, giving me precise instructions: how to press the seven death’s hands, starting lockside counterclockwise, one, three, five, six, two, four, seven, then you have a half an hour; after you have pressed the seven, do not monkey with the buttons—get out fast and don’t stop moving.”

I wasn’t getting this part and I couldn’t see that anyone else was, though Bruce was whispering to Lili. I remembered seeing skulls engraved on the bronze chest. I looked at Illy and he nodded a tentacle and spread two to say, I guessed, that yes, Benson-Carter had said something like that, but no, Illy didn’t know much about it.

===Chinese===
我使劲闭紧眼睛，可这样反倒更容易看见他们三个被虚空一寸寸压住的样子。（咱们这台还规矩吗？规矩，比比·玛丽亚姆（Bibi Miriam）。）管它有没有诗韵，这画面攥住了我。

「本森-卡特躺着等死的时候，也认定是蛇方干的。他知道死亡已在自己身子里，便把他的任务低声传给了我，交代得一字不差：怎样按那七只死亡之手，从锁的一侧起逆时针——一、三、五、六、二、四、七——按完你就有半个钟头；按完这七个之后，别拿那些钮瞎摆弄——撒腿快跑，脚下别停。」

这一段我没听明白，我看别人也没明白，虽说布鲁斯正对着莉莉耳语。我想起在那只青铜箱上见过的骷髅浮雕。我看向伊尔利，他点了点一条触手，又张开两条，我猜是说：没错，本森-卡特是说过类似的话，可是不，伊尔利自己也不甚了了。

===Original===
“All these things and more he whispered,” Kaby went on, “with the last gasps of his life-force, telling all his secret orders—for he’d not been sent to get us, he was on a separate mission, when he heard my SOS’s. Sid, it’s you he was to contact, as the first leg of his mission, pick up from you three black hussars, death’s-head Demons, daring Soldiers, then to wait until the Places next match rhythm with the cosmos—matter of two mealtimes, barely—and to tune in northern Egypt in the age of the last Caesar, in the year of Rome’s swift downfall, there to start an operation in a battle near a city named for Thrace’s Alexander, there to change the course of battle, blow sky-high the stinking Serpents, all their agents, all their Zombies!

“Goddess, pardon, now I savvy how you’ve guided my least footstep, when I thought you’d gone and left me—for I flubbed your three-mole signal. We’ve found Sid’s Place, that’s the first leg, and I see the three black hussars, and we’ve brought with us the weapon and the Parthian disguises, salvaged from the doomed Express Room when your Door appeared in time’s nick, and the Room around us closing spewed us through before it vanished with the corpse of Benson-Carter. Triple Goddess, draw the milk now from the womanhood I flaunt here and inject the blackest hatred! Vengeance now upon the Serpents, vengeance sweet in northern Egypt, for your island, Crete, Goddess!—and a victory for the Spiders! Goddess, Goddess, we can swing it!”

===Chinese===
「这一切还有更多，他都用生命力的最后一丝丝气，低声传给了我，把机密命令倾囊相授——因为他本不是派来接我们的，他另有一趟任务，是听见了我一道道 SOS 才顺路捞人。西德，他要联络的头一程正是你：从你这儿提走三名黑衣骠骑兵、骷髅魔鬼、亡命的士兵，然后等到各场所下一次与宇宙节奏相合——顶多两顿饭的工夫，撑死——再调到末代恺撒的时代、罗马速亡之年的北埃及，在一场战役里启动行动，战场挨着一座以色雷斯的亚历山大命名的城市；到那儿去扭转战局，把那帮发臭的蛇方，连同他们全部爪牙、全部僵尸，炸上天！

「女神啊，恕罪，如今我懂了，您一直引领着我最细微的脚步——在我以为您撇下我走掉的时候——因为我错会了您三颗痣的信号。我们寻到了西德的场所，这是头一程；我望见了三名黑衣骠骑兵；我们也把武器和帕提亚人的伪装带来了，是在您那扇门千钧一发地出现时，从那间注定的特快室里抢救出来的；随后围拢我们的特快室把我们喷了出去，赶在它带着本森-卡特的尸身消失之前。三重女神啊，此刻就从我夸示于人的这段女儿身里抽出乳汁，注入最黑的仇恨吧！现在就向蛇方复仇，在北埃及复仇，酣畅地复仇，为了您的海岛克里特，女神！——也为蜘蛛方赢一场胜利！女神，女神，咱们能成！」

===Original===
The roar that made me try to stop my ears with my shoulders didn’t come from Kaby—she’d spoken her piece—but from Sid. The dear boy was purple enough to make me want to remind him you can die of high blood pressure just as easy in the Change World.

“Dump me with ops! ’Sblood, I’ll not endure it! Is this a battle post? They’ll be mounting operations from field hospitals next. Kabysia Labrys, thou art mad to suggest it. And what’s this prattle of locks, clocks, and death’s heads, buttons and monkeys? This brabble, this farrago, this hocus-pocus! And where’s the weapon you prate of? In that whoreson bronze casket, I suppose.”

She nodded, looking blank and almost a little shy as poetic possession faded from her. Her answer came like its faltering last echo.

“It is nothing but a tiny tactical atomic bomb.”

===Chinese===
那一声吼震得我想拿肩膀去堵耳朵，吼声不是出自卡比——她的话已经说尽——而是出自西德。这位老哥脸紫得叫我想提醒他一句：在变更世界里，高血压照样能要人命。

「拿行动来埋汰我！皇天在上，我忍不了！这里是战斗岗位吗？照这么下去，下回他们怕不是要从野战医院里发兵打仗了。卡比西娅·拉布里斯，你这是痴人说梦。还有这一套锁啊钟啊骷髅啊、钮扣啊猴子啊的饶舌，算什么名堂？这通聒噪、这锅杂烩、这套障眼法！还有你吹嘘的武器在哪儿？我猜，就在那口婊子养的青铜匣子里。」

她点了点头，诗的附身从她身上褪去，她显得一片茫然，还几乎有点羞怯。她的回答传来，像那句咒文将断未断的最后一记回声。

「它不过是一枚小小的战术原子弹。」

## VII Time to Think / 思考的时间

===Original===
> After about 0.1 millisecond (one ten-thousandth part of a second) has elapsed, the radius of the ball of fire is some 45 feet, and the temperature is then in the vicinity of 300,000 degrees Centigrade. At this instant, the luminosity, as observed at a distance of 100,000 yards (5.7 miles), is approximately 100 times that of the sun as seen at the earth’s surface … the ball of fire expands very rapidly to its maximum radius of 450 feet within less than a second from the explosion.

===Chinese===
> 大约经过 0.1 毫秒（一秒的万分之一）后，火球半径约为 45 英尺，温度在 300,000 摄氏度上下。此刻，在 100,000 码（5.7 英里）外观测，其亮度约为地表所见太阳的 100 倍……爆炸后不到一秒，火球便急速膨胀至 450 英尺的最大半径。（译注：出自 1950 年《原子武器的效应》。）

---

===Original===
Brother, that was all we needed to make everybody but Kaby and the two E.T.s start yelping at once, me included. It may seem strange that Change People, able to whiz through time and space and roust around outside the cosmos and knowing at least by hearsay of weapons a billion years in the future, like the Mindbomb, should panic at being shut in with a little primitive mid-20th Century gadget. Well, they feel the same as atomic scientists would feel if a Bengal tiger were brought into their laboratory, neither more nor less scared.

I’m a moron at physics, but I do know the Fireball is bigger than the Place. Remember that, besides the bomb, we’d recently been presented with a lot of other fears we hadn’t had time to cope with, especially the business of the Snakes having learned how to get at our Places and melt the Maintainers and collapse them. Not to mention the general impression—first Saint Petersburg, then Crete—that the whole Change War was going against the Spiders.

Yet, in a free corner of my mind, I was shocked at how badly we were all panicking. It made me admit what I didn’t like to: that we were all in pretty much the same state as Doc, except that the bottle didn’t happen to be our out.

And had the rest of us been controlling our drinking so well lately?

===Chinese===
老兄，就这么一下，除了卡比和两位 E.T.，人人同时开嚎，包括我在内。说来也许奇怪：变更之民，能在时空里嗖嗖穿行、能在宇宙外头撒欢，至少也风闻过十亿年后的武器，比如心弹（Mindbomb），居然会跟一台 20 世纪中叶的小小原始玩意儿关在一处，吓成这副德行。呃，他们的感受，就跟有人往原子科学家的实验室里牵进一头孟加拉虎时他们的感受一模一样，不多一分惊，不少一分吓。

我物理上是个白痴，可我确实知道：那颗火球比整个场所还大。别忘了，除了炸弹，我们新近还领了一堆别的恐惧，都来不及消化，尤其是蛇方学会了怎么摸到我们的场所、熔掉维持器、把场所压垮这档子事。更别提那个总的印象——先是圣彼得堡，后是克里特——整场变更战争都在朝着不利于蜘蛛方的方向走。

可在头脑里一个还空着的角落，我还是被我们大家慌乱的程度吓了一跳。这逼得我承认了一件我不愿承认的事：我们都跟大夫差不太多，只不过我们的出路碰巧不是酒瓶子。

再说，我们其余这些人，最近的酒量就控制得那么好吗？

===Original===
Maud yelled, “Jettison it!” and pulled away from the satyr and ran from the bronze chest. Beau, harking back to what they’d thought of doing in the Express Room when it was too late, hissed, “Sirs, we must Introvert,” and vaulted over the piano bench and legged it for the control divan. Erich seconded him with a white-faced “*Gott in Himmel, ja!*” from beside the surly, forgotten Countess, holding, by its slim stem, an empty, rose-stained wine glass.

I felt my mind flinch, because Introverting a Place is several degrees worse than foxholing. It’s supposed not only to keep the Door tight shut, but also to lock it so even the Change Winds can’t get through—cut the Place loose from the cosmos altogether.

I’d never talked with anyone from a Place that had been Introverted.

===Chinese===
莫德尖叫：「把它抛出去！」挣开半人羊，从青铜箱边跑开。博想起他们在特快室里想干却为时已晚的那一手，嘶声道：「诸位先生，我们必须内闭，」随即翻过钢琴凳，直奔控制躺椅。埃里希在一边帮腔，脸色煞白地喊了声「*Gott in Himmel, ja!*（老天在上，正是！）」——他身旁，是被冷落在一旁、一脸愠色的伯爵夫人，纤纤捏着杯脚，杯里只剩玫瑰色的酒渍。

我的心智抽了一下，因为内闭一个场所，比钻狐狸洞还糟上好几级。按说法，它不光要把门关得死紧，还要落锁——连变更之风都透不进来——把场所从宇宙上整个砍断。

我从没跟哪个内闭过的场所里出来的人说过话。

---

===Original===
Mark dumped Phryne off his lap and ran after Maud. The Greek Ghostgirl, quite solid now, looked around with sleepy fear and fumbled her apple-green chiton together at the throat. She wrenched my attention away from everyone else for a moment, and I couldn’t help wondering whether the person or Zombie back in the cosmos, from whose lifeline the Ghost has been taken, doesn’t at least have strange dreams or thoughts when something like this happens.

Sid stopped Beau, though he almost got bowled over doing it, and he held the gambler away from the Maintainer in a bear hug and bellowed over his shoulders, “Masters, are you mad? Have you lost your wits? Maud! Mark! Marcus! Magdalene! On your lives, unhand that casket!”

Maud had swept the clothes and bows and quivers and stuff off it and was dragging it out from the bar toward the Door sector, so as to dump it through fast when we got one, I guess, while Mark acted as if he were trying to help her and wrestle it away from her at the same time.

===Chinese===
马克把弗里妮从膝上倒下去，追莫德去了。那位希腊幽灵少女此刻已相当成形，带着惺忪的惊惶四下张望，手指在喉前摸索着拢起她苹果绿的希顿长衣。有一瞬间，她把我的注意力从所有人身上拧了过去，我不禁琢磨：那个留在宇宙里的人、那个僵尸——幽灵正是从他的生命线上取下来的——碰上这种事的时候，至少会不会做些怪梦、起些怪念头？

西德拦下了博，虽说差点被撞个趔趄，他熊抱住这位赌徒，不让他挨着维持器，越过他的肩头吼道：「师傅们，你们疯了吗？丢了魂了吗？莫德！马克！马库斯！玛格达琳娜！要命就把那匣子放下！」

莫德已经把箱上的衣甲、弓、箭壶什么的扫到一边，正把它从吧台那儿朝门区拖，我猜是想等门一开就快点扔出去；马克那边的做派，却像是既想帮她拖、又想从她手里抢走，两样同时进行。

===Original===
They kept on as if they hadn’t heard a word Sid said, with Mark yelling, “Let go, *meretrix*! This holds Rome’s answer to Parthia on the Nile.”

Kaby watched them as if she wanted to help Mark but scorned to scuffle with a mere—well, Mark had said it in Latin, I guess—call girl.

Then, on the top of the bronze chest, I saw those seven lousy skulls starting at the lock as plain as if they’d been under a magnifying glass, though ordinarily they’d have been a vague circle to my eyes at the distance, and I lost my mind and started to run in the opposite direction, but Illy whipped three tentacles around me, gentle-like, and squeaked, “Easy now, Greta girl, don’t you be doing it, too. Hold still or Papa spank. My, my, but you two-leggers can whirl about when you have a mind to.”

My stampede had carried his featherweight body a couple of yards, but it stopped me and I got my mind back, partly.

“Unhand it, I say!” Sid repeated without accomplishing anything, and he released Beau, though he kept a hand near the gambler’s shoulder.

===Chinese===
两人只顾拖拽，仿佛西德的话一个字也没进耳朵，马克还吼着：「撒手，*meretrix*（拉丁语『娼妇』）！这里面装着罗马对尼罗河畔帕提亚的答复。」

卡比看着他们，像是想帮马克，又耻于跟一个区区——嗯，马克是用拉丁语说的，我猜——应召女郎动手。

接着，在青铜箱顶上，我清清楚楚看见了从锁边排开的那七颗倒霉骷髅，清楚得像搁在放大镜底下——照那个距离，平时在我眼里该只是模糊的一圈——我的脑子丢了缰，撒腿就朝反方向跑，伊尔利却轻柔地甩过三条触手把我一卷，吱吱道：「放轻松，格蕾塔姑娘，你可别也来这一套。站好，不然爹爹打屁股。哎呀呀，你们两条腿的，想转起来的时候，可真转得飞快。」

我这一阵狂奔，驮着他那副没几两羽毛分量的身子跑出去好几码，但它止住了我，我的脑子也回来了，回来了一半。

「我说，放下它！」西德重复了一遍，毫无成效，随后松开博，一只手还留在这赌徒肩膀边上。

===Original===
Then my fat friend from Lynn Regis looked real distraught at the Void and blustered at no one in particular, “ ’Sdeath, think you I’d mutiny against my masters, desert the Spiders, go to ground like a spent fox and pull my hole in after me? A plague of such cowardice! Who suggests it? Introversion’s no mere last-ditch device. Unless ordered, supervised and sanctioned, it means the end. And what if I’d Introverted ere we got Kaby’s call for succor, hey?”

===Chinese===
随后，我这位来自雷吉斯林恩（即金斯林）的胖朋友望着虚空，一脸真正的心乱如麻，冲着不特定的谁大放厥词：「皇天啊，你们以为我会背叛主上、开蜘蛛方的小差，像只跑乏了的狐狸钻进洞去，还把洞口拖塌在身后吗？这等怯懦，瘟死它！是谁出的这主意？内闭可不是走投无路才使的招。没有命令、监督和钦准，它就意味着完蛋。想想看，要是卡比呼救之前我就内闭了，嘿？」

---

===Original===
His warrior maid nodded with harsh approval and he noticed it and shook his free hand at her and scolded her, “Not that I say yea to your mad plan for that Devil’s casket, you half-clad lackwit. And yet to jettison. … Oh, ye gods, ye gods—” he wiped his hand across his face—“grant me a minute in which I may think!”

Thinking time wasn’t an item even on the strictly limited list at the moment, although Sevensee, squatting dourly on his hairy haunches where Maud had left him, threw in a deadpan “Thas tellin’ ’em, Gov.”

Then Doc at the bar stood up tall as Abe Lincoln in his top hat and shawl and 19th Century duds and raised an unwavering arm for silence and said something that sounded like: “Introversh, inversh, glovsh,” and then his enunciation switched to better than perfect as he continued, “I know to an absolute certainty what we must do.”

It showed me how rabbity we were that the Place got quiet as a church while we all stopped whatever we were doing and waited breathless for a poor drunk to tell us how to save ourselves.

He said something like, “Inversh … bosh …” and held our eyes for a moment longer. Then the light went out of his and he slobbered out a “*Nichevo*” and slid an arm far along the bar for a bottle and started to pour it down his throat without stopping sliding.

Before he completed his collapse to the floor, in the split second while our attention was still focused on the bar, Bruce vaulted up on top of it, so fast it was almost like he’d popped up from nowhere, though I’d seen him start from behind the piano.

===Chinese===
他的女战士点头，给予冷硬的赞许，他注意到了，朝她晃着空出来的那只手，训斥道：「我可不是赞成你对那口魔匣的疯计划，你这衣衫不整的蠢丫头。可要说抛出去……噢，众神啊，众神——」他抹了把脸——「赏我一分钟，容我想一想！」

思考时间这东西，眼下就连在那张紧巴巴的短缺清单上也排不上号，虽说七观——他蹲在莫德撇下他的地方，毛茸茸的臀腿上蹲得阴郁——面无表情地补了一句：「这话提气，长官。」

这时，吧台边的大夫站了起来，大礼帽、披肩加一身 19 世纪行头，个头高得赛林肯，举起一条纹丝不动的手臂示意肃静，说了句听着像「内闭休，逆置休，手套休」的话，随即他的咬字陡然清楚得赛过字正腔圆，接着道：「我们该做什么，我一清二楚，绝对的一清二楚。」

场所静得像座教堂，我们全都停下手里的事，屏息等一个可怜的醉鬼来教我们怎么自救——这可见我们怂到了何等地步。

他又说了句像「逆置……个屁……」的话，把我们的目光又攥住了一瞬。随后他眼里的光熄了，涎着嘴角吐出一声「*Nichevo*（没什么）」，一条胳膊沿吧台滑出去老远够到一瓶酒，人还在滑，酒已经往喉咙里灌。

不等他完成倒在地板上的最后一程，趁我们的注意力还钉在吧台上的那一瞬，布鲁斯已经跃上了吧台，快得几乎像凭空冒出来，虽说我是看见他从钢琴后面起步的。

===Original===
“I’ve a question. Has anyone here triggered that bomb?” he said in a voice that was very clear and just loud enough. “So it can’t go off,” he went on after just the right pause, his easy grin and brisk manner putting more heart into me all the time. “What’s more, if it were to be triggered, we’d still have half an hour. I believe you said it had that long a fuse?”

He stabbed a finger at Kaby. She nodded.

“Right,” he said. “It’d have to be that long for whoever plants it in the Parthian camp to get away. There’s another safety margin.

“Second question. Is there a locksmith in the house?”

===Chinese===
「我有个问题。在座有人引爆过那颗炸弹吗？」他的嗓音非常清亮，音量也恰到好处。「所以它不可能已经待发。」顿了恰到好处的一顿，他接着说，他那从容的笑和利落的做派一直往我心里添胆气。「再者，就算它被引爆，我们也还有半个钟头。我没记错的话，你说它的引信有这么长？」

他一指戳向卡比。她点头。

「不错，」他说。「把东西埋进帕提亚营盘的人要脱身，引信就得有这么长。这是又一道保险。」

「第二个问题。屋里有锁匠吗？」

---

===Original===
For all Bruce’s easiness, he was watching us like a golden eagle and he caught Beau’s and Maud’s affirmatives before they had a chance to explain or hedge them and said, “That’s very good. Under certain circumstances, you two’d be the ones to go to work on the chest. But before we consider that, there’s Question Three: Is anyone here an atomics technician?”

That one took a little conversation to straighten out, Illy having to explain that, yes, the Early Lunans had atomic power—hadn’t they blasted the life off their planet with it and made all those ghastly craters?—but no, he wasn’t a technician exactly, he was a “thinger” (I thought at first his squeakbox was lisping); what was a thinger?—well, a thinger was someone who manipulated things in a way that was truly impossible to describe, but no, you couldn’t possibly thing atomics; the idea was quite ridiculous, so he couldn’t be an atomics thinger; the term was worse than a contradiction, well, really!—while Sevensee, from his two-thousand-millennia advantage of the Lunan, grunted to the effect that his culture didn’t rightly use any kind of power, but just sort of moved satyrs and stuff by wrastling space-time around, “or think ’em roun’ ef we hafta. Can’t think ’em in the Void, tho, wus luck. Hafta have—I dunno wut. Dun havvit anyhow.”

===Chinese===
别看布鲁斯一派松弛，他拿眼睛扫我们扫得像只金雕，博和莫德的应声还没来得及解释或找补，就被他捉住了，他说：「很好。在某些情况下，动手开箱的就该是你们二位。可在考虑这个之前，还有第三个问题：在座有谁是原子技师吗？」

这个问题费了点口舌才理清：伊尔利不得不解释，没错，早期月球人是有原子能的——他们不就是拿它把星球上的生命炸了个精光、炸出那许多狰狞的环形山吗？——可是不，他不算技师，他是个「事物师」（thinger）（起先我还当是他的吱吱匣漏了风）；什么是事物师？——呃，事物师就是用一种实在没法描述的方式操持事物的人；可是不，你没法「事物」原子能；这想法荒唐透顶，所以他当不成原子事物师；这字眼比自相矛盾还糟，真是的！——与此同时，七观凭着比月球人晚了两千个千年的优势哼了几声，大意是：他们那个文化压根不正经用什么能，就是扭着时空搬半人羊什么的，「实在不成，就想着挪。可在虚空里想不动，倒霉催的。得有——俺也说不清啥。反正没有。」

===Original===
“So we don’t have an A-tech,” Bruce summed up, “which makes it worse than useless, downright dangerous, to tamper with the chest. We wouldn’t know what to do if we did get inside safely. One more question.” He directed it toward Sid. “How long before we can jettison anything?”

Sid, looking a shade jealous, yet mostly grateful for the way Bruce had calmed his chickens, started to explain, but Bruce didn’t seem to be taking any chance of losing his audience, and as soon as Sid got to the word “rhythm,” he pulled the answer away from him.

“In brief, not until we can effectively tune in on the cosmos again. Thank you, Master Lessingham. That’s at least five hours—two mealtimes, as the Cretan officer put it,” and he threw Kaby a quick soldierly smile. “So, whether the bomb goes to Egypt or elsewhere, there’s not a thing we can do about it for five hours. All right then!”

===Chinese===
「所以，我们没有原子技师（A-tech），」布鲁斯总结道，「这就使得乱动那口箱子不但无益，反而更糟——简直危险。就算平安进了箱，我们也两眼一抹黑。最后一个问题。」他把问题抛向西德。「多久之后我们才能往外抛东西？」

西德显出几分醋意，但更多的是感激——感激布鲁斯把他这群小鸡崽哄得服服帖帖——便开始解释，可布鲁斯似乎不打算冒丢掉听众的险，西德刚说到「节奏」两个字，他就把答案从人家嘴里拽了出来。

「简而言之，得等我们能再次有效调上宇宙。谢谢你，莱辛厄姆师傅。那至少是五个钟头——用这位克里特军官的话说，两顿饭。」说着他朝卡比飞快地投去一个军人式的微笑。「所以，不管炸弹是去埃及还是去别处，五个钟头之内，我们对它无计可施。好，那么！」

===Original===
His smile blinked out like a light and he took a couple of steps up and down the bar, as if measuring the space he had. Two or three cocktail glasses sailed off and popped, but he didn’t seem to notice them and we hardly did either. It was creepy the way he kept staring from one to another of us. We had to look up. Behind his face, with the straight golden hair flirting around it, was only the Void.

“All right then,” he repeated suddenly. “We’re twelve Spiders and two Ghosts, and we’ve time for a bit of a talk, and we’re all in the same bloody boat, fighting the same bloody war, so we’ll all know what we’re talking about. I raised the subject a while back, but I was steamed up about a glove, and it was a big jest. All right! But now the gloves are off!”

===Chinese===
他的笑像灯一样啪地灭了，他在吧台上来回踱了两趟，像在丈量自己名下的地盘。两三只鸡尾酒杯飞出去摔得脆响，他却似乎没觉察，我们也几乎没觉察。他挨个盯视我们每一个人的那副样子，瘆得慌。我们不得不仰起头。他那张脸的背后，笔直的金发在脸侧撩动，再往后，就只有虚空。

「好，那么，」他忽而又重复一遍。「我们是十二个蜘蛛方的人，加两位幽灵，有的是时间聊上一聊，我们又全在同一条血淋淋的船上，打着同一场血淋淋的仗，所以谈的是什么，人人心里有数。这话题我前一阵提过一回，可那时我为一只手套上了火，闹成了一个大笑话。好啊！可现在，手套摘下来了！」（译注：英语 the gloves are off，习语「动真格了」，此处与布鲁斯那对手套正相映成趣。）

---

===Original===
Bruce ripped them out of his belt where they’d been tucked and slammed them down on the bar, to be kicked off the next time he paced back and forth, and it wasn’t funny.

“Because,” he went right on, “I’ve been getting a completely new picture of what this Spiders’ war has been doing to each one of us. Oh, it’s jolly good sport to slam around in space and time and then have a rugged little party outside both of them when the operation’s over. It’s sweet to know there’s no cranny of reality so narrow, no privacy so intimate or sacred, no wall of was or will be strong enough, that we can’t shoulder in. Knowledge is a glamorous thing, sweeter than lust or gluttony or the passion of fighting and including all three, the ultimate insatiable hunger, and it’s great to be Faust, even in a pack of other Fausts.

===Chinese===
布鲁斯把那双手套从塞着的腰带里扯出来，掼在吧台上——下回他再来回踱步，一脚就能踢飞——这回一点也不好笑。

「因为，」他径直讲下去，「我一直在得到一幅全新的图景：这场蜘蛛的战争，对我们每一个人都做了些什么。哦，在空间和时间里横冲直撞，行动收场后再到两者之外开一场硬汉小派对，确实是妙趣。知道现实中没有任何一个角落窄得进不去，没有任何隐私亲昵或神圣到不容打扰，没有任何一堵『曾经』或『将来』的墙结实得推不倒，这很甜美。知识是个光彩夺目的东西，比情欲、饕餮和战斗的激情都甜，又把三者尽数包藏——是最终极的不知餍足的饥饿；而当一回浮士德是了不起的，哪怕是在一大群别的浮士德里当。

===Original===
“It’s sweet to jigger reality, to twist the whole course of a man’s life or a culture’s, to ink out his or its past and scribble in a new one, and be the only one to know and gloat over the changes—hah! killing men or carrying off women isn’t in it for glutting the sense of power. It’s sweet to feel the Change Winds blowing through you and know the pasts that were and the past that is and the pasts that may be. It’s sweet to wield the Atropos and cut a Zombie or Unborn out of his lifeline and look the Doubleganger in the face and see the Resurrection-glow in it and Recruit a brother, welcome a newborn fellow Demon into our ranks and decide whether he’ll best fit as Soldier, Entertainer, or what.

“Or he can’t stand Resurrection, it fries or freezes him, and you’ve got to decide whether to return him to his lifeline and his Zombie dreams, only they’ll be a little grayer and horrider than they were before, or whether, if she’s got that tantalizing something, to bring her shell along for a Ghostgirl—that’s sweet, too. It’s even sweet to have Change Death poised over your neck, to know that the past isn’t the precious indestructible thing you’ve been taught it was, to know that there’s no certainty about the future either, whether there’ll even be one, to know that no part of reality is holy, that the cosmos itself may wink out like a flicked switch and God be not and nothing left but nothing!”

===Chinese===
「篡改现实是甜的：拧转一个人或一种文化的整条命途，把他或它的旧过去一笔墨涂掉，再潦草写上新的，而且只有你一个人知情，只有你一个人对着这些改动暗自得意——哈！论填饱权力欲，杀人越货、抢男霸女都比不上这个。感觉变更之风穿身而过，知晓那些『曾经的过去』、眼下『实有的过去』、还有那些『可能的过去』，是甜的。挥动阿特罗波斯，把一个僵尸或未生者从他的生命线上割下来，直视那张二重身的脸，看见脸上的复活辉光，招募一位弟兄，欢迎一个新生的魔鬼同道入伙，再掂量他当士兵合适、当招待员合适，还是别的什么合适，也是甜的。

「又或者他扛不住复活，被它烤焦或冻僵，你就得掂量：是把他退回他的生命线和他的僵尸梦去——只是那梦会比从前更灰、更可怖——还是，倘若她身上有那种叫人心痒的什么，把她的空壳带上，充作一位幽灵少女——那也是甜的。甚至，脖颈上悬着变更之死也是甜的：知道过去并不是人家教你信的那件珍贵不坏之物，知道未来也无任何确定可言、连有没有都成问题，知道现实的任何一部分都不神圣，知道宇宙本身都可能像被啪嗒拨了一下开关似的一瞬熄灭，上帝不复存在，剩下的只有虚无，只剩下『没有』本身！」

===Original===
He threw out his arms against the Void. “And knowing all that, it’s doubly sweet to come through the Door into the Place and be out of the worst of the Change Winds and enjoy a well-earned Recuperation and share the memories of all these sweetnesses I’ve been talking about, and work out all the fascinating feelings you’ve been accumulating back in the cosmos, layer by black layer, in the company of and with the help of the best bloody little band of fellow Fausts and Faustines going!”

“Oh, it’s a sweet life, all right, but I’m asking you—” and here his eyes stabbed us again, one by one, fast—“I’m asking you what it’s done to us. I’ve been getting a completely new picture, as I said, of what my life was and what it could have been if there’d been changes of the sort that even we Demons can’t make, and what my life is. I’ve been watching how we’ve all been responding to things just now, to the news of Saint Petersburg and to what the Cretan officer told beautifully—only it wasn’t beautiful what she had to tell—and mostly to that bloody box of bomb. And I’m simply asking each one of you, what’s happened to you?”

===Chinese===
他朝虚空张开双臂。「而在知道这一切之后，再打门里跨进场所，躲开变更之风最毒的锋头，享受一份挣来的疗养，分享我刚才念叨的这种种甜头，再由着这支天底下最血淋淋可爱的一小队浮士德和浮士德娜同伴们帮衬着，把你在宇宙里积攒的那些迷人感受，一层黑过一层地细细消化——那就是甜上加甜了！」

「哦，这日子是甜的，没错，可我要问你们——」说到这里，他的眼睛又飞快地把我们挨个扎了一遍——「我问的是，它把我们做成了什么。我说了，我一直在得到一幅全新的图景：我的生活曾经是什么样；倘若有过连我们魔鬼都做不出的那种改动，它本可以是什么样；以及它现在是什么样。我一直看着我们大家方才对种种事件的反应——对圣彼得堡的消息，对这位克里特军官讲得那样美的故事——只是她要讲的并不美——尤其对那口血淋淋的炸弹箱。而我只想问你们每一个人：你被做成什么样了？」

---

===Original===
He stopped his pacing and stuck his thumbs in his belt and seemed to be listening to the wheels turning in at least eleven other heads—only I stopped mine pretty quick, with Dave and Father and the Rape of Chicago coming up out of the dark on the turn and Mother and the Indiana Dunes and Jazz Limited just behind them, followed by the unthinkable thing the Spider doctor had flicked into existence when I flopped as a nurse, because I can’t stand that to be done to my mind by anybody but myself.

I stopped them by using the old infallible Entertainers’ gimmick, a fast survey of the most interesting topic there is—other people’s troubles.

===Chinese===
他停下踱步，两个拇指别进腰带，像在侧耳听至少另外十一颗脑袋里齿轮的转动——只是我那颗很快就刹了闸，因为戴夫、父亲，还有芝加哥浩劫，正从黑暗里随转盘转上来，母亲、印第安纳沙丘和「爵士特快」紧随其后，最后压轴的，是那位蜘蛛医生在我护士生涯砸锅时啪嗒一声变出来的那件不可想象之物——因为我没法容忍任何人这样摆弄我的脑子，除了我自己。

我用招待员那套百试百灵的老把戏刹住了它们：飞快检阅一遍天底下最有意思的话题——别人的麻烦。

---

===Original===
Offhand, Beau looked as if he had most troubles, shamed by his boss and his girl given her heart to a Soldier; he was hugging them to himself very quiet.

I didn’t stop for the two E.T.s—they’re too hard to figure—or for Doc; nobody can tell whether a fallen-down drunk’s at the black or bright end of his cycle; you just know it’s cycling.

Maud ought to be suffering as much as Beau, called names and caught out in a panic, which always hurts her because she’s plus three hundred years more future than the rest of us and figures she ought to be that much wiser, which she isn’t always—not to mention she’s over fifty years old, though her home-century cosmetic science keeps her looking and acting teenage most of the time. She’d backed away from the bronze chest so as not to stand out, and now Lili came from behind the piano and stood beside her.

Lili had the opposite of troubles, a great big glow for Bruce, proud as a promised princess watching her betrothed. Erich frowned when he saw her, for he seemed proud too, proud of the way his *Kamerad* had taken command of us panicky whacks Führer-fashion. Sid still looked mostly grateful and inclined to let Bruce keep on talking.

===Chinese===
粗粗一看，博的麻烦好像最多：被东家数落了，心头的那位姑娘又把心给了一个士兵；他把这些麻烦悄悄搂在怀里，不出一声。

两位 E.T. 那儿我没停——太难琢磨——大夫那儿也没停；一个烂醉如泥的人正处在他周期的黑端还是亮端，谁也说不上来；你只知道那周期在转。

莫德该跟博一样难受：挨了骂，还被当场抓包惊慌失措——这最伤她，因为她比我们大家都多出三百年未来，自觉该相应地高明几分，其实未必——更别提她已经五十开外，虽说她本乡世纪的化妆科学让她大多数时候看着、动跳着都像十几岁。她已经从青铜箱边退开，免得扎眼，这时莉莉从钢琴后走出来，站到了她身旁。

莉莉的处境正相反——不是麻烦，是为布鲁斯燃着的一大团光，骄傲得像一位订了婚的公主望着她的未婚夫。埃里希看见她时皱了皱眉，因为他似乎也骄傲，为他这位*Kamerad*（伙计）以元首做派镇住了我们这群慌了神的傻蛋而骄傲。西德看上去主要还是感激，倾向于让布鲁斯继续讲下去。

===Original===
Even Kaby and Mark, those two dragons hot for battle, standing a little in front and to one side of us by the bronze chest, like its guardians, seemed willing to listen. They made me realize one reason Sid had for letting Bruce run on, although the path his talk was leading us down was flashing with danger signals: When it was over, there’d still be the problem of what to do with the bomb, and a real opposition shaping up between Soldiers and Entertainers, and Sid was hoping a solution would turn up in the meantime or at least was willing to put off the evil day.

But beyond all that, and like the rest of us, I could tell from the way Sid was squinting his browy eyes and chewing his beardy lip that he was shaken and moved by what Bruce had said. This New Boy had dipped into our hearts and counted our kicks so beautifully, better than most of us could have done, and then somehow turned them around so that we had to think of what messes and heels and black sheep and lost lambs we were—well, we wanted to keep on listening.

===Chinese===
就连卡比和马克——那两条求战心切的蛟龙——站在青铜箱旁我们侧前方一点的地方，像它的守卫，似乎也愿意听。他们叫我明白了西德放任布鲁斯讲下去的一个理由，尽管他这套话正引我们走的那条小路，信号灯闪个不停：等他讲完，炸弹怎么办的问题依然在那儿，士兵和招待员之间一场货真价实的对立也正在成形，西德指望这中间能自己冒出个解决办法，或者至少，乐意把那不祥的日子往后拖一拖。

但撇开这一切，跟我们大家一样，我从西德眯起浓眉眼、咀嚼着多须下唇的样子看得出，他被布鲁斯的话撼动了。这位新小子探进我们心里，把我们的心跳数得那么漂亮，比我们多数人自己数得都好，然后不知怎么又把它们掉了个个儿，叫我们不得不想想自己是一群怎样的烂摊子、浑球、害群之马和迷途羔羊——嗯，我们想继续听下去。

## VIII A Place to Stand / 立足之地

===Original===
> Give me a place to stand, and I will move the world.

===Chinese===
> 给我一个立足之点，我便能撬动整个世界。（译注：阿基米德语。）

---

===Original===
Bruce’s voice had a faraway touch and he was looking up left at the Void as he said, “Have you ever really wondered why the two sides of this war are called the Snakes and the Spiders? Snakes may be clear enough—you always call the enemy something dirty. But Spiders—our name for ourselves? Bear with me, Ilhilihis; I know that no being is created dirty or malignant by Nature, but this is a matter of anthropoid feelings and folkways. Yes, Mark, I know that some of your legions have nicknames like the Drunken Lions and the Snails, and that’s about as insulting as calling the British Expeditionary Force the Old Contemptibles.

“No, you’d have to go to bands of vicious youths in cities slated for ruin to find a habit of naming like ours, and even they would try to brighten up the black a bit. But simply—Spiders. And Snakes, for that’s their name for themselves too, you know. Spiders and Snakes. What are our masters, that we give them names like that?”

===Chinese===
布鲁斯的嗓音带上了几分悠远，他朝左上方望着虚空说：「你们可曾认真琢磨过，这场战争的双方为什么叫蛇方和蜘蛛方？蛇也许好懂——敌人嘛，你总要给起个脏名字。可蜘蛛——咱们给自己起的名字？多担待，伊尔希利希斯；我知道没有哪种生灵是天生肮脏或恶毒的，可这是类人猿感情和风俗的事。是的，马克，我知道你的某些军团有『醉狮』『蜗牛』之类的绰号，可那顶多就像把英国远征军叫成『老可蔑们』（译注：Old Contemptibles，一战英军自豪的自称，源出德皇的蔑称），损不到哪儿去。

「不，要找像咱们这样的起名习惯，你得去找那些注定完蛋的城市里的流氓少年帮，而且就连他们也会想法子给这团黑抹点亮色。可咱们干脆就是——蜘蛛。还有蛇，要知道，那也是他们给自己起的名字。蜘蛛和蛇。咱们的老爷们究竟是什么东西，才配得上咱们给他们起这样的名字？」

===Original===
It gave me the shivers and set my mind working in a dozen directions and I couldn’t stop it, although it made the shivers worse.

Illy beside me now—I’d never given it a thought before, but he did have eight legs of a sort, and I remembered thinking of him as a spider monkey, and hadn’t the Lunans had wisdom and atomic power and a billion years in which to get the Change War rolling?

Or suppose, in the far future, Terra’s own spiders evolved intelligence and a cruel cannibal culture. They’d be able to keep their existence secret. I had no idea of who or what would be on Earth in Sevensee’s day, and wouldn’t it be perfect black hairy poisoned spider-mentality to spin webs secretly through the world of thought and all of space and time?

And Beau—wasn’t there something real Snaky about him, the way he moved and all?

Spiders and Snakes. *Spinne und Schlange*, as Erich called them. S. & S. But S.S. stood for the Nazi Schutzstaffel, the Black Shirts, and what if some of those cruel, crazy Jerries had discovered time travel and—I brought myself up with a jerk and asked myself, “Greta, how nuts can you get?”

===Chinese===
这话听得我脊背发凉，又逗得我的脑子朝十几个方向一齐开动，刹都刹不住，虽说这样凉得更厉害。

这会儿伊尔利就在我身边——我以前从没想过，可他确确实实长着某种意义上的八条腿，我又想起自己曾把他当作蜘蛛猴，再想想，月球人不正是既有智慧又有原子能，还有十亿年的工夫把变更战争发动起来的吗？

再或者，设想一下，在遥远的未来，地球自家的蜘蛛演化出了智力和一种残忍的同类相食的文化。他们完全能把自身存在保密。七观那个年代地球上会是谁、会是什么，我一无所知，而由一种黑色多毛的毒蜘蛛心计，悄悄在思想的世界和全部时空里结网——这不是天衣无缝吗？

还有博——他身上是不是颇有几分蛇劲儿，瞧他那个行走的做派？

蜘蛛和蛇。用埃里希的话说，*Spinne und Schlange*（蜘蛛与蛇）。S.&S.。可 S.S. 代表的是纳粹的党卫军（Schutzstaffel），那些黑衫队——万一那帮残忍疯癫的德国佬里有哪些发现了时间旅行，而且——我一个急刹把自己拽住，问自己：格蕾塔，你还能疯到什么份上？

---

===Original===
From where he was on the floor, the front of the bar his sounding board, Doc shrieked up at Bruce like one of the damned from the pit, “Don’t speak against the Spiders! Don’t blaspheme! They can hear the Unborn whisper. Others whip only the skin, but they whip the naked brain and heart,” and Erich called out, “That’s enough, Bruce!”

But Bruce didn’t spare him a look and said, “But whatever the Spiders are and no matter how much whip they use, it’s plain as the telltale on the Maintainer that the Change War is not only going against them, but getting away from them. Dwell for a bit on the current flurry of stupid slugging and panicky anachronism, when we all know that anachronism is what gets the Change Winds out of control. This punch-drunk pounding on the Cretan-Dorian fracas as if it were the only battle going and the only way to work things. Whisking Constantine from Britain to the Bosporus by rocket, sending a pocket submarine back to sail with the Armada against Drake’s woodensides—I’ll wager you hadn’t heard those! And now, to save Rome, an atomic bomb.

“Ye gods, they could have used Greek fire or even dynamite, but a fission weapon. … I leave you to imagine what gaps and scars that will make in what’s left of history—the smothering of Greece and the vanishment of Provence and the troubadours and the Papacy’s Irish Captivity won’t be in it!”

===Chinese===
大夫瘫坐在地板上，拿吧台正面当共鸣箱，像深渊里爬出的受诅咒者似的朝布鲁斯尖喊：「不许说蜘蛛的坏话！不许亵渎！他们听得见未生者的低语。别人只鞭打皮肉，他们鞭打的是赤裸的脑子和心。」埃里希喝道：「够了，布鲁斯！」

可布鲁斯没分给他一个眼色，说道：「可不管蜘蛛们是什么，也不管他们鞭子抡得多狠，有一点明白得就像维持器上的指示灯：变更战争不光在败走，还在脱出他们的掌心。且想想眼下这阵愚蠢互殴和慌乱穿越的旋风——咱们谁都知道，时代错乱正是让变更之风失控的祸根。这记醉拳式的死捶，砸在克里特对多利安这场混战上，仿佛它是唯一打着仗的战场、唯一办事的路数。火箭把君士坦丁从不列颠速递到博斯普鲁斯，袖珍潜艇倒回去随无敌舰队出航，对付德雷克的木头船——我敢打赌这两桩你们还没听说过！而现在，为了拯救罗马，一颗原子弹。

「众神啊，他们本可以用希腊火，甚至炸药，可偏偏是裂变武器。……那些缺口和伤疤将刻进历史的残余，就留给你们自己想象吧——希腊的窒息、普罗旺斯与行吟诗人的湮灭、教廷的『爱尔兰之囚』，还都排不上号呢！」

===Original===
The cut on his cheek had opened again and was oozing a little, but he didn’t pay any attention to it, and neither did we, as his lips thinned in irony and he said, “But I’m forgetting that this is a cosmic war and that the Spiders are conducting operations on billions, trillions of planets and inhabited gas clouds through millions of ages and that we’re just one little world—one little solar system, Sevensee—and we can hardly expect our inscrutable masters, with all their pressing preoccupations and far-flung responsibilities, to be especially understanding or tender in their treatment of our pet books and centuries, our favorite prophets and periods, or unduly concerned about preserving any of the trifles that we just happen to hold dear.

“Perhaps there are some sentimentalists who would rather die forever than go on living in a world without the *Summa*, the Field Equations, *Process and Reality*, *Hamlet*, Matthew, Keats, and the *Odyssey*, but our masters are practical creatures, ministering to the needs of those rugged souls who want to go on living no matter what.”

===Chinese===
他脸颊上的刀口又裂开了，渗着一点血，可他毫不在意，我们也没在意，只听他嘴唇讥诮地抿薄了，说：「可我忘了，这是场宇宙战争，蜘蛛们正在亿万颗行星、数以万亿计的宜居气体云上、贯穿千百万个纪元地指挥作战，而我们不过是一个小小世界——一个小小的太阳系，七观——我们实在不能指望咱们那些高深莫测的老爷，在他们千头万绪的操劳和遍地开花的职责之余，对咱们心爱的书册和世纪、咱们中意的先知和时代，表现出格外的体恤或温柔，也不好指望他们过分操心去保全任何一件咱们碰巧珍视的小玩意儿。

「也许有些多愁善感之辈，宁可永远死去，也不愿活在一个没有《大全》（*Summa*）、没有场方程、没有《过程与实在》（*Process and Reality*）、没有《哈姆雷特》（*Hamlet*）、没有《马太福音》、没有济慈、没有《奥德赛》（*Odyssey*）的世界里，可咱们的老爷是务实的生灵，他们侍奉的，是那些不管天塌地陷也要活下去的硬汉的需求。」

---

===Original===
Erich’s “Bruce, I’m telling you that’s enough,” was lost in the quickening flow of the New Boy’s words. “I won’t spend much time on the minor signs of our major crack-up—the canceling of leaves, the sharper shortages, the loss of the Express Room, the use of Recuperation Stations for ops and all the other frantic patchwork—last operation but one, we were saddled with three Soldiers from outside the Galaxy and, no fault of theirs, they were no earthly use. Such little things might happen at a bad spot in any war and are perhaps only local. But there’s a big thing.”

He paused again, to let us wonder, I guess. Maud must have worked her way over to me, for I felt her dry little hand on my arm and she whispered out of the side of her mouth, “What do we do now?”

“We listen,” I told her the same way. I felt a little impatient with her need to be doing something about things.

She cocked a gold-dusted eyebrow at me and murmured, “You, too?”

I didn’t get to ask her me, too, what? Crush on Bruce? Nuts!—because just then Bruce’s voice took up again in the faraway range.

===Chinese===
埃里希那句「布鲁斯，我告诉你，够了」，淹没在新小子越说越急的字流里。「大崩盘的小征兆，我不多费口舌——取消休假、短缺加剧、丢了特快室、拿疗养站当行动站使，还有其他种种穷于应付的补丁活——上上回行动，硬塞给我们三个河外来的士兵，他们本人没过错，可全无用处。这种小事哪场战争的烂摊子上都会出，也许只是局部现象。可有一桩大事。」

他又顿了顿，我猜是留给我们咂摸。莫德准是一路蹭到了我身边，因为我觉出她那只干瘦的小手搭上我胳膊，她从嘴角挤出一句耳语：「咱们现在怎么办？」

「咱们听着。」我以同样方式告诉她。她这种非要拿事情做点什么的劲头，叫我有点不耐烦。

她朝我扬起一道扑着金粉的眉毛，低声道：「你也？」

「我也什么？」我还没来得及问——迷上布鲁斯了？呸！——布鲁斯的嗓音就又带着那股悠远远远地响了起来。

===Original===
“Have you ever asked yourselves how many operations the fabric of history can stand before it’s all stitches, whether too much Change won’t one day wear out the past? And the present and the future, too, the whole bleeding business. Is the law of the Conservation of Reality any more than a thin hope given a long name, a prayer of theoreticians? Change Death is as certain as Heat Death, and far faster. Every operation leaves reality a bit cruder, a bit uglier, a bit more makeshift, and a whole lot less rich in those details and feelings that are our heritage, like the crude penciled sketch on canvas when you’ve stripped off the paint.

“If that goes on, won’t the cosmos collapse into an outline of itself, then nothing? How much thinning can reality stand, having more and more Doublegangers cut out of it? And there’s another thing about every operation—it wakes up the Zombies a little more, and as its Change Winds die, it leaves them a little more disturbed and nightmare-ridden and frazzled. Those of you who have been on operations in heavily worked-over temporal areas will know what I mean—that look they give you out of the sides of their eyes as if to say, ‘You again? For Christ’s sake, go away. We’re the dead. We’re the ones who don’t want to wake up, who don’t want to be Demons and hate to be Ghosts. Stop torturing us.’ ”

===Chinese===
「你们可曾问过自己：历史的织物还能经得起多少次行动，才变得满身尽是针脚？变更过了头，会不会有一天把过去磨穿？还有现在和未来，整桩血淋淋的事业。现实守恒定律，除了一个披着长名字的渺茫希望、一纸理论家的祷告，还能是什么？变更之死跟热寂一样笃定，而且快得多。每一次行动，都把现实磨得更粗粝一点、更丑陋一点、更将就一点，而在那些构成我们遗产的细节和情感上匮乏得多——就像你把颜料全刮掉之后，画布上只剩那张铅笔打的粗稿。

「照这么下去，宇宙难道不会塌缩成它自己的一副轮廓，然后归于乌有？现实被剜出越来越多的二重身，还能消瘦到几分？每次行动还有一桩——它把僵尸们再唤醒一点，待它的变更之风平息，它留给他们的，是更多一分的惶惑、噩梦和憔悴。去过被反复翻整的时间区行动的人，都懂我的意思——他们拿眼角瞟你的那一眼，仿佛在说：『又是你？看在基督份上，走开吧。我们是死人。我们是不想醒来的那些，不想当魔鬼，也恨当幽灵。别再折磨我们了。』」

---

===Original===
I looked around at the Ghostgirls; I couldn’t help it. They’d somehow got together on the control divan, facing us, their backs to the Maintainers. The Countess had dragged along the bottle of wine Erich had fetched her earlier and they were passing it back and forth. The Countess had a big rose splotch across the ruffled white lace of her blouse.

Bruce said, “There’ll come a day when all the Zombies and all the Unborn wake up and go crazy together and figuratively come marching at us in their numberless hordes, saying, ‘We’ve had enough.’ ”

===Chinese===
我朝幽灵少女们望去；忍不住。不知几时，她们已经聚到了控制躺椅上，面朝我们，背对维持器。伯爵夫人把埃里希早先给她取的那瓶酒也拖了过来，两人你一口我一口地传递着。伯爵夫人胸前白色褶边蕾丝上，洇开了一大朵玫瑰色酒渍。

布鲁斯说：「总有一天，全体僵尸和全体未生者会一齐醒来、一齐发疯，比喻意义上地，浩浩荡荡朝我们开过来，说：『我们受够了。』」

===Original===
But I didn’t turn back to Bruce right away. Phryne’s chiton had slipped off one shoulder and she and the Countess were sitting sagged forward, elbows on knees, legs spread—at least, as far as the Countess’s hobble skirt would let her—and swayed toward each other a little. They were still surprisingly solid, although they hadn’t had any personal attention for a half hour, and they were looking up over my head with half-shut eyes and they seemed, so help me, to be listening to what Bruce was saying and maybe hearing some of it.

“We make a careful distinction between Zombies and Unborn, between those troubled by our operations whose lifelines lie in the past and those whose lifelines lie in the future. But is there any distinction any longer? Can we tell the difference between the past and the future? Can we any longer locate the now, the real now of the cosmos? The Places have their own nows, the now of the Big Time we’re on, but that’s different and it’s not made for real living.

“The Spiders tell us that the real now is somewhere in the last half of the 20th Century, which means that several of us here are also alive in the cosmos, have lifelines along which the now is traveling. But do you swallow that story quite so easily, Ilhilihis, Sevensee? How does it strike the servants of the Triple Goddess? The Spiders of Octavian Rome? The Demons of Good Queen Bess? The gentlemen Zombies of the Greater South? Do the Unborn man the starships, Maud?

“The Spiders also tell us that, although the fog of battle makes the now hard to pin down precisely, it will return with the unconditional surrender of the Snakes and the establishment of cosmic peace, and roll on as majestically toward the future as before, quickening the continuum with its passage. Do you really believe that? Or do you believe, as I do, that we’ve used up all the future as well as the past, wasted it in premature experience, and that we’ve had the real now smudged out of existence, stolen from us forever, the precious now of true growth, the child-moment in which all life lies, the moment like a newborn baby that is the only home for hope there is?”

===Chinese===
可我没有立刻转回布鲁斯那边。弗里妮的希顿衣滑下一边肩膀，她和伯爵夫人瘫坐着前倾，手肘撑膝，双腿岔开——至少，岔到伯爵夫人那条窄摆裙容许的极限——彼此微微摇晃着凑向对方。她们竟然还出奇地成形，虽说已有半个钟头没人照管，她们正眯缝着眼望过我头顶，而且老天作证，她们仿佛在听布鲁斯说的话，没准还听进去了几句。

「我们细致地区分僵尸和未生者，区分那些被我们的行动搅扰、生命线卧在过去的人，和生命线卧在将来的人。可这分别还存在吗？我们还分得出过去和未来吗？我们还找得到『现在』吗——宇宙真正的现在？各场所有各自的现在，我们所在的大时光的现在，可那是另一码事，而且不是为真正的生活造的。

「蜘蛛们告诉我们，真正的现在位于 20 世纪后半叶的某处，这意味着在座有几位在宇宙里也还活着，生命线上正驶着那个现在。可这故事你们咽得这么顺溜吗，伊尔希利希斯？七观？三重女神的仆婢们怎么想？奥古斯都时代罗马的蜘蛛们？好女王贝丝治下的魔鬼们？大南方的绅士僵尸们？开星舰的是未生者吗，莫德？

「蜘蛛们还告诉我们：虽然战争的硝烟让『现在』难以精确定位，但随着蛇方无条件投降、宇宙和平确立，它会回来，会像从前一样庄严地滚向未来，以它的过境催动连续体。你们真信吗？还是跟我一样相信：我们把未来连同过去都挥霍尽了，糟蹋在早到的体验里；真正的现在已经被涂毁、被永远偷走了——那真正成长的宝贵现在，一切生命所卧的赤子时刻，那像新生婴儿一样的时刻，是希望唯一的家？」

---

===Original===
He let that start to sink in, then took a couple of quick steps and went on, his voice rising over Erich’s “Bruce, for the last time—” and seeming to pick up a note of hope from the very word he had used, “But although things look terrifyingly black, there remains a chance—the slimmest chance, but still a chance—of saving the cosmos from Change Death and restoring reality’s richness and giving the Ghosts good sleep and perhaps even regaining the real now. We have the means right at hand. What if the power of time traveling were used not for war and destruction, but for healing, for the mutual enrichment of the ages, for quiet communication and growth, in brief, to bring a peace message—”

But my little commandant is quite an actor himself and knows a wee bit about the principles of scene-stealing, and he was not going to let Bruce drown him out as if he were just another extra playing a Voice from the Mob. He darted across our front, between us and the bar, took a running leap, and landed bang on the bloody box of bomb.

===Chinese===
他任这话渗了一渗，然后疾走两步，接着讲，嗓门压过埃里希的「布鲁斯，我最后一次——」，还仿佛从他自己用过的那个字眼里拾起了一丝希望：「可尽管局面黑得吓人，机会仍然存在——最细的一线，但终究是机会——能把宇宙从变更之死里救回来，恢复现实的丰饶，让幽灵们睡个安稳觉，没准还能夺回真正的现在。手段就攥在咱们手边。倘若时间旅行的力量不用于战争和毁灭，而用于疗愈，用于各个时代互通有无，用于安静的交流与成长——简而言之，去传递一道和平讯息——」

可我那位小指挥官本人也是一把演戏的好手，对抢戏的原理略知一二，他可不打算任布鲁斯把自己盖成一条龙套，一句「群众的声音」。他窜过我们阵前，插进我们和吧台之间，助跑，起跳，砰地一声正落在那口血淋淋的炸弹箱上。

===Original===
A bit later, Maud was silently showing me the white ring above her elbow where I’d grabbed her and Illy was teasing a clutch of his tentacles out of my other hand and squeaking reproachfully, “Greta girl, don’t ever do that.”

Erich was standing on the chest and I noticed that his boots carefully straddled the circle of skulls, and I should have known anyway you could hardly push them in the right order by jumping on them, and he was pointing at Bruce and saying, “—and that means mutiny, my young sir. *Um Gottes willen*, Bruce, listen to me and step down before you say anything worse. I’m older than you, Bruce. Mark’s older. Trust in your *Kameraden*. Guide yourself by their knowledge.”

He had got my attention, but I had much rather have him black my eye.

===Chinese===
过了一会儿，莫德正不出声地给我看她肘弯上方那一圈白痕——我掐的——伊尔利正把一小把触手从我另一只手里轻轻抽出去，带着责备吱吱道：「格蕾塔姑娘，可别再这么干了。」

埃里希站在箱顶上，我注意到他的靴子小心地跨开在那圈骷髅两侧——再说我本来也该如何道，跳上去可没法按对顺序踩它们——他直指着布鲁斯说：「——而这就意味着叛变，我年轻的朋友。*Um Gottes willen*（看在上帝份上），布鲁斯，听我的，趁你还没说出更糟的话，先下来。我比你老，布鲁斯。马克也比你老。信你的*Kameraden*（战友们）。拿他们的见识当你的指南。」

他赢得了我的注意，可我宁可他把我眼眶打青。

===Original===
“You older than me?” Bruce was grinning. “When your twelve-years’ advantage was spent in soaking up the wisdom of a race of sadistic dreamers gone paranoid, in a world whose thought-stream had already been muddied by one total war? Mark older than me? When all his ideas and loyalties are those of a wolf pack of unimaginative sluggers two thousand years younger than I am? Either of you older because you have more of the killing cynicism that is all the wisdom the Change World ever gives you? Don’t make me laugh!

“I’m an Englishman, and I come from an epoch when total war was still a desecration and the flowers and buds of thoughts not yet whacked off or blighted. I’m a poet and poets are wiser than anyone because they’re the only people who have the guts to think and feel at the same time. Right, Sid? When I talk to all of you about a peace message, I want you to think about it concretely in terms of using the Places to bring help across the mountains of time when help is really needed, not to bring help that’s undeserved or knowledge that’s premature or contaminating, sometimes not to bring anything at all, but just to check with infinite tenderness and concern that everything’s safe and the glories of the universe unfolding as they were intended to—”

“Yes, you are a poet, Bruce,” Erich broke in. “You can tootle soulfully on the flute and make us drip tears. You can let out the stops on the big organ pipes and make us tremble as if at Jehovah’s footsteps. For the last twenty minutes, you have been giving us some very *charmante* poetry. But what are you? An Entertainer? Or are you a Soldier?”

===Chinese===
「你比我老？」布鲁斯咧着嘴笑。「你那十二年的优势，都花在吸收一个虐待狂兼梦游者、已然偏执成狂的种族的智慧上了，而且是在一个思想之流已被一场总体战搅浑的世界里？马克比我老？他全部的观念和忠诚，都来自一狼群的毫无想象力的莽汉，还比我年轻两千岁？你们俩哪一个老，是因为你们身上杀气腾腾的犬儒更重——而那正是变更世界肯给你们的全副智慧？别逗我笑了！

「我是英国人，来自一个总体战还算是亵渎、思想的花朵和蓓蕾还没被敲掉或冻伤的时代。我是诗人，而诗人比谁都智慧，因为他们是唯一有胆量同时思考和感受的人。对不对，西德？我对你们大家谈和平讯息的时候，我要你们具体地想：用各场所跨过时间的大山，送去真正需要的援手；不送去不该得的帮助，不送去早熟或带污染的知识；有时候干脆什么都不送，只带着无限的温柔和挂念去确认一下——一切都安好，宇宙的诸般荣光，正按它们本来的意图铺展——」

「不错，你是诗人，布鲁斯，」埃里希插进来。「你能对着长笛如泣如诉，吹得我们滴眼泪。你能放开大管风琴的全部音栓，震得我们发抖，仿佛听见了耶和华的脚步。过去这二十分钟，你一直在给我们朗诵非常*charmante*（动听）的诗。可你是干什么的？一个招待员？还是一名士兵？」

---

===Original===
Right then—I don’t know what it was, maybe Sid clearing his throat—I could sense our feelings beginning to turn against Bruce. I got the strangest feeling of reality clamping down and bright colors going dull and dreams vanishing. Yet it was only then I also realized how much Bruce had moved us, maybe some of us to the verge of mutiny, even. I was mad at Erich for what he was doing, but I couldn’t help admiring his cockiness.

I was still under the spell of Bruce’s words and the more-than-words behind them, but then Erich would shift around a bit and one of his heels would kick near the death’s-head pushbuttons and I wanted to stamp with spike heels on every death’s-head button on his uniform. I didn’t know exactly what I felt yet.

===Chinese===
就在那一刻——不知是什么，也许是西德清了清嗓子——我觉出我们的感情开始倒向布鲁斯的对立面。我得到一种最古怪的感觉：现实收紧了，鲜亮的颜色黯下去，梦消散了。可也正是在那一刻，我才意识到布鲁斯把我们打动到了什么份上，没准我们中的一些人已经被推到了叛变的悬崖边。我为埃里希的所作所为恨得牙痒，可又忍不住佩服他那股狂劲。

我还罩在布鲁斯的话语、以及话语背后那些超出话语的东西的符咒里，可埃里希偏偏要挪来挪去，一只靴跟踢过来踢过去，总不离那些骷髅按钮的边，我就想拿高跟钉靴，把他制服上每一粒骷髅扣子都踩个稀烂。我还没弄清自己到底是什么滋味。

===Original===
“Yes, I’m a Soldier,” Bruce told him, “and I hope you won’t ever have to worry about my courage, because it’s going to take more courage than any operation we’ve ever planned, ever dreamed of, to carry the peace message to the other Places and to the wound-spots of the cosmos. Perhaps it will be a fast wicket and we’ll be bowled down before we score a single run, but who cares? We may at least see our real masters when they come to smash us, and for me that will be a deep satisfaction. And we may do some smashing of our own.”

“So you’re a Soldier,” Erich said, his smile showing his teeth. “Bruce, I’ll admit that the half-dozen operations you’ve been on were rougher than anything I drew in my first hundred sleeps. For that, I am all honest sympathy. But that you should let them get you into such a state that love and a girl can turn you upside down and start you babbling about peace messages—”

“Yes, by God, love and a girl have changed me!” Bruce shouted at him, and I looked around at Lili and I remembered Dave saying, “I’m going to Spain,” and I wondered if anything would ever again make my face flame like that. “Or, rather, they’ve made me stand up for what I’ve believed in all along. They’ve made me—”

“*Wunderbar*,” Erich called and began to do a little sissy dance on the bomb that set my teeth on edge. He bent his wrists and elbows at arty angles and stuck out a hip and ducked his head simperingly and blinked his eyes very fast. “Will you invite me to the wedding, Bruce? You’ll have to get another best man, but I will be the flower girl and throw pretty little posies to all the distinguished guests. Here, Mark. Catch, Kaby. One for you, Greta. *Danke schön. Ach, zwei Herzen in dreivierteltakt … ta-ta … ta-ta … ta-ta-ta-ta-ta …*”

“What the hell do you think a woman is?” Bruce raged. “Something to mess around with in your spare time?”

===Chinese===
「对，我是士兵，」布鲁斯告诉他，「我希望你永远不必为我的勇气操心，因为把和平讯息送到其他场所、送到宇宙的伤口上，需要的勇气，比我们策划过的、梦想过的任何行动都多。也许那是个快球场（译注：板球术语，指球路刁钻、击球手极易出局），我们一分未得就被杀光，可谁在乎呢？至少，等咱们真正的老爷们来碾碎我们的时候，我们能亲眼见上一面，对我来说，那是深深的满足。再说，咱们自己也能碾碎点什么。」

「这么说你是士兵，」埃里希说，笑容露出牙齿。「布鲁斯，我承认，你经历过的那六次行动，比我头一百次入眠里抽到的哪一次都凶险。为这个，我满怀诚实的同情。可你竟任由它们把你折腾到这步田地，任凭一场恋爱加一个丫头把你倒提起来，开始胡诌什么和平讯息——」

「对，老天作证，一场恋爱和一个姑娘改变了我！」布鲁斯朝他吼，我望向莉莉，想起戴夫当年说：「我要去西班牙了。」我不知道往后还有什么能再让我的脸烧成那样。「或者不如说，是他们让我挺身站出来，捍卫我一向相信的东西。他们让我——」

「*Wunderbar*（妙极了），」埃里希喊道，随即在炸弹上跳起一小段娘娘腔的舞，看得我牙根发麻。他把手腕和手肘折出风雅的角度，一胯外撇，装模作样地一低头，眼睛眨得飞快。「婚礼会请我吗，布鲁斯？伴郎你只好另请高明，我可以当花童，给各位贵宾抛可爱的小花束。接着，马克。接好，卡比。这朵给你，格蕾塔。*Danke schön*（多谢）。啊，*zwei Herzen in dreivierteltakt*（两颗心，三拍子）……嗒-嗒……嗒-嗒……嗒-嗒-嗒-嗒-嗒……」

「你到底把女人当成什么？」布鲁斯怒不可遏。「当成你闲暇时搓两把的消遣？」

---

===Original===
Erich kept on humming “Two Hearts in Waltz Time”—and jigging around to it, damn him—but he slipped in a nod to Bruce and a “Precisely.” So I knew where I stood, but it was no news to me.

“Very well,” Bruce said, “let’s leave this Brown Shirt *maricón* to amuse himself and get down to business. I made all of you a proposal and I don’t have to tell you how serious it is or how serious Lili and I are about it. We not only must infiltrate and subvert other Places, which luckily for us are made for infiltration, we also must make contact with the Snakes and establish working relationships with their Demons at our level as one of our first steps.”

That stopped Erich’s jig and got enough of a gasp from some of us to make it seem to come from practically everybody. Erich used it to work a change of pace.

===Chinese===
埃里希接着哼《两颗心，三拍子》（Two Hearts in Waltz Time）——还跟着颠啊颠的，该死的——却顺势朝布鲁斯一点头、一句「正是」。于是我知道了自己的位置，不过这对我本不算新闻。

「很好，」布鲁斯说，「那就让这位棕衫*maricón*（西班牙语『娘娘腔』）自娱自乐，咱们谈正事。我向诸位提出了一项倡议，它有多严重、莉莉和我对此有多认真，不必我多说。我们不仅必须渗透并策反其他场所——所幸那些场所天生就为渗透而造——还必须同蛇方建立接触，同他们那个层级的魔鬼们搭起合作关系，作为我们的头几步之一。」

这一下掐停了埃里希的颠舞，也引得我们中间几个倒抽冷气，抽得简直像人人都在抽。埃里希借机换了个节奏。

===Original===
“Bruce! We’ve let you carry this foolery further than we should. You seem to have the idea that because anything goes in the Place—dueling, drunkenness, *und so weiter*—you can say what you have and it will all be forgotten with the hangover. Not so. It is true that among such a set of monsters and free spirits as ourselves, and working as secret agents to boot, there cannot be the obvious military discipline that would obtain in a Terran army.

“But let me tell you, Bruce, let me grind it home into you—Sid and Kaby and Mark will bear me out in this, as officers of equivalent rank—that the Spider line of command stretches into and through this Place just as surely as the word of *der Führer* rules Chicago. And as I shouldn’t have to emphasize to you, Bruce, the Spiders have punishments that would make my countrymen in Belsen and Buchenwald—well, pale a little. So while there is still a shadow of justification for our interpreting your remarks as utterly tasteless clowning—”

“Babble on,” Bruce said, giving him a loose downward wave of his hand without looking. “I made you people a proposal.” He paused. “How do you stand, Sidney Lessingham?”

===Chinese===
「布鲁斯！我们让你把这场蠢剧演得太过了。你似乎以为，因为场子里百无禁忌——决斗、酗酒，*und so weiter*（等等等等）——你就可以想说什么说什么，宿醉一过全都烟消云散。没那么便宜。诚然，在咱们这样一群怪物兼自由不羁之徒里，干的又是密探的营生，自然没有地球军队里那种明面上的军纪。

「可我告诉你，布鲁斯，让我把它碾进你脑子里——西德、卡比和马克会为我作证，作为同级军官——蜘蛛的指挥链照样伸进、穿过这个场所，就跟*der Führer*（元首）的号令统治着芝加哥一样确凿。而且无需我向你强调，布鲁斯，蜘蛛们的刑罚，会叫我在贝尔森和布痕瓦尔德的同胞——嗯，也黯然失色几分。所以，趁我们还有一丝理由把你的话解读成毫无品位的插科打诨——」

「接着絮叨吧，」布鲁斯说，看也不看，朝下松松一挥手。「我向诸位提出了一项倡议。」他顿了顿。「你什么立场，悉尼·莱辛厄姆？」

===Original===
Then I felt my legs getting weak, because Sid didn’t answer right away. The old boy swallowed and started to look around at the rest of us. Then the feeling of reality clamping down got something awful, because he didn’t look around, but straightened his back a little. Just then, Mark cut in fast.

“It grieves me, Bruce, but I think you are possessed. Erich, he must be confined.”

Kaby nodded, almost absently. “Confine or kill the coward, whichever is easier, whip the woman, and let’s get on to the Egyptian battle.”

“Indeed, yes,” Mark said. “I died in it. But now perhaps no longer.”

Kaby said to him, “I like you, Roman.”

Bruce was smiling, barely, and his eyes were moving and fixing. “You, Ilhilihis?”

===Chinese===
跟着我就觉得双腿发软，因为西德没有立刻回答。老爷子咽了口唾沫，开始环视我们其余的人。随后那种现实收紧的感觉变得可怕起来，因为他没有环视，而是把背脊挺直了一点。就在这时，马克飞快插进来。

「我很痛心，布鲁斯，可我认为你被附身了。埃里希，必须把他关起来。」

卡比点点头，几乎心不在焉。「关起来或者杀了这懦夫，哪样省事哪样，鞭那女人一顿，咱们好开拔去埃及的战场。」

「正是，」马克说。「我死在那场仗里。不过现在，也许不用死了。」

卡比对他说：「我喜欢你，罗马人。」

布鲁斯几乎不可察觉地微笑着，目光移动、定格。「你呢，伊尔希利希斯？」

===Original===
Illy’s squeak box had never sounded mechanical to me before, but it did as he answered, “I’m a lot deeper into borrowed time than the rest of you, tra-la-la, but Papa still loves living. Include me very much out, Brucie.”

“Miss Davies?”

Beside me, Maud said flatly, “Do you think I’m a fool?” Beyond her, I saw Lili and I thought, “My God, I might look as proud if I were in her shoes, but I sure as hell wouldn’t look as confident.”

Bruce’s eyes hadn’t quite come to Beau when the gambler spoke up. “I have no cause to like you, sir, rather the opposite. But this Place has come to bore me more than Boston and I have always found it difficult to resist a long shot. A very long one, I fear. I am with you, sir.”
