===Chinese===
伊尔利的吱吱匣以前在我听来从不带机器味，可他回答时带上了：「俺比你们大伙都更深地透支着光阴，啦啦啦，可老爹爹还是爱活着。把俺算成铁定的『不算俺』，布鲁奇。」（译注：include me out，仿「算我一个」故意说反，好莱坞制片人高德温的名口误。）

「戴维斯小姐？」

我身旁，莫德平板地说：「你当我是傻子吗？」再过去，我望见莉莉，心想：「天哪，我要是站在她的鞋里，也许也能那么骄傲，可我打死也露不出那副笃定。」

布鲁斯的目光还没真正落到博身上，这位赌徒先开了口：「我没有理由喜欢你，先生，毋宁说恰恰相反。可这场所已经闷得赛过波士顿，而我一向顶不住一注大冷门的诱惑。恐怕是天字号的大冷门。我入伙，先生。」

===Original===
There was a pain in my chest and a roaring in my ears and through it I heard Sevensee grunting, “—sicka these lousy Spiders. Deal me in.”

And then Doc reared up in front of the bar and he’d lost his hat and his hair was wild and he grabbed an empty fifth by the neck and broke the bottom of it all jagged against the bar and he waved it and screeched, “*Ubivaytye Pauki—i Nyemetzi!*”

And right behind his words, Beau sang out fast the English of it, “Kill the Spiders—and the Germans!”

And Doc didn’t collapse then, though I could see he was hanging onto the bar tight with his other hand, and the Place got stiller, inside and out, than I’ve ever known it, and Bruce’s eyes were finally moving back toward Sid.

===Chinese===
我胸口发疼，耳朵里嗡嗡直响，就在这嗡响中间，我听见七观哼道：「——受够了这帮臭蜘蛛。算俺一份。」

接着大夫从吧台前直蹿起来，帽子不知去向，头发蓬乱，他攥住一只空酒瓶的脖颈，朝吧台磕出满口锯齿，挥舞着它尖声嘶喊：「*Ubivaytye Pauki—i Nyemetzi!*（俄语：杀了蜘蛛——还有德国人！）」

紧跟着他的话音，博飞快用英语唱了出来：「杀了蜘蛛——还有德国人！」

大夫这一次没有瘫倒，虽然我看得出他另一只手死死抠着吧台，而场所，里里外外，静得超出了我认知的极限，布鲁斯的目光终于移回西德。

---

===Original===
But the eyes stopped short of Sid and I heard Bruce say, “Miss Forzane?” and I thought, “That’s funny,” and I started to look around at the Countess, and felt all the eyes and I realized, “Hey, that’s me! But this can’t happen to me. To the others, yes, but not to me. I just work here. Not to Greta, no, no, no!”

But it had, and the eyes didn’t let go, and the silence and the feeling of reality were Godawful, and I said to myself, “Greta, you’ve got to say something, if only a suitable four-letter word,” and then suddenly I knew what the silence was like. It was like that of a big city if there were some way of shutting off all the noise in one second. It was like Erich’s singing when the piano had deserted him. It was as if the Change Winds should ever die completely … and I knew beforehand what had happened when I turned my back on them all.

The Ghostgirls were gone. The Major Maintainer hadn’t merely been switched to Introvert. It was gone, too.

===Chinese===
可那目光在到西德之前刹住了，我听见布鲁斯说：「福尔赞小姐？」我心想：「真逗，」还开始转头去找伯爵夫人，随即感到了所有的目光，这才醒悟：「嘿，是我！可这不该轮到我啊。轮到别人，行，不该轮到我。我只是在这儿打工的。不该轮到格蕾塔，不，不，不！」

可它偏偏轮到了，目光也不松口，那寂静和现实收紧的感觉可怕至极，我对自己说：「格蕾塔，你得说点什么，哪怕一个合适的四字脏话，」随后我突然明白了这寂静像什么。它像一座大城市，假如有办法在一秒钟内掐灭它全部的噪音。它像埃里希的歌声，在钢琴弃他而去之后。它仿佛变更之风彻底停歇之后的世界……而我还没转身，就预感到了发生了什么。

幽灵少女们不见了。主维持器也不仅仅是被拨到了内闭档。它也不见了。

## IX A Locked Room / 密室

===Original===
> “We examined the moss between the bricks, and found it undisturbed.”
> “You looked among D——’s papers, of course, and into the books of the library?”
> “Certainly; we opened every package and parcel; we not only opened every book, but we turned over every leaf in each volume. …”

===Chinese===
> 「我们检查了砖缝间的苔藓，未见翻动。」
> 「你们当然也查过 D——的文件，翻过藏书室的书？」
> 「当然；每个包裹我们都拆了；不仅每本书都打开，而且每一卷的每一页都翻过。……」（译注：爱伦·坡《失窃的信》。）

---

===Original===
Three hours later, Sid and I plumped down on the couch nearest the kitchen, though too tired to want to eat for a while yet. A tighter search than I could ever have cooked up had shown that the Maintainer was not in the Place.

Of course it had to be in the Place, as we kept telling each other for the first two hours. It had to be, if circumstances and the theories we lived by in the Change World meant anything. A Maintainer is what maintains a Place. The Minor Maintainer takes care of oxygen, temperature, humidity, gravity, and other little life-cycle and matter-cycle things generally, but it’s the Major Maintainer that keeps the walls from buckling and the ceiling from falling in. It is little, but oh my, it does so much.

It doesn’t work by wires or radio or anything complicated like that. It just hooks into local space-time.

I have been told that its inside working part is made up of vastly tough, vastly hard giant molecules, each one of which is practically a vest-pocket cosmos in itself. Outside, it looks like a portable radio with a few more dials and some telltales and switches and plug-ins for earphones and a lot of other sensory thingumajigs.

But the Maintainer was gone and the Void hadn’t closed in, yet. By this time, I was so fagged, I didn’t care much whether it did or not.

One thing for sure, the Maintainer had been switched to Introvert before it was spirited away or else its disappearance automatically produced Introversion, take your choice, because we sure were Introverted—real nasty martinet-schoolmaster grip of reality on my thoughts that I knew, without trying, liquor wouldn’t soften, not a breath of Change Wind, absolutely stifling, and the gray of the Void seeming so much inside my head that I think I got a glimmering of what the science boys mean when they explain to me that the Place is a kind of interweaving of the material and the mental—a Giant Monad, one of them called it.

Anyway, I said to myself, “Greta, if this is Introversion, I want no part of it. It is not nice to be cut adrift from the cosmos and know it. A lifeboat in the middle of the Pacific and a starship between galaxies are not in it for loneliness.”

===Chinese===
三个钟头后，西德和我一屁股瘫进离厨房最近的那张躺椅，累得一时半会儿还不想吃东西。一场比我能想出来的更严丝合缝的搜查已经证明：维持器不在场所里。

当然，它必须在场所里——头两个钟头我们一直这么互相打气。它必须在，倘若环境和我们在变更世界里赖以生存的那套理论还算数的话。维持器就是维持一处场所的东西。副维持器照管氧气、温度、湿度、重力和别的什么生命循环、物质循环的小杂务，可撑住墙壁不塌、顶棚不落的是主维持器。它个头小，可天哪，它干的营生真不少。

它不靠电线、无线电或任何那类复杂玩意儿工作。它就是挂进本地的时空里。

听人讲过，它内部的工作部分由坚韧无比、坚硬无比的巨型分子构成，每一个本身 practically 就是一座马甲口袋里的宇宙。外面看，它像一台手提收音机，多几个旋钮，带些指示灯、开关、耳机插孔，还有一堆别的感觉性小零碎。

可维持器没了，虚空却还没合围上来。到这会儿，我已经累瘫了，合不上合不上都懒得计较。

有一件事板上钉钉：维持器是在被偷走之前就被拨到了内闭档，要么就是它的消失自动造成了内闭，随你挑哪样，因为我们确确实实被内闭了——现实攥住我的念头，像顶顶讨厌的严师铁腕，我不用试也知道，酒泡不软它；一丝变更之风都没有，闷得人窒息；虚空的灰又那么深地钻进我脑袋里，以至于我想我隐约摸到了科学小子们给我讲解时的意思：场所是物质与心灵的一种交织——有位管它叫「巨型单子」（Giant Monad）的。

总之，我对自己说：「格蕾塔，如果这就是内闭，我可不领教。被从宇宙上砍断漂走，还心知肚明，这滋味不好受。论孤独，太平洋正中的一条救生艇、星系之间的一艘星舰，都还排不上号。」

---

===Original===
I asked myself why the Spiders had ever equipped Maintainers with Introversion switches anyway, when we couldn’t drill with them and weren’t supposed to use them except in an emergency so tight that it was either Introvert or surrender to the Snakes, and for the first time the obvious explanation came to me:

Introversion must be the same as scuttling, its main purpose to withhold secrets and materiel from the enemy. It put a place into a situation from which even the Spider high command couldn’t rescue it, and there was nothing left but to sink down, down (out? up?), down into the Void.

If that was the case, our chances of getting back were about those of my being a kid again playing in the Dunes on the Small Time.

I edged a little closer to Sid and sort of squunched under his shoulder and rubbed my cheek against the smudged, gold-worked gray velvet. He looked down and I said, “A long way to Lynn Regis, eh, Siddy?”

“Sweetling, thou spokest a mouthful,” he said. He knows very well what he is doing when he mixes his language that way, the wicked old darling.

“Siddy,” I said, “why this gold-work? It’d be a lot smoother without it.”

“Marry, men must prick themselves out and, ’faith I know not, but it helps if there’s metal in it.”

“And girls get scratched.” I took a little sniff. “But don’t put this doublet through the cleaner yet. Until we get out of the woods, I want as much you around as possible.”

“Marry, and why should I?” he asked blankly, and I think he wasn’t fooling me. The last thing time travelers find out is how they do or don’t smell. Then his face clouded and he looked as though he wanted to squunch under my shoulder. “But ’faith, sweetling, your forest has a few more trees than Sherwood.”

“Thou saidst it,” I agreed, and wondered about the look. He oughtn’t to be interested in my girlishness now. I knew I was a mess, but he had stuck pretty close to me during the hunt and you never can tell. Then I remembered that he was the other one who hadn’t declared himself when Bruce was putting it to us, and it probably troubled his male vanity. Not me, though—I was still grateful to the Maintainer for getting me out of that spot, whatever other it had got us all into. It seemed ages ago.

===Chinese===
我纳闷蜘蛛们到底为什么给维持器装内闭拨杆——咱们又没法拿它操练，除了在「要么内闭、要么向蛇方投降」的死急关头，本也不许用它——头一回，那个明摆着的解释冒了出来：

内闭必定等同于凿船自沉，主要目的是不让敌人捞到机密和军资。它把一处场所放进连蜘蛛最高统帅都救不出来的境地，剩下的只有往下沉，往下沉（往外？往上？），沉进虚空。

若是那样，我们回去的指望，约等于我变回小孩子，在寻常时光里到沙丘去玩耍。

我朝西德挪近了些，半缩进他肩膀底下，脸颊蹭着他那件蹭脏了的绣金灰丝绒。他低头看，我说：「离雷吉斯林恩老远了吧，呃，西迪？」

「小甜心，你这话说到点子上了。」他说。他那样文白夹杂的时候，自己心里清楚得很，这个老坏蛋宝贝。

「西迪，」我说，「干嘛绣这些金线？不绣它光溜多了。」

「哎呀，男人总得把自己装点起来，说真的我不懂个中道理，反正带点金属就顶事。」

「姑娘家就挨刮喽。」我轻轻嗅了嗅。「不过这件上衣先别送去洗。在咱们走出林子之前，我要你身上的味儿越多越好。」

「哎呀，我为什么要送洗？」他茫然地问，我想他不是逗我。时间旅行者最后才弄明白的事，就是自己身上有没有味儿。随后他的脸阴下来，样子像是想缩到我肩膀底下来了。「不过说真的，小甜心，你这片林子，比舍伍德森林还多出好几棵树呢。」

「可不是嘛。」我附和着，心里琢磨他那个眼神。他现在不该对我的姑娘气感兴趣。我知道自己一副狼狈相，可搜查当中他一直跟我贴得挺近，这种事说不准。随后我想起来，布鲁斯挨个问立场时，他是另一个没表态的人，这多半硌了他的男性自尊。我倒不——我还感激维持器把我从那一关里捞了出来呢，不管它把我们全体拖进了什么别的境地。那已经是上辈子的事了。

---

===Original===
We’d all jumped to the conclusion that the two Ghostgirls had run away with the Maintainer, I don’t know where or why, but it looked so much that way. Maud had started yipping about how she’d never trusted Ghosts and always known that some day they’d start doing things on their own, and Kaby had got it firmly fixed in her head, right between the horns, that Phryne, being a Greek, was the ringleader and was going to wreak havoc on us all.

But when we were checking Stores the first time, I had noticed that the Ghostgirl envelopes looked flat. Ectoplasm doesn’t take up much space when it’s folded, but I had opened one anyway, then another, and then called for help.

Every last envelope was empty. We had lost over a thousand Ghostgirls, Sid’s whole stock.

Well, at least it proved what none of us had ever seen or heard of being demonstrated: that there is a spooky link—a sort of Change Wind contact—between a Ghost and its lifeline; and when that umbilicus, I’ve heard it called, is cut, the part away from the lifeline dies.

Interesting, but what had bothered me was whether we Demons were going to evaporate too, because we are as much Doublegangers as the Ghosts and our apron strings had been cut just as surely. We’re more solid, of course, but that would only mean we’d take a little longer. Very logical.

I remember I had looked up at Lili and Maud—us girls had been checking the envelopes; it’s one of the proprieties we frequently maintain and anyway, if men check them, they’re apt to trot out that old wheeze about “instant women” which I’m sick to death of hearing, thank you.

Anyway, I had looked up and said, “It’s been nice knowing you,” and Lili had said, “Twenty-three, skiddoo,” and Maud had said, “Here goes nothing,” and we had shook hands all around.

We figured that Phryne and the Countess had faded at the same time as the other Ghostgirls, but an idea had been nibbling at me and I said, “Siddy, do you suppose it’s just barely possible that, while we were all looking at Bruce, those two Ghostgirls would have been able to work the Maintainer and get a Door and lam out of here with the thing?”

“Thou speakst my thoughts, sweetling. All weighs against it: *Imprimis*, ’tis well known that Ghosts cannot lay plots or act on them. *Secundo*, the time forbade getting a Door. *Tercio*—and here’s the real meat of it—the Place folds without the Maintainer. *Quadro*, ’twere folly to depend on not one of—how many of us? ten, *elf*—not looking around in all the time it would have taken them—”

“I looked around once, Siddy. They were drinking and they had got to the control divan under their own power. Now when was that? Oh, yes, when Bruce was talking about Zombies.”

“Yes, sweetling. And as I was about to cap my argument with *quinquo* when you ’gan prattle, I could have sworn none could touch the Maintainer, much less work it and purloin it, without my certain knowledge. Yet …”

“Eftsoons yet,” I seconded him.

===Chinese===
我们全都一跃而下结论：是那两个幽灵少女卷着维持器跑了，跑去了哪儿、为什么跑，我不知道，可表面证据实在太像。莫德已经开嚷，说她从来信不过幽灵，早知道有朝一日她们会自作主张；卡比则把它牢牢钉进了脑子里——就钉在两只角中间——认定弗里妮既然是希腊人，就是首恶，正要对我们大施毒手。

可头一回清点库房区时，我就注意到那些幽灵少女的封套看着瘪。灵质折起来本占不了多大地方，可我还是拆开了一只，又一只，然后喊了人。

每一只封套都是空的。我们损失了上千名幽灵少女，西德的全部存货。

好吧，至少它证明了一件我们谁也没见过、也没听说谁演示过的事：幽灵与它的生命线之间存在一种幽秘的联系——一种变更之风式的接触；而这根脐带——我听人这么叫过——一旦剪断，离开生命线的那一半就死了。

有意思，可更让我坐不住的是：我们魔鬼会不会也蒸发掉，因为我们和幽灵同样是二重身，我们的围裙带也一样被剪得干干净净。我们当然更结实些，可那只意味着我们多撑一小会儿。逻辑严密。

我记得我当时抬头看了看莉莉和莫德——查封套的是我们几个姑娘家的活儿；这是我们常守的一条规矩，再说，男人查这种东西，动不动就搬出那句「速成女人」的老掉牙笑话，那玩意儿我听得耳朵起茧，谢谢。

总之，我当时抬起头说：「认识各位，三生有幸。」莉莉说：「二十三，走你。」（译注：23, skiddoo，二十世纪初芝加哥俚语，「快散了吧」。）莫德说：「成不成的，就这么着了。」我们围成一圈握了手。

我们推测弗里妮和伯爵夫人同其他幽灵少女一道消散了，可有个念头一直啃我，我说：「西迪，你说有没有万分之一的可能：咱们都盯着布鲁斯的时候，那两个幽灵少女摆弄得动维持器，开出一扇门，卷着那玩意儿溜之大吉？」

「你说到我心里去了，小甜心。可条条都否：*Imprimis*（其一），幽灵不会设谋、也不会行谋，此乃尽人皆知。*Secundo*（其二），当时的时辰不容开出门。*Tercio*（其三）——这才是真章——没有维持器，场所就塌。*Quadro*（其四），指望我们当中没有一个——咱们是多少人来着？十个，*elf*（德语『十一』）——在她们得手所需的全过程中不回头看一眼，那是犯傻——」

「我回头看了一眼，西迪。她们在喝酒，而且是自己个儿挪到控制躺椅上去的。那是什么时候来着？哦，对，布鲁斯讲僵尸那会儿。」

「正是，小甜心。你开腔岔掉的那会儿，我正要用*quinquo*（其五）给我的论证封顶：我敢起誓，谁也别想碰维持器，更别说摆弄它、偷走它，还能瞒过我确切的眼线。然而……」

「然也，且待须臾。」我用同款古腔接了他一句。

---

===Original===
Somebody must have got a door and walked out with the thing. It certainly wasn’t in the Place. The hunt had been a lulu. Something the size of a portable typewriter is not easy to hide and we had been inside everything from Beau’s piano to the renewer link of the Refresher.

We had even fluoroscoped everybody, though it had made Illy writhe like a box of worms, as he’d warned us; he said it tickled terribly and I insisted on smoothing his fur for five minutes afterward, although he was a little standoffish toward me.

Some areas, like the bar, kitchen and Stores, took a long while, but we were thorough. Kaby helped Doc check Surgery: since she last made the Place, she has been stationed in a Field Hospital (it turns out the Spiders actually are mounting operations from them) and learned a few nice new wrinkles.

However, Doc put in some honest work on his own, though, of course, every check was observed by at least three people, not including Bruce or Lili. When the Maintainer vanished, Doc had pulled out of his glassy-eyed drunk in a way that would have surprised me if I hadn’t seen it happen to him before, but when we finished Surgery and got on to the Art Gallery, he had started to putter and I noticed him hold out his coat and duck his head and whip out a flask and take a swig and by now he was well on his way toward another peak.

===Chinese===
总有人开过一扇门，揣着那玩意儿走了出去。它肯定不在场所里。这场大搜捕盛况空前。一台手提打字机大小的东西不好藏，我们翻遍了所有物件，从博的钢琴到盥洗区的更新链环，无一漏网。

我们甚至给每个人都做了透视，虽说那玩意儿害得伊尔利像一盒蚯蚓似的拧来拧去——他早警告过我们；他说痒得要命，事后我坚持给他顺了五分钟的毛，尽管他对我有点敬而远之。

有些区域，比如吧台、厨房和库房区，花了好一阵，但我们一丝不苟。卡比帮大夫查了手术区：自打她上回来场所，她被派驻在一座野战医院（看来蜘蛛们还真在从野战医院发起行动），学了几手漂亮的新门道。

不过，大夫自己也下了几分实在的功夫，当然，每一项检查都至少有三个人盯着，布鲁斯和莉莉不计在内。维持器消失那会儿，大夫从那种眼神发直的烂醉里拔了出来，那架势本该吓我一跳的，若不是我从前见过——可等我们查完手术区、转到艺术品廊，他又开始磨磨蹭蹭，我看见他抖开衣襟、一低头、唰地摸出一只小酒瓶抿了一大口，到这会儿，他已经在向下一座醉峰稳稳进发了。

===Original===
The Art Gallery had taken time too, because there’s such a jumble of strange stuff, and it broke my heart but Kaby took her ax and split a beautiful blue woodcarving of a Venusian medusa because, although there wasn’t a mark in the paw-polished surface, she claimed it was just big enough.

After we’d finished everything else, Mark had insisted on tackling the floor. Beau and Sid both tried to explain to him how this is a one-sided Place, that there is nothing, but nothing, under the floor; it just gets a lot harder than the diamonds crusting it as soon as you get a quarter inch down—that being the solid equivalent of the Void. But Mark was knuckle-headed (like all Romans, Sid assured me on the Q.T.) and broke four diamond-plus drills before he was satisfied.

Except for some trick hiding places, that left the Void, and things don’t vanish if you throw them at the Void—they half melt and freeze forever unless you can fish them out. Back of the Refresher, at about eye-level, are three Venusian coconuts that a Hittite strongman threw there during a major brawl. I try not to look at them because they are so much like witch heads they give me the woolies. The parts of the Place right up against the Void have strange spatial properties which one of the gadgets in Surgery makes use of in a way that gives me the worse woolies, but that’s beside the point.

===Chinese===
艺术品廊也耗了工夫，因为那里怪东西堆积如山，而且——真叫我心碎——卡比抡起她的斧头，劈开了一座美丽的金星水母蓝木雕，因为虽说那件被爪子磨得光溜溜的表面连道印子都没有，她一口咬定它大小正合适。

其余全查完之后，马克坚持要攻坚地板。博和西德都试着向他解释：这场所是单面的，地板底下什么都没有，绝对什么都没有；只要你往下钻进四分之一英寸，它就变得比罩在外面的钻石硬得多——那正是虚空的固态等价物。可马克是个死脑筋（跟所有罗马人一样，西德私下跟我说的），折断了四根加强钻石钻头才罢休。

除掉几处机关藏点，剩下的就只有虚空了，而朝虚空扔东西它们不会消失——只会半熔半冻地钉在那儿，除非你捞得回来。盥洗区背后、约莫齐眼的高度，嵌着三颗金星椰子，是一位赫梯大力士在一场大型混战里扔的。我尽量不看它们，因为它们太像巫婆头，看得我发毛。场所紧贴虚空的那些部分有着古怪的空间属性，手术区有件装置就利用了它，利用的方式让我起更大的鸡皮疙瘩，不过这跑题了。

---

===Original===
During the hunt, Kaby and Erich had used their Callers as direction finders to point out the Maintainer, just as they’re used in the cosmos to locate the Door—and sometimes in the Big Places, people tell me. But the Callers only went wild—like a compass needle whirling around without stopping—and nobody knew what that meant.

The trick hiding places were the Minor Maintainer, a cute idea, but it is no bigger than the Major and has its own mysterious insides and had obviously kept on doing its own work, so that was out for several reasons, and the bomb chest, though it seemed impossible for anyone to have opened it, granting they knew the secret of its lock, even before Erich jumped on it and put it in the limelight double. But when you’ve ruled out everything else, the word impossible changes meaning.

Since time travel is our business, a person might think of all sorts of tricks for sending the Maintainer into the past or future, permanently or temporarily. But the Place is strictly on the Big Time and everybody that should know tells me that time traveling *through* the Big Time is out. It’s this way: the Big Time is a train, and the Little Time is the countryside and we’re on the train, unless we go out a Door, and as Gertie Stein might put it, you can’t time travel through the time you time travel in when you time travel.

I’d also played around with the idea of some fantastically obvious hiding place, maybe something that several people could pass back and forth between them, which would mean a conspiracy, and, of course, if you assume a big enough conspiracy, you can explain anything, including the cosmos itself. Still, I’d got a sort of shell-game idea about the Soldiers’ three big black shakos and I hadn’t been satisfied until I’d got the three together and looked in them all at the same time.

===Chinese===
搜查期间，卡比和埃里希把呼叫器当测向仪用，想指出维持器的方位，就像在宇宙里用它定位门一样——据说在大型场所有时也这么使。可呼叫器只顾发疯——像一根停不下来打转的罗盘针——没人知道那意味着什么。

机关藏点，一是副维持器——点子挺俏皮，可它不比主维持器大，自有一副神秘的内脏，而且明摆着一直在干自己的活，所以出于好几层理由被排除；二是那口炸弹箱——虽说就算有人知道锁的机关，也似乎不可能打开它，更别提埃里希还跳上去踩过，让它双倍地处于聚光灯下。可当你把其余一切排除干净，「不可能」这个词就得换换意思了。

既然时间旅行是咱们的本行，人也许能想出种种把维持器永久或暂时送往过去未来的花招。可场所是板上钉钉地在大时光里，而该知道的行家都告诉我：在大时光*内部*进行时间旅行，免谈。是这么个道理：大时光是一列火车，小时光是窗外的乡野，咱们在车上，除非跨出门去；而照格蒂·斯泰因的做派说，你没法在你进行时间旅行的那段时间里，穿行你进行时间旅行所经过的时间，当你进行时间旅行的时候。（译注：仿格特鲁德·斯泰因「玫瑰就是玫瑰就是玫瑰」的绕口句式。）

我还把玩过一个念头：某个离谱到扎眼的藏点，没准是件可以在几个人手里倒来倒去的东西，那就意味着有同谋——当然，只要你假设的阴谋够大，你什么都能解释，包括宇宙本身。尽管如此，我还是对着士兵们那三顶大黑筒帽起了个三仙归洞的疑心，直到把三顶凑到一块儿、同时往里瞧过，才算安心。

===Original===
“Wake up, Greta, and take something. I can’t stand here forever.” Maud had brought us a tray of hearty snacks from then and yon, and I must say they were tempting; she whips up a mean hors d’oeuvre.

I looked them over and said, “Siddy, I want a hot dog.”

“And I want a venison pasty! Out upon you, you finical jill, you o’erscrupulous jade, you whimsic and tyrannous poppet!”

I grabbed a handful and snuggled back against him.

“Go on, call me some more, Siddy,” I told him. “Real juicy ones.”

===Chinese===
「醒醒，格蕾塔，吃点东西。我不能在这儿站到地老天荒。」莫德给我们端来一盘东拼西凑的丰盛点心，我必须说，相当诱人；她那手开胃小食，够辣够味。

我把它们检阅了一遍，说：「西迪，我要一只热狗。」

「我还想要鹿肉馅饼呢！去你的，你这挑剔的小娇娘，你这过分讲究的辣妹子，你这任性又霸道的小人儿！」

我抓了一把，偎回他身上。

「接着骂，西迪，再骂我几句，」我告诉他。「要够味的。」

## X Motives and Opportunities / 动机与机会

===Original===
> My thought, whose murder yet is but fantastical,
 

 Shakes so my single state of man that function
 

 Is smother’d in surmise, and nothing is
 

 But what is not.

===Chinese===
> 我这念头，杀意尚且只是空中楼阁，
> 却已震动我孑然一身，
> 使官能窒毙于玄想，而万物
> 皆成子虚。（译注：莎士比亚《麦克白》。）

---

===Original===
My big bad waif from King’s Lynn had set the tray on his knees and started to wolf the food down. The others were finishing up. Erich, Mark and Kaby were having a quietly furious argument I couldn’t overhear at the end of the bar nearest the bronze chest, and Illy was draped over the piano like a real octopus, listening in.

Beau and Sevensee were pacing up and down near the control divan and throwing each other a word now and then. Beyond them, Bruce and Lili were sitting on the opposite couch from us, talking earnestly about something. Maud had sat down at the other end of the bar and was knitting—it’s one of the habits like chess and quiet drinking, or learning to talk by squeak box, that we pick up to pass the time in the Place in the long stretches between parties. Doc was fiddling around the Gallery, picking things up and setting them down, still managing to stay on his feet at any rate.

===Chinese===
我那位来自金斯林的大号流浪坏小子已经把托盘搁上膝盖，开始狼吞虎咽。其他人也在收尾。埃里希、马克和卡比在离青铜箱顶近的那头吧台边进行一场安静而狂怒的争论，我听不真切，伊尔利则像只正经章鱼似的趴在钢琴上旁听。

博和七观在控制躺椅附近来回踱步，时不时朝对方抛一个词。再过去，布鲁斯和莉莉坐在我们对面的躺椅上，正就什么事谈得恳切。莫德在吧台另一头坐下，织起了毛线——这属于我们为打发派对之间漫长空白而在场所里养成的习惯，同类还有下棋、静饮，或者学用吱吱匣说话。大夫在艺术品廊里东摸西摸，拿起这件放下那件，好歹还站稳着没倒。

---

===Original===
Lili and Bruce stood up, still gabbing intensely at each other, and Illy began to pick out with one tentacle a little tune in the high keys that didn’t sound like anything on God’s earth. “Where do they get all the energy?” I wondered.

As soon as I asked myself that, I knew the answer and I began to feel the same way myself. It wasn’t energy; it was nerves, pure and simple.

Change is like a drug, I realized—you get used to the facts never staying the same, and one picture of the past and future dissolving into another maybe not very different but still different, and your mind being constantly goosed by strange moods and notions, like nightclub lights of shifting color with weird shadows between shining right on your brain.

The endless swaying and jogging is restful, like riding on a train.

You soon get to like the movement and to need it without knowing, and when it suddenly stops and you’re just you and the facts you think from and feel from are exactly the same when you go back to them—boy, that’s rough, as I found out now.

The instant we got Introverted, everything that ordinarily leaks into the Place, wake or sleep, had stopped coming, and we were nothing but ourselves and what we meant to each other and what we could make of that, an awfully lonely, scratchy situation.

I decided I felt like I’d been dropped into a swimming pool full of cement and held under until it hardened.

I could understand the others bouncing around a bit. It was a wonder they didn’t hit the Void. Maud seemed to be standing it the best; maybe she’d got a little preparation from the long watches between stars; and then she is older than all of us, even Sid, though with a small *o* in “older.”

===Chinese===
莉莉和布鲁斯站起身，还在激烈地咬耳朵，伊尔利开始用一条触手在高音键上戳一支小曲，那调子在人间的任何地方都听不着。「他们哪来这么多劲儿？」我纳闷。

这问题一出口给自己，我就知道了答案，而且我自己也开始有同感。那不是劲儿；是神经，纯粹又简单。

变更就像毒品，我意识到——你习惯了事实从不安分，习惯了过去和未来的一幅画面溶解进另一幅，也许差别不大，终究不同，习惯了你的脑子不断被怪异的情绪和念头戳一下戳一下，就像夜总会那些变色的灯，灯与灯之间还夹着怪影，直射在你的脑髓上。

那无休止的摇晃颠簸是安神的，像坐火车。

你很快喜欢上这种运动，不知不觉地离不开它，而当它戛然而止，你只剩下你，你据以思考、据以感受的那些事实，回头再去翻竟一模一样——小子，这滋味可真够呛，我这算是领教了。

被内闭的那一瞬，平常不论醒睡都会渗进场所来的一切，都断了供，我们只剩下我们自己、只剩下我们彼此的意义、只剩下我们还能从这上头做出什么——一种孤独得要命、扎得慌的处境。

我认定，自己的感觉就像被人丢进一池水泥，按在水底直到它凝固。

别人多少蹦跶两下，我能理解。他们没一头撞上虚空已是奇迹。莫德似乎挺得最稳；没准她在星际间漫长守望的日子里预过习；再者她比我们所有人都老，连西德也不例外——虽说那个「老」得记成*小写*的。

---

===Original===
The restless work of the search for the Maintainer had masked the feeling, but now it was beginning to come full force. Before the search, Bruce’s speech and Erich’s interruptions had done a passable masking job too. I tried to remember when I’d first got the feeling and decided it was after Erich had jumped on the bomb, about the time he mentioned poetry. Though I couldn’t be sure. Maybe the Maintainer had been Introverted even earlier, when I’d turned to look at the Ghostgirls. I wouldn’t have known. Nuts!

Believe me, I could feel that hardened cement on every inch of me. I remembered Bruce’s beautiful picture of a universe without Big Change and decided it was about the worst idea going. I went on eating, though I wasn’t so sure now it was a good idea to keep myself strong.

“Does the Maintainer have an Introversion telltale? Siddy!”

“ ’Sdeath, chit, and you love me, speak lower. Of a sudden, I feel not well, as if I’d drunk a butt of Rhenish and slept inside it. Marry yes, blue. In short flashes, saith the manual. Why ask’st thou?”

“No reason. God, Siddy, what I’d give for a breath of Change Wind.”

“Thou can’st say that eftsoons,” he groaned. I must have looked pretty miserable myself, for he put his arm around my shoulders and whispered gruffly, “Comfort thyself, sweetling, that while we suffer thus sorely, we yet cannot die the Change Death.”

“What’s that?” I asked him.

I didn’t want to bounce around like the others. I had a suspicion I’d carry it too far. So, to keep myself from going batty, I started to rework the business of who had done what to the Maintainer.

===Chinese===
搜寻维持器那阵无休止的忙碌掩盖过这感觉，可现在它开始全力回涌。搜寻之前，布鲁斯的演说和埃里希的搅局也曾凑合遮过一阵。我试着回想这感觉头一回冒头是什么时候，断定是在埃里希跳上炸弹之后，大约他提到诗的那当口。虽说也拿不准。没准维持器被内闭得更早，在我回头去看幽灵少女那会儿。我根本无从知晓。见鬼！

相信我，那层凝固的水泥，我寸寸肌肤都感觉得到。我想起布鲁斯描绘的那幅没有大变更的宇宙美图，断定这大约是眼下最烂的主意。我继续吃，虽说这时候我已经不太确定，把自己养壮实是不是好主意了。

「维持器有没有内闭指示灯？西迪！」

「皇天啊，小丫头，你要还爱着我，就小点声。我突然觉得不爽利，像灌下一大桶莱茵酒、又睡在酒桶里过了一夜。哎呀，有的，蓝色的。手册上说，只短短地闪。你问这个作甚？」

「不为什么。天哪，西迪，我肯拿什么换一口变更之风啊。」

「这话你也只好再等等了。」他呻吟道。我自己的模样想必也够呛，因为他揽住我的肩，哑着嗓音低声道：「宽宽心，小甜心，咱们虽然受这份大罪，到底还死不成变更之死。」

「此话怎讲？」我问他。

我不想像其他人那样乱蹦。我怀疑自己一蹦就会收不住。于是，为了别把自己逼疯，我开始在心里重新过一遍那桩案子：谁对维持器做了什么。

===Original===
During the hunt, there had been some pretty wild suggestions tossed around as to its disappearance or at least its Introversion: a feat of Snake science amounting to sorcery; the Spider high command bunkering the Places from above, perhaps in reaction to the loss of the Express Room, in such a hurry that they hadn’t even time to transmit warnings; the hand of the Late Cosmicians, those mysterious hypothetical beings who are supposed to have successfully resisted the extension of the Change War into the future much beyond Sevensee’s epoch—unless the Late Cosmicians are the ones fighting the Change War.

One thing these suggestions had steered very clear of was naming any one of us as a suspect, whether acting as Snake spy, Spider political police, agent of—who knows, after Bruce?—a secret Change World Committee of Public Safety or Spider revolutionary underground, or strictly on our own. Just as no one had piped a word, since the Maintainer had been palmed, about the split between Erich’s and Bruce’s factions.

Good group thinking probably, to sink differences in the emergency, but that didn’t apply to what I did with my own thoughts.

===Chinese===
搜查期间，关于它的失踪、或至少它的被内闭，飞过一些相当离奇的猜测：一桩等同于巫术的蛇方科学绝技；蜘蛛最高统帅自上而下对各场所实施的「闭关自守」，没准是对特快室失守的应激，仓促得连警报都来不及发；再有就是晚期宇宙人（Late Cosmicians）之手——那伙神秘的假想存在，据信已成功地把变更战争向未来的延伸挡在了七观纪元之后很远——除非晚期宇宙人正是打变更战争的那一方。

这些猜测绕得远远的，唯独自一点：把我们中的任何一个列为嫌疑人——不管他是蛇方奸细、蜘蛛政治警察，还是——布鲁斯之后，谁知道呢——变更世界某个秘密公安委员会或蜘蛛革命地下的特工，又或者纯粹单干。同样，自打维持器被摸走，对埃里希和布鲁斯两派的分裂，也没有人吱过半个字。

兴许是好的集体思维：危难当头，化异见于一炉——可这条管不着我自己怎么想。

---

===Original===
Who wanted to escape so bad they’d Introvert the Place, cutting off all possible contact and communication either way with the cosmos and running the very big risk of not getting back to the cosmos at all?

Leaving out what had happened since Bruce had arrived and stirred things up, Doc seemed to me to have the strongest motive. He knew that Sid couldn’t keep covering up for him forever and that Spider punishments for derelictions of duty are not just the clink of a firing squad, as Erich had reminded us. But Doc had been flat on the floor in front of the bar from the time Bruce had jumped on top of it, though I certainly hadn’t had my eye on him every second.

Beau? Beau had said he was bored with the Place at a time when what he said counted, so he’d hardly lock himself in it maybe forever, not to mention locking Bruce in with himself and the babe he had a yen for.

Sid loves reality, Changing or not, and every least thing in it, people especially, more than any man or woman I’ve ever known—he’s like a big-eyed baby who wants to grab every object and put it in his mouth—and it was hard to imagine him ever cutting himself off from the cosmos.

Maud, Kaby, Mark and the two E.T.s? None of them had any motive I knew of, though Sevensee’s being from the very far future did tie in with that idea about the Late Cosmicians, and there did seem to be something developing between the Cretan and the Roman that could make them want to be Introverted together.

“Stick to the facts, Greta,” I reminded myself with a private groan.

That left Erich, Bruce, Lili and myself.

Erich, I thought—now we’re getting somewhere. The little commandant has the nervous system of a coyote and the courage of a crazy tomcat, and if he thought it would help him settle his battle with Bruce better to be locked in with him, he’d do it in a second.

But even before Erich had danced on the bomb, he’d been heckling Bruce from the crowd. Still, there would have been time between heckles for him to step quietly back from us, Introvert the Maintainer and … well, that was nine-tenths of the problem.

If I was the guilty party, I was nuts and that was the best explanation of all. Gr-r-r!

Bruce’s motives seemed so obvious, especially the mortal (or was it immortal?) danger he’d put himself in by inciting mutiny, that it seemed a shame he’d been in full view on the bar so long. Surely, if the Maintainer had been Introverted before he jumped on the bar, we’d all have noticed the flashing blue telltale. For that matter, I’d have noticed it when I looked back at the Ghostgirls—if it worked as Sid claimed, and he said he had never seen it in operation, just read in the manual—oh, ’sdeath!

===Chinese===
谁会想逃得这么急，急到要把场所内闭——切断与宇宙双向的一切可能接触与通讯——还冒着从此回不去宇宙的天大风险？

撇开布鲁斯到来并搅动风云之后发生的种种不谈，在我看，动机顶硬的是大夫。他知道西德不可能永远替他打掩护，也知道蜘蛛方对渎职的刑罚绝不止行刑队那一响枪栓，埃里希早提醒过我们。可打布鲁斯跳上吧台那一刻起，大夫就直挺挺趴在吧台前的地板上，虽说我也绝没每秒钟都盯着他。

博呢？博可是在一言千金的当口说过他腻味这个场所的，所以他断不至于把自己锁进来，没准还是永锁，更别说还把布鲁斯，连同他馋着的那位小宝贝，一起锁进来。

西德爱现实，变也罢不变也罢，爱其中的每一件最小物事，尤其爱人，胜过我认识的任何男人女人——他像个大眼睛娃娃，见什么都想抓来往嘴里塞——很难想象他会把自己同宇宙一刀两断。

莫德、卡比、马克和两位 E.T. 呢？据我所知个个都没动机，虽说七观来自极远的未来，确实跟晚期宇宙人那条思路对得上榫，而那位克里特人和罗马人之间，也确实像在酝酿点什么，兴许会让他们乐意被一同关进来。

「紧扣事实，格蕾塔。」我暗自哀叹着提醒自己。

那就剩埃里希、布鲁斯、莉莉和我。

埃里希，我想——这就有眉目了。小指挥官长着郊狼的神经系统、疯公猫的胆量，只要他觉得跟布鲁斯锁在一处更有利于打完他那一仗，他一秒钟内就干得出来。

可早在埃里希跳上炸弹跳舞之前，他就一直混在人堆里冲布鲁斯起哄。不过，起哄与起哄之间，他尽可以从容退到我们身后，内闭维持器，然后……唉，难题的九成就在这儿。

倘若罪犯是我，那我就是疯子，而这恰恰是全案最好的解释。吼——！

布鲁斯的动机显眼得很，尤其是他煽动叛变给自己招来的那种致命——或者说，夺他永生——的危险，以至于他那么久暴露在吧台的众目睽睽之下，简直可惜了。可以肯定，维持器若是在他跳上吧台之前就被内闭，那盏闪烁的蓝灯我们谁都该瞧见了。就此而言，我回头看幽灵少女那会儿也该瞧见——倘若它像西德说的那样工作的话，何况他说自己也从没见过它动真格，只在手册里读到过——噢，皇天！

---

===Original===
But Bruce didn’t need opportunity, as I’m sure all the males in the Place would have told me right off, because he had Lili to pull the job for him and she had as much opportunity as any of the rest of us. Myself, I have large reservations to this woman-putty-in-the-hands-of-the-man-she-loves-madly theory, but I had to admit there was something to be said for it in this case, and it had seemed quite natural to me when the rest of us had decided, by unspoken agreement, that neither Lili’s nor Bruce’s checks counted when we were hunting for the Maintainer.

That took care of all of us and left only the mysterious stranger, intruding somehow through a Door (how’d he get it without using our Maintainer?) or from an unimaginable hiding place or straight out of the Void itself. I know that last is impossible—nothing can step out of nothing—but if anything ever looked like it was specially built for something not at all nice to come looming out of, it’s the Void—misty, foggily churning, slimy gray. …

===Chinese===
可布鲁斯不需要机会——场所里的男性们保管会这样抢着告诉我——因为他有莉莉替他动手，而她的机会跟我们任何一个人一样多。我个人嘛，对这套「女人是面团、任由她爱得发狂的男人搓圆捏扁」论持重大保留，可我不得不承认，这一案里它有几分道理；而我们其余人凭默契议定，搜维持器时莉莉和布鲁斯经手的都不算数，当时我也觉得天经地义。

这一来我们全体都有了交代，只剩下那位神秘的生客：不知怎么从一扇门里闯了进来（不使咱们的维持器，他哪来的门？），或者从某个无法想象的藏身处，或者干脆从虚空本身里走了出来。我知道最后一项不可能——没有什么能从『没有』里迈出来——可要说天下有什么东西，长得就像专门为某种绝不善良的玩意儿破空而出量身定做的，那就是虚空——雾蒙蒙、灰浆翻滚、黏滑的灰……

===Original===
“Wait a second,” I told myself, “and hang onto this, Greta. It should have smacked you in the face at the start.”

Whatever came out of the Void, or, more to the point, whoever slipped back from our crowd to the Maintainer, Bruce would have seen them. He was looking at the Maintainer past our heads the whole time, and whatever happened to it, he saw it.

Erich wouldn’t have, even after he was on the bomb, because he’d been stagewise enough to face Bruce most of the time to build up his role as tribune of the people.

But Bruce would have—unless he got so caught up in what he was saying. …

No, kid, a Demon is always an actor, no matter how much he believes in what he’s saying, and there never was an actor yet who wouldn’t instantly notice a member of the audience starting to walk out on his big scene.

So Bruce knew, which made him a better actor than I’d have been willing to grant, since it didn’t look as if anyone else had thought of what had just occurred to me, or they’d have gone over and put it to him.

Not me, though—I don’t work that way. Besides, I didn’t feel up to it—Nervy Anna enfold me, I felt like pure hell.

“Maybe,” I told myself encouragingly, “the Place is Hell,” but added, “Be your age, Greta—be a real rootless, ruleless, ruthless twenty-nine.”

===Chinese===
「且慢，」我对自己说，「抓住这条，格蕾塔。它本该一开始就扇你个正着。」

不管什么东西从虚空里出来，或者更要紧的，不管是咱们这堆人里谁悄悄退到维持器跟前，布鲁斯都该看见了。他自始至终越过我们的头顶望着维持器，它出了什么事，他都看见了。

埃里希就看不见，哪怕他站上炸弹之后也一样，因为他舞台意识十足，大部分时间都拿脸对着布鲁斯，好立稳他人民护民官的角色。

可布鲁斯一定看见了——除非他讲得太忘我……

不，丫头，魔鬼永远是演员，不管他多么相信自己说的话，而天底下还没有哪个演员，会注意不到有位听众在他的重头戏上中途起立退场。

所以布鲁斯是知情的，这使他成了一个比我肯承认的更好的演员，因为看起来，别人谁也没想到我刚刚想到的这层，否则早过去当面质他了。

我不去——我不做那种事。再说，我也提不起劲——涅维·安娜（Nervy Anna）抱住我吧，我难受得要死。

「没准，」我给自己打气，「这场所就是地狱。」随即又补一句：「有点大人样，格蕾塔——做一个货真价实、无根、无规、无情的二十九岁。」

## XI The Western Front, 1917 / 西线，1917

===Original===
> The barrage roars and lifts. Then, clumsily bowed
 

 With bombs and guns and shovels and battle gear,
 

 Men jostle and climb to meet the bristling fire.
 

 Lines of gray, muttering faces, masked with fear,
 

 They leave their trenches, going over the top,
 

 While time ticks blank and busy on their wrists

===Chinese===
> 弹幕轰鸣，而后抬升。于是，背着炸弹、
> 步枪、铁铲和全副披挂、笨拙伛偻的
> 男人们挤作一团往上爬，迎向炸毛的火线。
> 一行行灰色的、喃喃的面孔，蒙着恐惧，
> 他们离开战壕，爬向坡顶，
> 而时间在他们腕上空白而忙碌地走着。（译注：西格夫里德·萨松《序曲：部队》。）

---

===Original===
“Please don’t, Lili.”

“I shall, my love.”

“Sweetling, wake up! Hast the shakes?”

I opened my eyes a little and lied to Siddy with a smile and locked my hands together tight and watched Bruce and Lili quarrel nobly near the control divan and wished I had a great love to blur my misery and provide me with a passable substitute for Change Winds.

Lili won the argument, judging from the way she threw her head back and stepped away from Bruce’s arms while giving him a proud, tender smile. He walked off a few steps; praise be, he didn’t shrug his shoulders at us like an old husband, though his nerves were showing and he didn’t seem to be standing Introversion well at all, as who of us were?

Lili rested a hand on the head of the control divan and pressed her lips together and looked around at us, mostly with her eyes. She’d wound a gray silk bandeau around her bangs. Her short gray silk dress without a waistline made her look, not so much like a flapper, though she looked like that all right, as like a little girl, except the neckline was scooped low enough to show she wasn’t.

Her gaze hesitated and then stopped at me and I got a sunk feeling of what was coming, because women are always picking on me for an audience. Besides, Sid and I were the centrist party of two in our fresh-out-of-the-shell Place politics.

She took a deep breath and stuck out her chin and said in a voice that was even a little higher and Britisher than she usually uses, “We girls have often cried, ‘Shut the Door!’ But now the Door is jolly well shut for keeps!”

===Chinese===
「请别这样，莉莉。」

「我偏要，我亲爱的。」

「小甜心，醒醒！打起战来了？」

我睁开一条眼缝，冲西迪撒了个谎还带着笑，把双手死死扣在一起，看布鲁斯和莉莉在控制躺椅附近进行一场高贵的争吵，心里巴望自己也有场伟大的恋爱，好晕开我的愁苦，给我当一剂过得去的变更之风代用品。

吵架是莉莉赢了——看那样子就知道：她仰头甩发，退出布鲁斯的臂弯，却回他一个又骄傲又温柔的笑。他走开几步；谢天谢地，他没像老丈夫那样朝我们耸肩，虽说他的神经已经挂在明面上，而且他似乎把内闭撑得一点也不好——可我们谁又撑得好？

莉莉一只手搭上控制躺椅的椅头，抿紧嘴唇，用眼睛多于用脸地环视我们。她在刘海里箍了一圈灰丝缎发带。她那条没有腰线的灰丝缎短裙，与其说让她像个飞来波女郎——虽说像也确实像——不如说让她像个小女孩，只是领口开得够低，足以证明她不是。

她的目光迟疑了一下，落定在我身上，我心头一沉，知道要来什么了，因为女人挑观众总挑我。再说，在这处刚刚破壳的场所政治里，西德和我可是两人一伙的中间派。

她深吸一口气，抬起下巴，用一种比平常还要高出几度、还要英国腔的嗓音说：「我们姑娘家常喊：『关上门！』可现在，这扇门可是永远地关死了！」

===Original===
I knew I’d guessed right and I felt crawly with embarrassment, because I know about this love business of thinking you’re the other person and trying to live their life—and grab their glory, though you don’t know that—and carry their message for them, and how it can foul things up. Still, I couldn’t help admitting what she said wasn’t too bad a start—unpleasantly apt to be true, at any rate.

“My fiancé believes we may yet be able to open the Door. I do not. He thinks it is a bit premature to discuss the peculiar pickle in which we all find ourselves. I do not.”

There was a rasp of laughter from the bar. The militarists were reacting. Erich stepped out, looking very happy. “So now we have to listen to women making speeches,” he called. “What is this Place, anyhow? Sidney Lessingham’s Saturday Evening Sewing Circle?”

===Chinese===
我猜中了，尴尬得浑身像爬满了小虫。因为我懂这桩恋爱事业：以为自己是对方，试着替他活他的人生——还捎走他的荣光，虽然你自己不知道——替他传他的讯息，还有这一切能把事情搅成什么样。话虽如此，我也不得不承认，她这话起头起得不赖——至少，不祥地近似事实。

「我的未婚夫相信，我们终究还开得了这扇门。我不信。他认为现在就讨论咱们大伙陷身的这缸特制酱菜，未免早了点。我不认为。」

吧台那边响起一声锉刀似的笑。军国主义者们开始反攻。埃里希跨出来，一脸快活。「这下咱们还得听女人演讲了，」他喊道，「这场所到底算什么？悉尼·莱辛厄姆的『星期六晚邮』缝纫会吗？」

---

===Original===
Beau and Sevensee, who’d stopped their pacing halfway between the bar and the control divan, turned toward Erich, and Sevensee looked a little burlier, a little more like half a horse, than satyrs in mythology book illustrations. He stamped—medium hard, I’d say—and said, “Ahh, go flya kite.” I’d found out he’d learned English from a Demon who’d been a longshoreman with syndicalist-anarchist sympathies. Erich shut up for a moment and stood there grinning, his hands on his hips.

Lili nodded to the satyr and cleared her throat, looking scared. But she didn’t speak; I could see she was thinking and feeling something, and her face got ugly and haggard, as if she were in a Change Wind that hadn’t reached me yet, and her mouth went into a snarl to fight tears, but some spurted out, and when she did speak her voice was an octave lower and it wasn’t just London talking but New York too.

===Chinese===
博和七观——两人正走到吧台和控制躺椅的半途——转身对着埃里希，七观显得比神话图册里的半人羊更壮一圈、更像半匹马。他跺了一下蹄子——依我说，中等力道——说：「啊哈，放风筝去吧你。」我后来查明，教他英文的那位魔鬼，从前是个同情工团主义-无政府主义的码头装卸工。埃里希闭嘴了一会儿，站在那儿咧着嘴，双手叉腰。

莉莉朝半人羊点了点头，清了清嗓子，一脸惶恐。可她没有开口；我看得出来她正在想什么、感到什么，她的脸变得丑陋而憔悴，仿佛正处在一股还没刮到我这儿的变更之风里，她的嘴扭成一个与眼泪搏斗的狞笑，还是有几滴迸了出来，而等她真开了口，嗓音低了八度，说话的不光是伦敦，还有纽约。

===Original===
“I don’t know how Resurrection felt to you people, because I’m new and I loathe asking questions, but to me it was pure torture and I wished only I’d had the courage to tell Suzaku, ‘I wish to remain a Zombie, if you don’t mind. I’d rather the nightmares.’ But I accepted Resurrection because I’ve been taught to be polite and because there is the Demon in me I don’t understand that always wishes to live, and I found that I still felt like a Zombie, although I could flit about, and that I still had the nightmares, except they’d grown a deal vivider.

“I was a young girl again, seventeen, and I suppose every woman wishes to be seventeen, but I wasn’t seventeen inside my head—I was a woman who had died of Bright’s disease in New York in 1929 and also, because a Big Change blew my lifeline into a new drift, a woman who had died of the same disease in Nazi-occupied London in 1955, but rather more slowly because, as you can fancy, the liquor was in far shorter supply. I had to live with both those sets of memories and the Change World didn’t blot them out any more than I’m told it does those of any Demon, and it didn’t even push them into the background as I’d hoped it would.

“When some Change Fellow would say to me, ‘Hallo, beautiful, how about a smile?’ or ‘That’s a posh frock, kiddo,’ I’d be back at Bellevue looking down at my swollen figure and the light getting like spokes of ice, or in that dreadful gin-steeped Stepney bedroom with Phyllis coughing herself to death beside me, or at best, for a moment, a little girl in Glamorgan looking at the Roman road and wondering about the wonderful life that lay ahead.”

===Chinese===
「复活在你们各位身上是什么滋味，我不知道，因为我是新来的，又讨厌问东问西，可对我来说，那是纯粹的酷刑，我只恨不得当时有胆量对朱雀（Suzaku）说：『如果您不介意，我情愿继续当僵尸。噩梦我认了。』可我接受了复活，一因为我从小被教养成懂礼貌的人，二因为我身体里有一个我搞不懂的、永远想活下去的魔鬼，而我发现，自己虽然能四处飘了，感觉却仍然像僵尸，噩梦也照做不误——只是鲜活了老大一截。

「我又成了年轻姑娘，十七岁，我猜哪个女人都想回到十七岁，可我脑袋里装的并不是十七岁——我是一个 1929 年在纽约死于布赖特氏病的女人，同时，因为一场大变更把我的生命线吹进了新的矿层，也是一个 1955 年在纳粹占领下的伦敦死于同一种病的女人，而且死得慢得多，因为你们可以想见，那儿的酒紧缺得多。我只好抱着两套记忆活，而变更世界并没有把它们涂掉——据我所知，对哪个魔鬼它都不涂——甚至也没像我希望的那样，把它们推到背景里去。

「每逢哪位变更世界的爷们儿对我说：『嗨，美人儿，给个笑？』或者『这件裙子真阔气，小丫头』，我就一下回到贝尔维尤医院，低头看自己肿胀的身子和那变得像冰凌辐条一样的光；或者回到斯特普尼那间泡透了杜松子味的卧室，菲莉丝在我旁边咳嗽着把自己咳死；顶好也不过是恍惚一刻，变回格拉摩根的一个小姑娘，望着罗马古道，憧憬着铺在前头的锦绣人生。」

---

===Original===
I looked at Erich, remembering he had a long nasty future back in the cosmos himself, and at any rate he wasn’t smiling, and I thought maybe he’s getting a little humility, knowing someone else has two of those futures, but I doubted it.

“Because, you see,” Lili kept forcing it out, “all my three lives I’d been a girl who fell in love with a great young poet she’d never met, the voice of the new youth and all youth, and she’d told her first big lie to get in the Red Cross and across to France to be nearer him, and it was all danger and dark magics and a knight in armor, and she pictured how she’d find him wounded but not seriously, with a little bandage around his head, and she’d light a fag for him and smile lightly, never letting him guess what she felt, but only being her best self and watching to see if that made something happen to him. …

“And then the Boche machine guns cut him down at Passchendaele and there couldn’t ever have been bandages big enough and the girl stayed seventeen inside and messed about and tried to be wicked, though she wasn’t very good at that, and to drink, and she had a bit more talent there, though drinking yourself to death is not nearly as easy as it sounds, even with a kidney weakness to help. But she turned the trick.

“Then a cock crows. She wakes with a tearing start from the gray dreams of death that fill her lifeline. It’s cold daybreak. There’s the smell of a French farm. She feels her ankles and they’re not at all like huge rubber boots filled with water. They’re not swollen the least bit. They’re young legs.

“There’s a little window and the tops of a row of trees that may be poplars when there’s more light, and what there is shows cots like her own and heads under blankets and hanging uniforms make large shadows and a girl is snoring. There’s a very distant rumble and it moves the window a bit. Then she remembers they’re Red Cross girls many, many kilometers from Passchendaele and that Bruce Marchant is going to die at dawn today.

“In a few more minutes, he’s going over the top where there’s a crop-headed machine-gunner in field gray already looking down the sights and swinging the gun a bit. But she isn’t going to die today. She’s going to die in 1929 and 1955.

“And just as she’s going mad, there’s a creaking and out of the shadows tiptoes a Jap with a woman’s hairdo and the whitest face and the blackest eyebrows. He’s wearing a rose robe and a black sash which belts to his sides two samurai swords, but in his right hand he has a strange silver pistol. And he smiles at her as if they were brother and sister and lovers at the same time and he says, ‘*Voulez-vous vivre, mademoiselle?*’ and she stares and he bobs his head and says, ‘Missy wish live, yes, no?’ ”

===Chinese===
我看了看埃里希，想起他自己在宇宙里也拖着一个又长又臭的未来，好歹他这会儿没笑，我心想，知道别人一个人就摊上两个那种未来，他没准也长了点谦卑——可我表示怀疑。

「因为，你们瞧，」莉莉硬往外挤，「我那三辈子里，始终是一个爱上了一位素未谋面的伟大青年诗人的姑娘——新青年乃至一切青年的喉舌——她为了进红十字会、过海去法国离他近些，撒了平生第一个大谎；那一切都是险境、黑魔法和甲胄骑士，她想象自己会寻见他挂了彩但不重，头上缠一小圈绷带，她会替他点上一支烟，轻轻一笑，绝不让他猜到她的心事，只拿出最好的自己，静静看着这是否能让他心里起点什么。……

「然后德国佬的机枪在帕斯亨达勒把他放倒了，天底下不会再有够大的绷带，而那姑娘在心里永远是十七岁，浑浑噩噩，试着学坏——虽说干这个不太在行——又试着喝酒，这方面她倒多点天分，尽管把自己喝死远没有听上去那么容易，哪怕有一副虚弱的肾帮衬。可她还是成了。

「然后一声鸡啼。她从塞满她生命线的灰色死亡之梦里猛然挣醒。是寒冷的拂晓。有一股法国农庄的气味。她摸摸自己的脚踝，一点也不像灌满水的大胶靴。一丝也不肿。是两条年轻的腿。

「有一扇小窗，一排树的树梢——等天再亮些，多半是白杨——借着微光能看见一张张跟她一样的行军床，毯子底下一颗颗脑袋，挂着的军服投下大大的影子，一个姑娘在打鼾。极远处有一阵滚雷似的闷响，震得窗子轻轻一动。这时她才想起，她们是红十字会的姑娘，离帕斯亨达勒还隔着千山万水的公里数，而布鲁斯·马钱特今天拂晓就要死了。

「再过几分钟，他就要爬出战壕，那边的野灰军装、平头的机枪手已经贴上了瞄具，枪口正缓缓地摇。可她今天不会死。她要死在 1929 年和 1955 年。

「而就在她快要疯掉的时候，吱呀一响，阴影里踮脚走出一个日本人，梳着女人的发式，一张顶白的脸，两道顶黑的眉。他穿玫瑰色长袍，黑腰带上左右别着两把武士刀，右手却握着一支古怪的银色手枪。他朝她微笑，仿佛两人既是兄妹又是恋人，他说：『*Voulez-vous vivre, mademoiselle?*（小姐，您愿意活吗？）』她怔住，他点点头，说：『小姐想活，是，不是？』」

---

===Original===
Sid’s paw closed quietly around my shaking hands. It always gets me to hear about anyone’s Resurrection, and although mine was crazier, it also had the Krauts in it. I hoped she wouldn’t go through the rest of the formula and she didn’t.

“Five minutes later, he’s gone down a stairs more like a ladder to wait below and she’s dressing in a rush. Her clothes resist a little, as if they were lightly gummed to the hook and the stained wall, and she hates to touch them. It’s getting lighter and her cot looks as if someone were still sleeping there, although it’s empty, and she couldn’t bring herself to put her hand on the place if her new life depended on it.

“She climbs down and her long skirt doesn’t bother her because she knows how to swing it. Suzaku conducts her past a sentry who doesn’t see them and a puffy-faced farmer in a smock coughing and spitting the night out of his throat. They cross the farmyard and it’s filled with rose light and she sees the sun is up and she knows that Bruce Marchant has just bled to death.

“There’s an empty open touring car chugging loudly, waiting for someone; it has huge muddy wheels with wooden spokes and a brass radiator that says ‘Simplex.’ But Suzaku leads her past it to a dunghill and bows apologetically and she steps through a Door.”

I heard Erich say to the others at the bar, “How touching! Now shall I tell everyone about my operation?” But he didn’t get much of a laugh.

===Chinese===
西德的巴掌轻轻拢住我发抖的双手。听人讲自己的复活，我回回受不了，虽说我的那一段更疯，里面也有德国佬。我巴望她别把那套程式的后半截也走完，她没走。

「五分钟后，他已顺着那架更像梯子的楼梯下去等着，她也在慌慌张张穿衣。衣服微微作梗，仿佛被轻轻粘在挂钩和斑驳的墙上，她碰都怕碰。天越来越亮，她那张行军床看上去仿佛还有人睡着，虽说已经空了，哪怕新生命全系于此，她也下不去手往那床面上按一按。

「她爬下去，长裙难不倒她，因为她会使唤它。朱雀领着她绕过一个看不见他们的哨兵，绕过一张虚肿脸、罩着粗布罩衫、正把一夜的浊气从喉咙里咳出来啐掉的农夫。他们穿过场院，院里灌满玫瑰色的光，她看见太阳升起来了，她知道，布鲁斯·马钱特刚刚流尽了血。

「有一辆空的敞篷旅行车突突地响着等谁；巨大的泥轮子配木辐条，黄铜散热器上刻着『Simplex』。可朱雀领着她绕过它，走到一座粪堆前，歉意地一躬身，她跨过了一扇门。」

我听见埃里希对吧台边那伙人说：「真感人！现在要不要由我来给大伙讲讲我的行动？」可他没换回几声笑。

===Original===
“That’s how Lilian Foster came into the Change World with its steel-engraved nightmares and its deadly pace and deadlier lassitudes. I was more alive than I ever had been before, but it was the kind of life a corpse might get from unending electrical shocks and I couldn’t summon any purpose or hope and Bruce Marchant seemed farther away than ever.

“Then, not six hours ago, a Soldier in a black uniform came through the Door and I thought, ‘It can’t be, but it does look like his photographs,’ and then I thought I heard someone say the name Bruce, and then he shouted as if to all the world that he was Bruce Marchant, and I knew there was a Resurrection beyond Resurrection, a true resurrection. Oh, Bruce—”

She looked at him and he was crying and smiling and all the young beauty flooded back into her face, and I thought, “It has to be Change Winds, but it can’t be. Face it without slobbering, Greta—there’s something that works bigger miracles than Change.”

And she went on, “And then the Change Winds died when the Snakes vaporized the Maintainer or the Ghostgirls Introverted it and all three of them vanished so swiftly and silently that even Bruce didn’t notice—those are the best explanations I can summon and I fancy one of them is true. At all events, the Change Winds died and my past and even my futures became something I could bear lightly, because I have someone to bear them with me, and because at last I have a true future stretching out ahead of me, an unknown future which I shall create by living. Oh, don’t you see that all of us have it now, this big opportunity?”

“*Hussa* for Sidney’s suffragettes and the W.C.T.U.!” Erich cheered. “Beau, will you play us a medley of ‘Hearts and Flowers’ and ‘Onward, Christian Soldiers’? I’m deeply moved, Lili. Where do the rest of us queue up for the Great Love Affair of the Century?”

===Chinese===
「莉莲·福斯特就是这样走进了变更世界，连同它钢版蚀刻的噩梦、它致命的节奏和更致命的倦怠。我比以往任何时候都更有生气，可那是尸体从无休止的电击里得到的那种生气，我召集不起半点目的或希望，而布鲁斯·马钱特似乎比任何时候都更遥远。

「然后，不到六个钟头之前，一名黑军装的士兵跨门而入，我心想：『不可能，可真像照片上的他，』接着我好像听见有人叫出「布鲁斯」这个名字，随后他向全世界似的喊出他是布鲁斯·马钱特，于是我知道，复活之外还有复活，一场真正的复活。哦，布鲁斯——」

她望着他，他正又哭又笑，而青春的美丽尽数涌回她的脸，我心想：「这一定是变更之风，可又不可能是。别哭丧着面对它，格蕾塔——有一种东西，行的神迹比变更更大。」

她接着说：「随后变更之风死了——蛇方蒸发了维持器，或者幽灵少女内闭了它，他们仨消失得那么迅疾、那么无声，连布鲁斯都没能察觉——这是我能召集出的最好的解释，我猜其中之一是真的。无论如何，变更之风死了，我的过去、甚至我的两个未来，都变成了我能轻轻扛住的东西，因为我有个人陪我一起扛，也因为我终于有了一个真正的未来在前方铺展，一个未知的未来，我要用活着本身去创造它。哦，你们难道看不出来吗，如今我们人人都有了它——这个大机会？」

「悉尼的妇女参政派和基督教妇女禁酒联合会（W.C.T.U.）万岁——*Hussa*（好哇）！」埃里希喝彩。「博，给咱们来一段《心与花》连《信徒精兵歌》的串烧好吗？我深受感动，莉莉。我们其余的人到哪儿排队，报名这桩『世纪之恋』呢？」

## XII A Big Opportunity / 一个大机会

===Original===
> Now is a bearable burden. What buckles the back is the added weight of the past’s mistakes and the future’s fears.
> I had to learn to close the front door to tomorrow and the back door to yesterday and settle down to here and now.

===Chinese===
> 「现在」是扛得动的担子。压弯脊背的，是加上去的过去之错与未来之惧的分量。
> 我必须学会关上通往明天的前门、通往昨天的后门，安顿在此时此地。

---

===Original===
Nobody laughed at Erich’s screwball sarcasms and still I thought, “Yes, perish his hysterical little gray head, but he’s half right—Lili’s got the big thing now and she wants to serve it up to the rest of us on a platter, only love doesn’t cook and cut that way.”

Those weren’t bad ideas she had about the Maintainer, though, especially the one about the Ghostgirls doing the Introverting—it would explain why there couldn’t be Introversion drill, the manual stuff about blue flashes being window-dressing, and something disappearing without movement or transition is the sort of thing that might not catch the attention—and I guess they gave the others something to think about too, for there wasn’t any followup to Erich’s frantic sniping.

But I honestly didn’t see where there was this big opportunity being stuck away in a gray sack in the Void and I began to wonder and I got the strangest feeling and I said to myself, “Hang onto your hat, Greta. It’s hope.”

===Chinese===
埃里希那些疯疯癫癫的挖苦没换来一声笑，而我仍在想：「是啊，让他那歇斯底里的灰色小脑袋见鬼去，可他有一半说对了——大东西如今在莉莉手里，她想把它盛在托盘里端给我们大伙，只是爱情这东西，不按那个路数烹、不按那个路数切。」

不过她关于维持器的那些想法并不赖，尤其是幽灵少女执行内闭那一条——它顺带解释了为什么内闭没法操练，解释了手册里蓝光闪烁的说法纯属橱窗装点，也解释了一件东西毫无移动、毫无过渡地消失，正是那种未必引人注意的事——我猜这些也够其他人咀嚼一阵了，因为埃里希那阵疯狂的冷枪，再没有人接茬。

可老实说，我看不出这个被塞进虚空里一只灰口袋的大机会在哪儿，我开始犯嘀咕，接着涌起一种顶顶陌生的感觉，我对自己说：「扶稳帽子，格蕾塔。那是希望。」

===Original===
“The dreadful thing about being a Demon is that you have all time to range through,” Lili was saying with a smile. “You can never shut the back door to yesterday or the front door to tomorrow and simply live in the present. But now that’s been done for us: the Door is shut, we need never again rehash the past or the future. The Spiders and Snakes can never find us, for who ever heard of a Place that was truly lost being rescued? And as those in the know have told me, Introversion is the end as far as those outside are concerned. So we’re safe from the Spiders and Snakes, we need never be slaves or enemies again, and we have a Place in which to live our new lives, the Place prepared for us from the beginning.”

She paused. “Surely you understand what I mean? Sidney and Beauregard and Dr. Pyeshkov are the ones who explained it to me. The Place is a balanced aquarium, just like the cosmos. No one knows how many ages of Big Time it has been in use, without a bit of new material being brought in—only luxuries and people—and not a bit of waste cast off. No one knows how many more ages it may not sustain life. I never heard of Minor Maintainers wearing out. We have all the future, all the security, anyone can hope for. We have a Place to live together.”

===Chinese===
「当魔鬼的可怕之处，在于全部时间都任你游荡，」莉莉面带微笑地说。「你永远关不上通往昨天的后门、通往明天的前门，痛痛快快活在当下。可如今这已经替我们办妥了：门关死了，我们再不必翻过去和未来的旧账。蜘蛛和蛇方永远找不到我们，因为谁听说过一处彻底失踪的场所还能获救？而知情人士告诉我，对外面的人来说，内闭就是终结。所以我们既躲开了蜘蛛和蛇方，也再不必当奴隶或当敌人，我们还有一处场所来过我们的新生活——这场所，从一开始就是为我们备下的。」

她顿了顿。「你们一定明白我的意思吧？是悉尼、博雷加德和佩什科夫大夫（Dr. Pyeshkov）讲给我听的。场所是一座收支平衡的水族箱，跟宇宙一模一样。谁也说不清它已在大时光里使用了多少纪元，没运进一星半点新物质——只有奢侈品和人——也没倾出一星半点废料。谁也说不清它还能再供养生命多少纪元。我从未听说哪台副维持器磨损过。我们拥有任何人敢于指望的全部未来、全部安稳。我们有一处场所，可以同住。」

---

===Original===
You know, she was dead right and I realized that all the time I’d had the conviction in the back of my mind that we were going to suffocate or something if we didn’t get a Door open pretty quick. I should have known differently, if anybody should, because I’d once been in the Place without a Door for as long as a hundred sleeps during a foxhole stretch of the Change War and we’d had to start cycling our food and it had been okay.

And then, because it is also the way my mind works, I started to picture in a flash the consequences of our living together all by ourselves like Lili said.

I began to pair people off; I couldn’t help it. Let’s see, four women, six men, two E.T.s.

“Greta,” I said, “you’re going to be Miss Polly Andry for sure. We’ll have a daily newspaper and folk-dancing classes, we’ll shut the bar except evenings, Bruce’ll keep a rhymed history of the Place.”

I even thought, though I knew this part was strictly silly, about schools and children. I wondered what Siddy’s would look like, or my little commandant’s. “Don’t go near the Void, dears.” Of course that would be specially hard on the two E.T.s, but Sevensee at least wasn’t so different and the genetics boys had made some wonderful advances and Maud ought to know about them and there were some amazing gadgets in Surgery when Doc sobered up. The patter of little hoofs. …

===Chinese===
你要知道，她说得千真万确，我这才意识到，自己心里后屋一直窝着一个念头：要是不能快点开出一扇门，我们迟早要闷死还是怎么的。本该最清楚的是我，因为变更战争最挖战壕的那段日子，我曾在没有门的场所里一待就是一百次入眠，我们那时只好让食物循环起来用，结果也安然无恙。

接着，因为这同样是我脑子的做派，我一瞬间就开始勾勒：真像莉莉说的那样，我们自给自足地住在一起，会是什么光景。

我开始给人配对；忍不住。来，四名女子，六名男子，外加两位 E.T.。

「格蕾塔，」我对自己说，「你铁定要当波莉·安德丽小姐（Miss Polly Andry，谐音 polyandry『一妻多夫』——译注）了。咱们要办一份日报，开民间舞班，酒吧只晚上开，布鲁斯执笔一部押韵的场所编年史。」

我甚至还想到——虽说清楚这一节纯属犯傻——学校和孩子们。我琢磨西迪的孩子会长什么样，或者我那位小指挥官的。「宝贝们，别靠近虚空哦。」当然，这对两位 E.T. 特别残忍，可七观至少不算太悬殊，遗传学小子们也取得了几项了不起的进展，莫德应该门儿清，而且等大夫清醒的时候，手术区里还有些了不得的小装置。小蹄子的嗒嗒声……

===Original===
“My fiancé spoke to you about carrying a peace message to the rest of the cosmos,” Lili added, “and bringing an end to the Big Change, and healing all the wounds that have been made in the Little Time.”

I looked at Bruce. His face was set and strained, as will happen to the best of them when a girl starts talking about her man’s business, and I don’t know why, but I said to myself, “She’s crucifying him, she’s nailing him to his purpose as a woman will, even when there’s not much point to it, as now.”

And Lili went on, “It was a wonderful thought, but now we cannot carry or send any message and I believe it is too late in any event for a peace message to do any good. The cosmos is too raveled by change, too far gone. It will dissolve, fade, ‘leave not a rack behind.’ We’re the survivors. The torch of existence has been put in our hands.

“We may already be all that’s left in the cosmos, for have you thought that the Change Winds may have died at their source? We may never reach another cosmos, we may drift forever in the Void, but who of us has been Introverted before and who knows what we can or cannot do? We’re a seed for a new future to grow from. Perhaps all doomed universes cast off seeds like this Place. It’s a seed, it’s an embryo, let it grow.”

She looked swiftly at Bruce and then at Sid and she quoted, “ ‘Come, my friends, ’tis not too late to seek a newer world.’ ”

===Chinese===
「我的未婚夫向诸位讲过，要把一道和平讯息送到宇宙其余地方去，」莉莉补充道，「终结大变更，疗愈寻常时光里已成的种种创伤。」

我看向布鲁斯。他脸绷得紧紧的——姑娘家一开腔谈她男人的事业，最沉得住气的男人也会这样——也不知怎么，我心里冒出一句：「她在把他钉上十字架，像所有女人那样，把他钉死在他的使命上，哪怕这已经没多大意义，比如现在。」

莉莉接着说：「那是个了不起的念头，可如今我们既带不出、也发不出任何讯息，而且我相信，无论怎样，和平讯息都已来不及起作用了。宇宙被变更拆得太散，病入膏肓。它将溶解，消散，『连一朵云影也不留』（译注：《暴风雨》中语）。我们是幸存者。存在的火炬已经交到我们手里。

「我们可能已经是宇宙中剩下的一切，因为你们想过没有：变更之风没准已从源头死去？我们可能永远抵达不了另一个宇宙，可能在虚空里永远漂流，可咱们谁从前被内闭过？谁知道我们办得到什么、办不到什么？我们是一粒种子，一个新的未来将由它长起。也许所有劫数难逃的宇宙都会撒下这样的种子——像这处场所。它是种子，是胚芽，让它长。」

她飞快地看了看布鲁斯，又看了看西德，引诵道：「『来吧，朋友们，去寻一个更新的世界，为时未晚。』」（译注：丁尼生《尤利西斯》。）

---

===Original===
I squeezed Sid’s hand and I started to say something to him, but he didn’t know I was there; he was listening to Lili quote Tennyson with his eyes entranced and his mouth open, as if he were imagining new things to put into it—oh, Siddy!

And then I saw the others were looking at her the same way. Ilhilihis was seeing finer feather forests than long-dead Luna’s grow. The greenhouse child Maud ap-Ares Davies was stowing away on a starship bound for another galaxy, or thinking how different her life might have been, the children she might have had, if she’d stayed on the planets and out of the Change World. Even Erich looked as though he might be blitzing new universes, and Mark subduing them, for an eight-legged *Führer-imperator*. Beau was throbbing up a wider Mississippi in a bigger-than-life sidewheeler.

Even I—well, I wasn’t dreaming of a Greater Chicago. “Let’s not go hog-wild on this sort of thing,” I told myself, but I did look up at the Void and I got a shiver because I imagined it drawing away and the whole Place starting to grow.

===Chinese===
我捏了捏西德的手，正要对他说点什么，可他不知道我在跟前；他正听得入迷，嘴都张开了，听莉莉诵丁尼生，仿佛正琢磨着往里面添些什么新东西——噢，西迪！

随后我看见，其他人望她的眼神也是一路货色。伊尔希利希斯眼里，正长出比早已死去的月亮更精美的羽毛森林。温室里长大的孩子莫德·阿普-阿瑞斯·戴维斯（Maud ap-Ares Davies），正偷偷爬上一艘开往另一座星系的星船，或者正在想：倘若她留在行星上、没进变更世界，她的人生会是多么不同，她又会有怎样的孩子。连埃里希看上去都像在闪电战新的宇宙，而马克把它们一一降伏，献给他们那位八条腿的*元首-凯撒*（*Führer-imperator*）。博正驾着一艘超越尺度的明轮船，突突地驶上一条更宽阔的密西西比河。

连我也——得了，我倒没梦见什么大芝加哥。「这种事可别得意忘形，」我告诫自己，可我确实抬头望了虚空，并且打了个寒噤，因为我想象见它徐徐退去，整个场所开始生长。

===Original===
“I truly meant what I said about a seed,” Lili went on slowly. “I know, as you all do, that there are no children in the Change World, that there cannot be, that we all become instantly sterile, that what they call a curse is lifted from us girls and we are no longer in bondage to the moon.”

She was right, all right—if there’s one thing that’s been proved a million times in the Change World, it’s that.

“But we are no longer in the Change World,” Lili said softly, “and its limitations should no longer apply to us, including that one. I feel deeply certain of it, but—” she looked around slowly—“we are four women here and I thought one of us might have a surer indication.”

My eyes followed hers around like anybody’s would. In fact, everybody was looking around except Maud, and she had the silliest look of surprise on her face and it stayed there, and then, very carefully, she got down from the bar stool with her knitting. She looked at the half-finished pink bra with the long white needles stuck in it and her eyes bugged bigger yet, as if she were expecting it to turn into a baby sweater right then and there. Then she walked across the Place to Lili and stood beside her. While she was walking, the look of surprise changed to a quiet smile. The only other thing she did was throw her shoulders back a little.

I was jealous of her for a second, but it was a double miracle for her, considering her age, and I couldn’t grudge her that. And to tell the truth, I was a little frightened, too. Even with Dave, I’d been bothered about this business of having babies.

===Chinese===
「我说的种子，是真心话，」莉莉缓缓续道。「我知道，你们全知道：变更世界里没有孩子，也不可能有，我们个个当场绝育；他们所谓的诅咒，从我们姑娘身上解除了，我们不再受月亮的奴役。」

她说得对，没错——变更世界里若有一件事被验证过一百万遍，就是这件。

「可我们已经不在变更世界里了，」莉莉轻声说，「它的种种限制，理应不再管着我们，包括那一条。对此我有深切的笃定，不过——」她缓缓环视——「我们这里有四位女性，我想，其中一位也许能给我更确凿的征兆。」

我的目光跟着她的转，谁都会这样。事实上人人都在东张西望，只有莫德没有，她脸上挂着最傻气的一副惊愕，而且一直挂着，随后，她小心翼翼地抱着毛线活从吧台凳上下来。她望着那件织了一半的粉色胸衣、插在上面的长白毛针，眼睛瞪得更圆了，仿佛正等着它当场变成一件婴儿小毛衣。然后她穿过场所走到莉莉身边，站定。走着走着，那副惊愕换成了一抹安静的微笑。她做的唯一另一个动作，是把肩膀微微向后一振。

我嫉妒了她一秒钟，可考虑到她的年纪，这是双重奇迹，我嫉妒不起来。而且说老实话，我也有点心慌。即便跟戴夫在一起的时候，生孩子这档子事也让我犯过嘀咕。

---

===Original===
Yet I stood up with Siddy—I couldn’t stop myself and I guess he couldn’t either—and hand in hand we walked to the control divan. Beau and Sevensee were there and Bruce, of course, and then, so help me, those Soldiers to the death, Kaby and Mark, started over from the bar and I couldn’t see anything in their eyes about the greater glory of Crete and Rome, but something, I think, about each other, and after a moment Illy slowly detached himself from the piano and followed, lightly trailing his tentacles on the floor.

I couldn’t exactly see him hoping for little Illies in this company, unless it was true what the jokes said about Lunans, but maybe he was being really disinterested and maybe he wasn’t; maybe he was simply figuring that Illy ought to be on the side with the biggest battalions.

I heard dragging footsteps behind us and here came Doc from the Gallery, carrying in his folded arms an abstract sculpture as big as a newborn baby. It was an agglomeration of perfect shiny gray spheres the size of golf balls, shaping up to something like a large brain, but with holes showing through here and there. He held it out to us like an infant to be admired and worked his lips and tongue as if he were trying very hard to say something, though not a word came out that you could understand, and I thought, “Maxey Aleksevich may be speechless drunk and have all sorts of holes in his head, but he’s got the right instincts, bless his soulful little Russian heart.”

We were all crowded around the control divan like a football team huddling. The Peace Packers, it came to me. Sevensee would be fullback or center and Illy left end—what a receiver! The right number, too. Erich was alone at the bar, but now even he—“Oh, no, this can’t be,” I thought—even he came toward us. Then I saw that his face was working the worst ever. He stopped halfway and managed to force a smile, but it was the worst, too. “That’s my little commandant,” I thought, “no team spirit.”

“So now Lili and Bruce—yes, and *Grossmutterchen* Maud—have their little nest,” he said, and he wouldn’t have had to push his voice very hard to get a screech. “But what are the rest of us supposed to be—cowbirds?”

===Chinese===
可我还是跟西迪一起站了起来——我自己刹不住，我猜他也一样——手拉手走向控制躺椅。博和七观在那里，布鲁斯自然也在，然后，老天作证，那两位要战斗到死的士兵，卡比和马克，也从吧台动身了，他们眼里我看不见半点克里特和罗马的更大荣光，倒有些，我想，是冲着彼此的，过了一会儿，伊尔利慢慢把自己从钢琴上剥下来，跟了上去，触手在地板上轻轻曳着。

在这种人堆里指望小伊尔利们，我实在看不出来——除非关于月球人的那些笑话是真的——可没准他真是大公无私，也没准不是；没准他只是在盘算，伊尔利理应站到兵最壮的那一边。

身后传来拖沓的脚步，大夫从艺术品廊来了，合抱双臂捧着一座新生儿大小的抽象雕塑。那是高尔夫球大小、完美光亮的灰球体攒成的一团，攒出个大致像大号脑子的形状，四处透着窟窿。他把它像要人夸的婴儿一样朝我们递过来，嘴唇舌头一劲儿蠕动，仿佛在拼命想说什么，只是吐不出一个你能听懂的字，我心想：「马克西·阿列克谢维奇（Maxey Aleksevich）也许是醉得说不出话，脑袋上也尽是窟窿，可他的本能站对了边，保佑他这颗深情的小俄国心。」

我们全围拢在控制躺椅边，活像一支橄榄球队围圈喊话。咱们是「和平打包队」，这名称自己跳进我脑子。七观当后卫或中锋，伊尔利打左边锋——好一个接球手！号码也吉利。埃里希独自留在吧台，可现在连他——「哦不，不会吧，」我心想——连他也朝我们走来。随即我看见，他的脸抽动得空前难看。他在半路停下，勉强挤出一个笑，那也是空前难看的一个。「这就是我的小指挥官，」我心想，「毫无团队精神。」

「这么说，如今莉莉和布鲁斯——对，还有*Grossmutterchen*（老外婆）莫德——有他们的小窝了，」他说，他那嗓门再使一点劲就能劈成尖叫。「可我们其余的算什么——专往人家窝里下蛋的牛鹂？」

---

===Original===
He crooked his neck and flapped his hands and croaked, “Cuc-koo! Cuc-koo!” And I said to myself, “I often thought you were crazy, boy, but now I know.”

“*Teufelsdreck!*—yes, Devil’s dirt!—but you all seem to be infected with this dream of children. Can’t you see that the Change World is the natural and proper end of evolution?—a period of enjoyment and measuring, an ultimate working out of things, which women call destruction—‘Help, I’m being raped!’ ‘Oh, what are they doing to my children?’—but which men know as fulfillment.

“You’re given good parts in *Götterdämmerung* and you go up to the author and tap him on the shoulder and say, ‘Excuse me, Herr Wagner, but this Twilight of the Gods is just a bit morbid. Why don’t you write an opera for me about the little ones, the dear little blue-eyed curly-tops? A plot? Oh, boy meets girl and they settle down to breed, something like that.’

“Devil’s dirt doubled and damned! Have you thought what life will be like without a Door to go out of to find freedom and adventure, to measure your courage and keenness? Do you want to grow long gray beards hobbling around this asteroid turned inside out? Putter around indoors to the end of your days, mooning about little baby cosmoses?—incidentally, with a live bomb for company. The cave, the womb, the little gray home in the nest—is that what you want? It’ll grow? Oh, yes, like the city engulfing the wild wood, a proliferation of *Kinder, Kirche, Küche*—I should live so long!

“Women!—how I hate their bright eyes as they look at me from the fireside, bent-shouldered, rocking, deeply happy to be old, and say, ‘He’s getting weak, he’s giving out, soon I’ll have to put him to bed and do the simplest things for him.’ Your filthy Triple Goddess, Kaby, the birther, bride, and burier of man! Woman, the enfeebler, the fetterer, the crippler! Woman!—and the curly-headed little cancers she wants!”

===Chinese===
他扭起脖子，扑扇双手，鸦声道：「咕——咕！咕——咕！」我对自己说：「我常当你是个疯子，小子，现在我有数了。」

「*Teufelsdreck*（魔鬼的粪土）！——你们像是全染上了这场生孩子的梦。你们看不出来吗，变更世界是演化天然又正当的终点？——一段享受与度量的时期，一场对万物的终极清算，女人们管它叫毁灭——『救命，我被人强暴了！』『哦，他们对我的孩子们做了什么？』——而男人们认得它的真名：成全。

「人家在《诸神的黄昏》（*Götterdämmerung*）里派给你好角色，你却走上台去拍作者的肩膀说：『对不起，瓦格纳先生，这出《诸神的黄昏》未免有点病态。您何不为我写一部关于小家伙们的歌剧，写那些可爱的蓝眼睛小卷毛？剧情？哦，小伙子遇上姑娘，然后安顿下来生养，差不多就这路子。』

「魔鬼的粪土，翻倍再该死！你们想过没有，没有了出走的门，没有了自由和历险，没有了称量你胆量与锐气的去处，日子会是什么滋味？你们想在这颗翻了个面的小行星上拄着腿挪到灰胡子拖地？想在屋子里面磨蹭到老死，痴想些宇宙小宝宝？——顺带一提，还有一颗活炸弹作伴。山洞，子宫，窝里那个灰色的小家——你们要的就是这个？它会长大？哦是呀，像城市吞掉野林那样长，滋生出成片的*Kinder, Kirche, Küche*（孩子、教堂、厨房）——我可得活那么长！

「女人们！——我恨透了她们炉火边望过来的亮眼睛，弯着肩，摇着椅，为老去而深深幸福，还说着：『他不行了，他快熬干了，回头我得服侍他上床，最简单的活儿也得替他干。』你那脏兮兮的三重女神，卡比，男人的生者、嫁者、葬者！女人，削弱者、锁链者、致残者！女人！——还有她想要的那些卷毛小癌瘤！」

===Original===
He lurched toward us, pointing at Lili. “I never knew one who didn’t want to cripple a man if you gave her the chance. Cripple him, swaddle him, clip his wings, grind him to sausage to mold another man, hers, a doll man. You hid the Maintainer, you little smother-hen, so you could have your nest and your Brucie!”

He stopped, gasping, and I expected someone to bop him one on the schnozzle, and I think he did, too. I turned to Bruce and he was looking, I don’t know how, sorry, guilty, anxious, angry, shaken, inspired, all at once, and I wished people sometimes had simple suburban reactions like magazine stories.

Then Erich made the mistake, if it was one, of turning toward Bruce and slowly staggering toward him, pawing the air with his hands as if he were going to collapse into his arms, and saying, “Don’t let them get you, Bruce. Don’t let them tie you down. Don’t let them clip you—your words or your deeds. You’re a Soldier. Even when you talked about a peace message, you talked about doing some smashing of your own. No matter what you think and feel, Bruce, no matter how much lying you do and how much you hide, you’re really not on their side.”

That did it.

===Chinese===
他朝我们踉跄而来，直指莉莉。「我认得的每一个，只要给她机会，没有不想弄残一个男人的。弄残他，裹住他，剪了他的翅膀，把他磨成肉泥，好捏出另一个男人，她的男人，一个玩偶男人。是你藏了维持器，你这闷窝小母鸡，好守你的窝和你的小布鲁基！」

他停下来喘气，我等着谁上去照他鼻子来一拳，我想他自己也在等。我看向布鲁斯，他的神情——怎么说呢——歉疚、负罪、焦急、愤怒、动摇、感奋，一齐在脸上，我倒真希望人有时候能有点杂志小说里那种郊区居民式的简单反应。

随后埃里希犯了个错误——如果算错误的话——他转向布鲁斯，慢慢朝他踉跄过去，双手在空气里扒挠，仿佛就要倒进他怀里，嘴里说：「别让她们抓住你，布鲁斯。别让她们拴住你。别让她们剪了你——你的言语，你的作为。你是一名士兵。哪怕谈和平讯息的时候，你也说过要亲手碾碎点什么。不管你怎么想怎么感觉，布鲁斯，不管你撒了多少谎、藏了多少事，你其实不在她们那一边。」

这一下成了。

---

===Original===
It didn’t come soon enough or, I think, in the right spirit to please me, but I will say it for Bruce that he didn’t muck it up by tipping or softening his punch. He took one step forward and his shoulders spun and his fist connected sweet and clean.

As he did it, he said only one word, “Loki!” and darn if that didn’t switch me back to a campfire in the Indiana Dunes and my mother telling me out of the Elder Saga about the malicious, sneering, all-spoiling Norse god and how, when the other gods came to trap him in his hideaway by the river, he was on the point of finishing knotting a mysterious net big enough, I had imagined, to snare the whole universe, and that if they’d come a minute later, he would have.

Erich was stretched on the floor, his head hitched up, rubbing his jaw and glaring at Bruce. Mark, who was standing beside me, moved a little and I thought he was going to do something, maybe even clobber Bruce in the old spirit of you can’t do that to my buddy, but he just shook his head and said, “*Omnia vincit amor.*” I nudged him and said, “Meaning?” and he said, “Love licks everything.”

I’d never have expected it from a Roman, but he was half right at any rate. Lili had her victory: Bruce clearing the field for the marriage by laying out the woman-hating boy friend who would be trying to get him to go out nights. At that moment, I think Bruce wanted Lili and a life with her more than he wanted to reform the Change World. Sure, us women have our little victories—until the legions come or the Little Corporal draws up his artillery or the Panzers roar down the road.

===Chinese===
这一拳来得不够快，劲头——依我看——也谈不上叫我痛快，可我替布鲁斯说句公道话：他没有因为手抖或收力把这一拳糟蹋掉。他上前一步，肩膀一拧，拳头又脆又正地咬上了。

出拳的同时，他只说了一个字：「洛基！」好家伙，这一个字把我一下子拨回印第安纳沙丘的篝火边，母亲正给我念《老埃达》里的故事：那个恶毒、讥诮、毁尽一切的北欧之神，众神赶到河边巢穴捉他时，他正编完最后几个结——那张神秘的网，在我的想象里，大得兜得住整个宇宙；他们若晚到一分钟，他就成了。

埃里希直挺挺躺在地板上，头抬着，揉着下巴，怒瞪布鲁斯。站在我旁边的马克动了动，我以为他要干什么，没准还要本着「不许动我哥们儿」的古义给布鲁斯一拳，可他只摇摇头，说：「*Omnia vincit amor*（爱征服一切）。」我捅了捅他：「什么意思？」他说：「爱情摆平一切。」

这话出自一个罗马人之口，我万万想不到，可他至少说对了一半。莉莉赢了她的那一仗：布鲁斯放倒那个恨女人的、往后夜里还总要拉他出去鬼混的男朋友，为婚事清了场。在那一刻，我想，布鲁斯要莉莉、要和她过日子的心，胜过了他要改造变更世界。当然了，我们女人自有我们的小胜仗——直到军团开到，直到小伍长架起炮兵，直到装甲车队轰隆着碾过公路。

===Original===
Erich scrambled to his feet and stood there in a half-slump, half-crouch, still rubbing his jaw and glaring at Bruce over his hand, but making no move to continue the fight, and I studied his face and said to myself, “If he can get a gun, he’s going to shoot himself, I know.”

Bruce started to say something and hesitated, like I would have in his shoes, and just then Doc got one of his unpredictable inspirations and went weaving out toward Erich, holding out the sculpture and making deaf-and-dumb noises like he had to us. Erich looked at him as if he were going to kill him, and then grabbed the sculpture and swung it up over his head and smashed it down on the floor, and for a wonder, it didn’t shatter. It just skidded along in one piece and stopped inches from my feet.

That thing not breaking must have been the last straw for Erich. I swear I could see the red surge up through his eyes toward his brain. He swung around into the Stores sector and ran the few steps between him and the bronze bomb chest.

Everything got very slow motion for me, though I didn’t do any moving. Almost every man started out after Erich. Bruce didn’t, though, and Siddy turned back after the first surge forward, while Illy squunched down for a leap, and it was between Sevensee’s hairy shanks and Beau’s scissoring white pants that I saw that under-the-microscope circle of death’s heads and watched Erich’s finger go down on them in the order Kaby had given: one, three, five, six, two, four, seven. I was able to pray seven distinct times that he’d make a mistake.

He straightened up. Illy landed by the box like a huge silver spider and his tentacles whipped futilely across its top. The others surged to a frightened halt around them.

Erich’s chest was heaving, but his voice was cool and collected as he said, “You mentioned something about our having a future, Miss Foster. Now you can make that more specific. Unless we get back to the cosmos and dump this box, or find a Spider A-tech, or manage to call headquarters for guidance on disarming the bomb, we have a future exactly thirty minutes long.”

===Chinese===
埃里希手脚并用地爬起来，半塌半蹲地站在那儿，还揉着下巴，越过手背怒瞪布鲁斯，却没有再战的意思，我端详着他的脸，对自己说：「只要他能弄到一把枪，他会崩了自己的，我知道。」

布鲁斯想说什么，又犹豫了——换我站在他的鞋里也一样——就在这时，大夫得了他那种不可预测的灵感，捧着那座雕塑，摇摆着朝埃里希走去，冲他发出冲我们发过的那种又聋又哑的声响。埃里希看他的眼神像要杀了他，随后一把夺过雕塑，抡过头顶，狠狠砸向地板——怪了，它居然没碎。它囫囵个儿滑出去，停在我脚边几英寸处。

那玩意儿不碎，想必是压垮埃里希的最后一根稻草。我发誓，我看得见红色顺着他的眼睛朝脑子涌上去。他一转身冲进库房区，朝那口青铜炸弹箱跑去，只差几步。

在我这里，一切都变成了慢镜头，尽管我自己一动没动。几乎每个月人都追着埃里希冲了出去。布鲁斯却没有，西迪冲了一步也折了回来，伊尔利蹲身欲跃，而就在七观毛茸茸的小腿和博那两条绞动的白裤之间，我看见那圈显微镜下的骷髅，看着埃里希的指头按卡比给的顺序按下去：一、三、五、六、二、四、七。我得以整整祷告了七次，求他按错一个。

他直起身。伊尔利像一只巨大的银蜘蛛落在箱边，触手在箱顶上徒劳地抽打。其余的人惊惶地涌到近前，刹住。

埃里希胸口起伏着，嗓音却冷静自持：「福斯特小姐，你方才提到我们有未来。现在你可以把它说得更具体了。除非我们回到宇宙扔掉这口箱子，或者找到一名蜘蛛方原子技师，或者没法接通总部请教如何拆掉这颗炸弹，否则我们的未来，长度不多不少，三十分钟。」

## XIII The Tiger Is Loose / 老虎出笼

===Original===
> But whence he was, or of what wombe ybore,
 

 Of beasts, or of the earth, I have not red:
 

 But certes was with milke of wolves and tygres fed.

===Chinese===
> 可他来自何方、孕于何腹，
> 是生自兽类，还是出于泥土，我未尝读到：
> 但千真万确，他是吃狼奶和虎奶长大的。（译注：斯宾塞《仙后》。）

---

===Original===
I guess when they really push the button or throw the switch or spring the trap or focus the beam or what have you, you don’t faint or go crazy or anything else convenient. I didn’t. Everything, everybody, every move that was made, every word that was spoken, was painfully real to me, like a hand twisting and squeezing things deep inside me, and I saw every least detail spotlighted and magnified like I had the seven skulls.

Erich was standing beyond the bomb chest; little smiles were ruffling his lips. I’d never seen him look so sharp. Illy was beside him, but not on his side, you understand. Mark, Sevensee and Beau were around the chest to the nearer side. Beau had dropped to a knee and was scanning the chest minutely, terror-under-control making him bend his head a little closer than he needed to for clear vision, but with his hands locked together behind his back, I guess to restrain the impulse to push any and everything that looked like a disarming button.

Doc was sprawled face down on the nearest couch, out like a light, I suppose.

Us four girls were still by the control divan. With Kaby, that surprised me, because she didn’t look scared or frozen, but almost as intensely alive as Erich.

Sid had turned back, as I’d said, and had one hand stretched out toward but not touching the Minor Maintainer, and a look on his beardy face as if he were calling down death and destruction on every boozy rogue who had ever gone up from King’s Lynn to Cambridge and London, and I realized why: if he’d thought of the Minor Maintainer a second sooner, he could have pinned Erich down with heavy gravity before he could touch the buttons.

Bruce was resting one hand on the head of the control divan and was looking toward the group around the chest, toward Erich, I think, as if Erich had done something rather wonderful for him, though I can’t imagine myself being tickled at being included in anybody’s suicide surprise party. Bruce looked altogether too dreamy, Brahma blast him, for someone who must have the same steel-spiked thought in his head that I know darn well the rest of us had: that in twenty-nine minutes or so, the Place would be a sun in a bag.

===Chinese===
我想，等他们真按下按钮、扳下开关、触发陷阱、聚好光束或诸如此类的时候，你并不会晕倒，也不会发疯，或来点别的什么省事反应。我就没有。每件事、每个人、做出的每个动作、说出的每个字，都痛切地真实，像一只手在我身体深处拧着、攥着，而每一点最小的细节，我都看得像打了聚光、上了放大——好像那七颗骷髅到了我眼里。

埃里希站在炸弹箱那一头；细小的笑纹一阵阵拂过他的嘴唇。我从没见他神情这么锋利过。伊尔利在他身边，但你要明白，不在他那一边。马克、七观和博围在箱子近侧。博已单膝跪地，一寸一寸检视箱体，那份被压住的恐惧让他的头凑得比看清所需还低了几分，双手却反剪在背后扣死——我猜是按捺住冲动，免得看见什么像拆卸钮的东西都想去按。

大夫直挺挺趴在最近的躺椅上，我想是彻底断电了。

我们四个姑娘还守在控制躺椅边。卡比也在其中，这倒让我意外，因为她看上去既不怕也不僵，反而几乎跟埃里希一样烧得发亮地活着。

西德如我所说折了回来，一只手朝副维持器伸去，但没有碰，那张多须的脸上，一副要把死亡与毁灭呼到每一个从金斯林醉醺醺闯去剑桥和伦敦的泼皮头上的神情，我随即明白了缘由：他若早一秒想到副维持器，本可以用重压重力把埃里希钉死在够不着按钮的地方。

布鲁斯一只手搭着控制躺椅的椅头，望向围在箱子边的那群人，我想是望着埃里希，那眼神仿佛埃里希为他做了件相当美妙的事，虽说换了我，被人拉进任何人的自杀惊喜派对，实在乐不起来。布鲁斯看上去实在太悠然出神了，梵天雷劈他——要知道他脑袋里必然钉着同一根钢刺，而我们其余人脑子里钉着它，我是再清楚不过的：再过二十九分钟左右，场所就要变成一只口袋里的太阳。

---

===Original===
Erich was the first to get down to business, as I’d have laid any odds he would be. He had the jump on us and he wasn’t going to lose it.

“Well, when are you going to start getting Lili to tell us where she hid the Maintainer? It has to be her—she was too certain it was gone forever when she talked. And Bruce must have seen from the bar who took the Maintainer, and who would he cover up for but his girl?”

There he was plagiarizing my ideas, but I guess I was willing to sign them over to him in full if he got us the right pail of water for that time-bomb.

He glanced at his wrist. “According to my Caller, you have twenty-nine and a half minutes, including the time it will take to get a Door or contact headquarters. When are you going to get busy on the girl?”

Bruce laughed a little—deprecatingly, so help me—and started toward him. “Look here, old man,” he said, “there’s no need to trouble Lili, or to fuss with headquarters, even if you could. Really not at all. Not to mention that your surmises are quite unfounded, old chap, and I’m a bit surprised at your advancing them. But that’s quite all right because, as it happens, I’m an atomics technician and I even worked on that very bomb. To disarm it, you just have to fiddle a bit with some of the ankhs, those hoopy little crosses. Here, let me—”

===Chinese===
头一个直奔正题的是埃里希——我本来押多大注都押他。他抢了我们的先手，不打算丢掉。

「那么，你们打算什么时候开始让莉莉交代她把维持器藏哪儿了？准是她——她讲话时对它一去不返太有把握了。而布鲁斯从吧台上必然看见了是谁拿走维持器，除了他的姑娘，他还会替谁打掩护？」

瞧，他在剽窃我的思路，不过我想，只要他能给那颗定时炸弹浇对一桶水，我情愿把版权整个签让给他。

他瞟了一眼手腕。「按我的呼叫器，你们还有二十九分半钟，包括开门或联络总部所需的时间。你们打算什么时候对那姑娘动手？」

布鲁斯轻轻笑了——老天作证，是谦和地笑——朝他走过去。「我说，老伙计，」他说，「犯不上麻烦莉莉，也犯不上惊动总部，就算你联络得上。真的完全犯不上。更别说你的猜测全无根据，老兄，你居然说得出口，我还有点吃惊。不过没关系，因为碰巧得很：我就是一名原子技师，而且那颗炸弹我本人还参与过。要拆掉它，只需要拨弄几个安卡（ankh，带环小十字），就有惊无险。来，让我——」

===Original===
*Allah il allah*, but it must have struck everybody as it did me as being just too incredible an assertion, too bloody British a barefaced bluff, for Erich didn’t have to say a word; Mark and Sevensee grabbed Bruce by the arms, one on each side, as he stooped toward the bronze chest, and they weren’t gentle about it. Then Erich spoke.

“Oh, no, Bruce. Very sporting of you to try to cover up for your girl friend, but we aren’t going to let ourselves be blown to stripped atoms twenty-eight minutes too soon while you monkey with the buttons, the very thing Benson-Carter warned against, and pray for a guesswork miracle. It’s too thin, Bruce, when you come from 1917 and haven’t been on the Big Time for a hundred sleeps and were calling for an A-tech yourself a few hours ago. Much too thin. Bruce, something is going to happen that I’m afraid you won’t like, but you’re going to have to put up with it. That is, unless Miss Foster decides to be cooperative.”

“I say, you fellows, let me go,” Bruce demanded, struggling experimentally. “I know it’s a bit thick to swallow and I did give you the wrong impression calling for an A-tech, but I just wanted to capture your attention then; I didn’t want to have to work on the bomb. Really, Erich, would they have ordered Benson-Carter to pick us up unless one of us were an A-tech? They’d be sure to include one in the bally operation.”

“When they’re using patchwork tactics?” Erich grinningly quoted back at him.

===Chinese===
*Allah il allah*（真主啊），这话砸在每个人身上的分量，想必跟我一样：实在太不可信，太英式太血淋淋的当面讹诈，因为埃里希一个字都没说；布鲁斯刚朝青铜箱俯身，马克和七观一边一个，抓住了他的胳膊，而且手底下毫不留情。随后埃里希开口了。

「哦，不行，布鲁斯。你想替你的女朋友打掩护，很有骑士风度，可我们犯不上陪你瞎按按钮——那恰恰是本森-卡特警告过的头等忌讳——再顺手祈祷一个瞎蒙的奇迹，就提前二十八分钟被炸成裸原子。太单薄了，布鲁斯：你来自 1917 年，上大时光还不到一百次入眠，几个钟头前还在自己喊着要原子技师。太单薄了。布鲁斯，接下来要发生一件事，恐怕你不会喜欢，可你只好受着。当然——除非福斯特小姐决定合作。」

「喂，你们几位，放开我，」布鲁斯要求，试探性地挣了两下。「我知道这话有点难以下咽，喊原子技师也确实给了你们错误印象，可我当时只是想抓住你们的注意力，并不想真去碰炸弹。说真的，埃里希，倘若我们中间没有一位原子技师，上头会命令本森-卡特来接我们吗？这么要命的行动，他们必定要塞一个进来。」

「在他们使着打补丁战术的时候吗？」埃里希笑着把他自己的话原样掷回去。

---

===Original===
Kaby spoke up beside me and said, “Benson-Carter was a magician of matter and he was going on the operation disguised as an old woman. We have the cloak and hood with the other garments,” and I wondered how this cold fish of a she-officer could be the same girl who was giving Mark slurpy looks not ten minutes ago.

“Well?” Erich asked, glancing at his Caller and then swinging his eyes around at us as if there must be some of the old *Wehrmacht* iron somewhere.
